Landau–Ginzburg mirror, quantum differential equations and qKZ difference equations for a partial flag variety
Vitaly Tarasov ∘ \mskip 2.00002mu{}^{\circ} and Alexander Varchenko ⋆ \mskip 2.00002mu{}^{\star}

Abstract
. We consider the system of quantum differential equations for a partial flag variety and construct a basis of solutions in the form of multidimensional hypergeometric functions, that is, we construct a Landau–Ginzburg mirror for that partial flag variety. In our construction, the solutions are labeled by elements of the K \mskip 2.00002muK\mskip-2.00002mu -theory algebra of the partial flag variety. To establish these facts we consider the equivariant quantum differential equations for a partial flag variety and introduce a compatible system of difference equations, which we call the qKZ equations. We construct a basis of solutions of the joint system of the equivariant quantum differential equations and qKZ difference equations in the form of multidimensional hypergeometric functions. Then the facts about the non-equivariant quantum differential equations are obtained from the facts about the equivariant quantum differential equations by a suitable limit. Analyzing these constructions we obtain a formula for the fundamental Levelt solution of the quantum differential equations for a partial flag variety.

1.
Introduction

The genus zero Gromov–Witten invariants of a partial flag variety
\,V\mskip-0.99998mu

answer enumerative questions about rational curves in
\,V\mskip-0.99998mu
and are put
together in various ways to define rich mathematical structures such as the
quantum cohomology algebra and the system of quantum differential equations.
Those structures are part of the so-called  ‘
\!A
-model’ of
V
.
Landau–Ginzburg mirror symmetry seeks to describe these structures in terms
of a mirror dual  ‘
\mskip-0.99998muB
-model’ associated with
\mskip 2.00002muV
. The way originated by
A. Givental in
[
Gi1
,
Gi2
]
seeks to encode the data from the
\mskip-0.99998muA
-model by
oscillating integrals of a superpotential function
\mskip 2.00002muW
, which is defined on
a ‘mirror dual’ affine variety
\mskip 2.00002muV^{o}
. In particular, these oscillating
integrals are supposed to give all solutions of the system of quantum
differential equations.

This Landau–Ginzburg mirror symmetry has been established by A. Givental
for full flag varieties and projective spaces in
[
Gi1
,
Gi2
,
GKi
]
.
The construction for Grassmannians has been performed by R. J. Marsh and
K. Rietsch in
[
MR
]
. A standard problem, related to generalizations of
Givental’s approach, is to check that the number of critical points of
the superpotential equals the dimension of the cohomology algebra of
V
, and
then check that the oscillating integrals, which are intrinsically labeled
by the critical points, indeed generate a basis of solutions of the system of
quantum differential equations. While the first part is of algebraic nature,
the second part is of global topological nature (to determine integration cycles
exiting critical points). For example, in
[
MR
]
a superpotential for
a Grassmannian was constructed, and it was shown that the number of its critical
points is the correct one, but the part that the oscillating integrals indeed
give a basis of solutions was not addressed.

In this paper, we suggest a new ‘hypergeometric Landau–Ginzburg mirror
symmetry model’ for a partial flag variety
\mskip 2.00002muV\mskip-0.99998mu
and construct the full set of
solutions of the system of quantum differential equation of
\mskip 2.00002muV\mskip-0.99998mu
in the form
of multidimensional hypergeometric functions. In these functions the role of
Givental’s exponential of the superpotential is played by what we call the
master function, which is the product of Gamma functions multiplied by the
exponential of a linear function, and the role of the mirror dual
\mskip 2.00002muV^{o}\mskip-0.99998mu
is
played by the complement in an affine space to the set of all poles of the
product of Gamma functions composing the master function. In particular, our

\mskip 2.00002muV^{o}\mskip-0.99998mu

is not
an algebraic variety, but a complex analytic variety.

It is interesting to note that in our construction, the solutions of the system
of quantum differential equations of
\mskip 2.00002muV\mskip-0.99998mu
are naturally labeled by elements
of the
\mskip 2.00002muK\mskip-2.00002mu
-theory algebra of
\mskip 2.00002muV
. In particular, that observation
suggests that the monodromy and Stokes phenomenon of the system of quantum
differential equations of
\mskip 2.00002muV\mskip-0.99998mu
may be described in terms of
the
\mskip 2.00002muK\mskip-2.00002mu
-theory algebra of
\mskip 2.00002muV
, in accordance with the philosophy
of B. Dubrovin, see
[
D1
,
D2
,
CDG
,
TV6
,
TV7
,
TV2
,
CV
]
.

Consider the example of the
\,m\mskip 0.99998mu
-dimensional complex projective space

\mskip 2.00002muV\mskip-2.00002mu={\mathbb{C}}\mskip 0.99998mu{\mathbb{P}}^{\mskip 2.00002mum}
. In the body of the paper, this example corresponds
to the case
\,n=m+1\,
,
\mskip 2.00002muN=2\,
,
\,{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}=(1,m)\,
. The cohomology algebra
is
\,\displaystyle H^{*}(V;{\mathbb{C}})={\mathbb{C}}[x]\big/\langle\mskip 0.99998mux^{m+1}\mskip 0.99998mu\rangle\,
, where
\,x\,
is the first
Chern class of the tautological line bundle. The quantum multiplication
\,*_{p}

on
\,\displaystyle H^{*}(V;{\mathbb{C}})\mskip 2.00002mu
depends on a parameter
\,p\,
,

x^{i}\mskip-2.00002mu*_{p}x^{j}=\mskip 0.99998mux^{i+j}\quad\text{for}\;\;i+j\leqslant m\,,\qquad x^{i}\mskip-2.00002mu*_{p}x^{j}=\mskip 0.99998mup\mskip 2.00002mux^{i+j-m-1}\quad\text{for}\;\;i+j>m\,,\kern-20.00003pt

i,j=0,\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak m\,
. The parameter
\,p\,
corresponds to
\,p_{2}/\mskip-0.99998mup_{1}
in the body
of the paper. The quantum differential equation is

(1.1)

-\mskip 0.99998mu\kappa\mskip 0.99998mup\mskip 2.00002mu\frac{\partial I}{\partial p}\,=\,x*_{p}\mskip-0.99998muI\,,\ignorespaces

where
\,\kappa\,
is the parameter of the differential equation and
\,I\,
is
the unknown
\,\displaystyle H^{*}(V;{\mathbb{C}})\mskip 0.99998mu
-valued function. The
\mskip 2.00002muK\mskip-2.00002mu
-theory algebra is

\,K(V;{\mathbb{C}})={\mathbb{C}}[X,X^{-1}]\big/\mskip-0.99998mu\bigl\langle(X\mskip-2.00002mu-1)^{m+1}\mskip 0.99998mu\bigr\rangle\,
, where

\mskip 2.00002muX
is the class of the tautological line bundle.

The
\,\displaystyle H^{*}(V;{\mathbb{C}})\mskip 0.99998mu
-valued weight function is

\;W(t,x)=\sum_{\mskip 0.99998mui=0}^{\mskip 0.99998mum}\mskip 0.99998mut^{m-i}\mskip 0.99998mux^{i}\mskip 0.99998mu
. The master function is

\Phi(t,p,\kappa)\,=\,(\kappa^{m+1}\!/p\mskip 0.99998mu)^{t/\mskip-2.00002mu\kappa}\mskip 2.00002mu\bigl(\mskip 0.99998mu\Gamma(\mskip 0.99998mut/\mskip-0.99998mu\kappa)\bigr)^{m+1}\mskip 0.99998mu.\kern-10.00002pt\ignorespaces

For a univariate Laurent polynomial
\,P(X)\,
, define

\Psi_{P}(p,\kappa)\,=\,\kappa^{-1}\sum_{r=0}^{\infty}\,\operatorname{Res}_{\mskip 2.00002mut\mskip 2.00002mu=\mskip 0.99998mu-\mskip 0.99998mur\kappa\mskip 2.00002mu}\bigl(\Phi(t,p,\kappa)\mskip 2.00002muP(e^{\mskip 0.99998mu2\mskip 0.99998mu\pi\sqrt{\mskip-0.99998mu-1}\;t/\mskip-2.00002mu\kappa})\mskip 2.00002muW(t,x)\bigr)\,.\kern-13.99995pt

The function
\,\Psi_{P}(p,\kappa)\,
depends only on the class of
\,P\mskip 0.99998mu
in

\,K(V;{\mathbb{C}})\mskip 2.00002mu
. Alternatively,
\,\Psi_{P}(p,\kappa)\,
can be written as
an integral over an appropriate contour
\,C\mskip 2.00002mu
encircling the poles of

\,\Phi(t,p,\kappa)\,
in the positive direction,

\Psi_{P}(p,\kappa)\,=\,\frac{1}{2\mskip 0.99998mu\pi\mskip 0.99998mu\sqrt{\mskip-0.99998mu-1}}\,\int_{\mskip 0.99998muC}\Phi(t,p,\kappa)\mskip 2.00002muP(e^{\mskip 0.99998mu2\mskip 0.99998mu\pi\sqrt{\mskip-0.99998mu-1}\;t/\mskip-2.00002mu\kappa})\mskip 2.00002muW(t,x)\,d\mskip 0.99998mut\,.\kern-10.00002pt\ignorespaces

For instance, one can take the parabola

\,C=\,\{\,\kappa\mskip 2.00002mu\bigl(1-s^{2}\mskip-2.00002mu+s\mskip 2.00002mu\sqrt{\mskip-0.99998mu-1}\,\bigr)\ \,|\ \,s\in{\mathbb{R}}\,\}\,
.

Theorem 1.1
.

For any Laurent polynomial
\,P
, the
\mskip 0.99998mu\displaystyle H^{*}(V)
-valued function

\,\Psi_{P}(p,\kappa)\mskip 0.99998mu
is an entire function of
\,\mskip 0.99998mu\log\mskip 0.99998mup\mskip 2.00002mu
that solves
the quantum differential equation (
1.1
). If the classes of Laurent
polynomials
\,P_{0},\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak P_{m}
give a basis of
\;K(V;{\mathbb{C}})\mskip 2.00002mu
, then the functions

\,\Psi_{P_{0}}(p,\kappa),\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak\Psi_{P_{m}}(p,\kappa)\mskip 2.00002mu
give a basis of solutions of
the quantum differential equation.

Theorem
1.1
follows from Proposition
6.25
,
see also Proposition
5.17
. The fact that solutions

\,\Psi_{P_{0}}(p,\kappa),\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak\Psi_{P_{m}}(p,\kappa)\mskip 2.00002mu
of Theorem
1.1

form a basis follows from the following determinant formula.

Theorem 1.2
.

We have

\det\mskip 0.99998mu\biggl(\mskip 2.00002mu\sum_{r=0}^{\infty}\,\operatorname{Res}_{\mskip 2.00002mut\mskip 0.99998mu=\mskip 0.99998mu-\mskip 0.99998mur\kappa}\bigl(t^{\mskip 0.99998mui}\mskip 0.99998mue^{-\mskip 0.99998mu2\mskip 0.99998mu\pi\sqrt{\mskip-0.99998mu-1}\,\mskip 0.99998muj\mskip 0.99998mut/\mskip-2.00002mu\kappa}\,\Phi(t,p,\kappa)\bigr)\!\biggr)_{\!i,\mskip 0.99998muj=\mskip 0.99998mu0}^{\!m}\!=\,\bigl(2\mskip 0.99998mu\pi\mskip 0.99998mu\sqrt{\mskip-0.99998mu-1}\,\bigr)^{m\mskip 0.99998mu(m+1)\mskip-0.99998mu/2}\mskip 2.00002mu\kappa^{\mskip 0.99998mu(m+1)(m+2)\mskip-0.99998mu/2}\mskip 0.99998mu.\kern-6.00006pt\ignorespaces

Theorem
1.2
follows from Theorem
5.18
,
see also formula (
5.60
) .

Notice that in
[
Gu
]
, D. Guzzetti considers a scalar linear differential
equation of order
m+1
equivalent to the quantum differential equation
(
1.1
) and constructs a basis of solutions in the form of similar
integrals.

Our construction of the basis of solutions of the system of quantum differential
equations for a partial flag variety is done in several steps and is based on
constructions from representation theory.

We consider the joint compatible system of rational
qKZ
difference equations
and dynamical differential equations for sections of the trivial bundle

\pi\,:\,({\mathbb{C}}^{N})^{\otimes\mskip 0.99998mun}\times{\mathbb{C}}^{n}\times{\mathbb{C}}^{N}\,\to\,{\mathbb{C}}^{n}\times{\mathbb{C}}^{N}
. This system is
defined in terms of the Yangian
Y_{h}(\mathfrak{gl}_{N})
action on
({\mathbb{C}}^{N})^{\otimes\mskip 0.99998mun}
and depends
on the Yangian deformation parameter
h
. In this paper, we describe the

h\to\infty
limit of this system of difference and differential equations.
This is our first main result, see Section
3
. We call
the obtained equations the limiting
qKZ
difference equations and limiting
dynamical differential equations.

In
[
TV1
,
TV6
]
we constructed solutions of the joint system of initial
qKZ

difference equations and dynamical differential equations in the form of
multidimensional hypergeometric functions, see Theorem
4.9
.
In this paper, we calculate the
h\to\infty
limit of these solutions and obtain
solutions of the joint system of limiting equations, see Theorem
5.13
.
This is our second main result.

According to the general theory in
[
MO
]
the initial system of
qKZ

difference equations and dynamical differential equations is identified with
the system of
qKZ
difference equations and equivariant quantum differential
equations in the equivariant cohomology of cotangent bundles of partial flag
varieties. The precise formulae for that identification can be found in

[
GRTV
,
RTV1
]
. It was expected that a suitable
\,{h\to\infty}\,
limit
of these equations gives a system of difference and differential equations
related to the equivariant quantum cohomology of partial flag varieties
themselves instead of their cotangent bundles. In particular, it was shown
in
[
BMO
]
that the
\,{h\to\infty}\,
limit of the equivariant quantum
differential equations for the cotangent bundle of the full flag variety gives
the equivariant quantum differential equations of the corresponding full
flag variety. Also, the appropriate
\,{h\to\infty}\,
limit of the Yangian

R\mskip 0.99998mu
-matrix associated with the quantum cohomology of the cotangent bundle
of a Grassmannian, was used in
[
GK
,
GKS
]
to calculate the quantum
multiplication in the cohomology of the Grassmannian itself.

In this paper, we identify our limiting dynamical differential equations for
sections of the bundle
\,\pi\,
with the equivariant quantum differential
equations for partial flag varieties. Under this identification, our limiting

qKZ
difference equations become a system of difference equations in
the equivariant cohomology of partial flag varieties, hence, a new system
of difference equations compatible with the equivariant quantum differential
equations. At the same time, the multidimensional hypergeometric solutions of
the limiting equations for sections of the bundle
\,\pi\,
become solutions of
the equivariant quantum differential equation and
qKZ
difference equations
for partial flag varieties. This is our third main result,
see Theorem
6.17
.

The particular case of a projective space was considered in
[
TV7
,
CV
]
,
where the joint system of the equivariant quantum differential equation
and compatible
qKZ
difference equations, together with
hypergeometric solutions, was used to analyze the Stokes phenomenon for
the equivariant quantum differential equation of a projective space.
We expect similar applications of our compatible
qKZ
equations for partial
flag varieties.

The paper is organized as follows. In Section
2
we define the initial
joint system of
qKZ
and dynamical equations. In Section
3

we describe the
\,{h\to\infty}\,
limit of these equations. In Section

4
we describe multidimensional
hypergeometric solutions of the initial
qKZ
and dynamical equations.
In Section
5
we obtain solutions of the limiting equations by
taking the
\,{h\to\infty}\,
limit of the solutions of the initial equations.
In Section
6
we identify the limiting equations with the equation in
equivariant cohomology of partial flag varieties.
Appendices
A
–
D
contain technical information.

In Appendix
E
we show that the monodromy of the system of dynamical
differential equations (
2.7
) is abelian under a certain resonance
condition for the equivariant parameters. This is analogous to the corresponding
property of the quantum differential equation of the Hilbert scheme of points
in the plane, see
[
OP
]
.

Notice that Proposition
6.23
provides an equivariant Gamma theorem
for a partial flag variety
\,{\mathcal{F}}_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\,
, cf. Theorem B.2 and formula (11.19)
in
[
TV6
]
. Notice also that in Theorems
4.10
,
4.17
,

5.14
,
5.18
we prove different determinant formulas which
imply that the functions entering the determinants form bases in the spaces
of solutions of the corresponding differential and difference equations.
The Levelt fundamental solutions are discussed in Sections
4.7
and

5.5
.

The authors thank G. Cotti, A. Givental, V. Gorbounov, L. Mihalcea,
K. Rietsch, R. Rimányi for useful discussions. The second author thanks
Max Planck Institute for Mathematics in Bonn for hospitality in May–June of
2022.

2.
Dynamical and
qKZ
equations

2.1.
Notations

Fix
\,N,n\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{>0}\,
.
Let
\,{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{N}_{\geqslant 0}\,
,
\,|\mskip 0.99998mu{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\mskip 0.99998mu|=\lambda_{1}+\ldots+\lambda_{N}=n\,
.
Let
\,I=(I_{1},\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak I_{N})\,
be a partition of
\,\{1,\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak n\}\,
into disjoint
subsets
\,I_{1},\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak I_{N}\,
. Denote by
\,{{\mathcal{I}}_{{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}}}\mskip 2.00002mu
the set of all partitions

\,I\mskip 2.00002mu
with
\,|I_{j}|=\lambda_{j}\,
,
\,\mskip 0.99998muj=1,\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak N\mskip 2.00002mu
.

Consider the space
\,{\mathbb{C}}^{N}\mskip 0.99998mu
with the standard basis

\,v_{i}=(0,\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak 0,1_{i},0,\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak 0)\,
,
\;i=1,\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak N
, and
the tensor product
\,({\mathbb{C}}^{N})^{\otimes\mskip 0.99998mun}\mskip 2.00002mu
with the basis
\,(v_{I})_{I}\mskip 2.00002mu
,

v_{I}\mskip 2.00002mu=\,v_{i_{1}}\otimes\allowbreak\dots\otimes\allowbreak v_{i_{n}}\,,\kern-20.00003pt

where
\,i_{j}=m\,
if
\,j\in I_{m}\,
.

The space
\,({\mathbb{C}}^{N})^{\otimes\mskip 0.99998mun}
is a module over the Lie algebra
{\mathfrak{gl}_{N}}
with basis

\,e_{i,\mskip 0.99998muj}\,
,
i,j=1,\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak N
. The
{\mathfrak{gl}_{N}}
-module
({\mathbb{C}}^{N})^{\otimes\mskip 0.99998mun}
has weight decomposition

\,({\mathbb{C}}^{N})^{\otimes\mskip 0.99998mun}=\sum_{|{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}|=n}({\mathbb{C}}^{N})^{\otimes\mskip 0.99998mun}_{\mskip 0.99998mu{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}}
, where
\,({\mathbb{C}}^{N})^{\otimes\mskip 0.99998mun}_{\mskip 0.99998mu{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}}
is the subspace with basis

\,(v_{I})_{I\in{{\mathcal{I}}_{{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}}}}\mskip 0.99998mu
.

2.2.
Difference
qKZ
equations

Define the
\,R
-matrix acting on
\mskip 0.99998mu({\mathbb{C}}^{N})^{\otimes 2}
,

R(u;h)\,=\,\frac{u-h\mskip 0.99998muP}{u-h}\,,\ignorespaces

where
P
is the permutation of factors of
({\mathbb{C}}^{N})^{\otimes 2}
and

u,h\in{\mathbb{C}}\mskip 2.00002mu
.
The
R\mskip 0.99998mu
-matrix satisfies the Yang-Baxter and unitarity equations,

(2.1)

\displaystyle R^{(12)}(u-v;h)R^{(13)}(u;h)R^{(23)}(v;h)\,=\,R^{(23)}(v;h)R^{(13)}(u;h)R^{(12)}(u-v;h)\,,

(2.2)

\displaystyle R^{(12)}(u;h)R^{(21)}(-u;h)\,=\,1\,.

The first equation is an equation in
\operatorname{End}(({\mathbb{C}}^{N})^{\otimes 3})
.
The superscript indicates the factors of
\mskip 0.99998mu({\mathbb{C}}^{N})^{\otimes 3}

on which the corresponding operators act.

Let
\,{\boldsymbol{z}}=(z_{1},\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak z_{n})\in{\mathbb{C}}^{n}
,
\,{\boldsymbol{q}}=(q_{1},\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak q_{N})\in{\mathbb{C}}^{N}
, and
\kappa\in{\mathbb{C}}\mskip 2.00002mu
.
Define the
qKZ
operators
\,K_{1},\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak K_{n}\mskip 2.00002mu
acting on
({\mathbb{C}}^{N})^{\otimes\mskip 0.99998mun}
:

(2.3)

\displaystyle\kern 11.99998ptK_{a}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h;{\boldsymbol{q}}\mskip 0.99998mu;\kappa)\mskip 2.00002mu

\displaystyle{}=\,R^{(a,\mskip 0.99998mua-1)}(z_{a}\mskip-0.99998mu-z_{a-1}+\kappa;h)\,\dots\,R^{(a,1)}(z_{a}\mskip-0.99998mu-z_{1}+\kappa;h)\,\times{}

\displaystyle{}\mskip 2.00002mu\times\mskip-0.99998mu\;q_{1}^{e_{1,1}^{(a)}}\!\dots\,q_{N}^{e_{N,N}^{(a)}}\,R^{(a,n)}(z_{a}\mskip-0.99998mu-z_{n}\mskip 0.99998mu;h)\,\dots\,R^{(a,\mskip 0.99998mua+1)}(z_{a}\mskip-0.99998mu-z_{a+1}\mskip 0.99998mu;h)\,.\kern-11.00008pt

The
qKZ
operators preserve the weight decomposition of
\,({\mathbb{C}}^{N})^{\otimes\mskip 0.99998mun}
and
have the property

(2.4)

\displaystyle K_{a}(z_{1},\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak z_{b}+\kappa,\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak z_{n};h;{\boldsymbol{q}}\mskip 0.99998mu;\kappa)\,K_{b}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h;{\boldsymbol{q}}\mskip 0.99998mu;\kappa)

\displaystyle{}\,={}

\displaystyle{}=\,K_{b}(z_{1},\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak z_{a}+\kappa,\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak z_{n};h;{\boldsymbol{q}}\mskip 0.99998mu;\kappa)

\displaystyle\,K_{a}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h;{\boldsymbol{q}}\mskip 0.99998mu;\kappa)\kern-13.99995pt

for all
a,b=1,\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak n
, see
[
FR
]
. We say that the collection of operators

\,K_{1},\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak K_{n}\mskip 2.00002mu
with this property define a
discrete flat connection
,
see
[
TV2
]
.

The system of difference equations with step
\kappa
,

(2.5)

f(z_{1},\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak z_{a}+\kappa,\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak z_{n};h;{\boldsymbol{q}}\mskip 0.99998mu;\kappa)\,=\,K_{a}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h;{\boldsymbol{q}}\mskip 0.99998mu;\kappa)\,f({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h;{\boldsymbol{q}}\mskip 0.99998mu;\kappa)\,,\qquad a=1,\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak n\mskip 2.00002mu,\ignorespaces

for a
({\mathbb{C}}^{N})^{\otimes n}\mskip-0.99998mu
-valued
function
f({\boldsymbol{z}},h,{\boldsymbol{q}}\mskip 0.99998mu;\kappa)
is called the
qKZ

equations
.

Remark 2.1
.

2.3.
Differential dynamical equations

Define the linear operators
\,X_{1},\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak X_{N}
acting on
({\mathbb{C}}^{N})^{\otimes\mskip 0.99998mun}
,
called the dynamical Hamiltonians:

\displaystyle X_{i}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h;{\boldsymbol{q}})\,=\,\sum_{a=1}^{n}\,z_{a}\mskip 0.99998mue^{(a)}_{i,\mskip 0.99998mui}-\mskip 2.00002muh\mskip 2.00002mu\Bigl(\mskip 2.00002mu\frac{\tilde{e}_{i,i}(1-\tilde{e}_{i,\mskip 0.99998mui})}{2}\,

\displaystyle{}+\!\sum_{1\leqslant a<b\leqslant n}\,\sum_{k=1}^{N}\,e_{i,\mskip 0.99998muk}^{(a)}\mskip 2.00002mue_{k,\mskip 2.00002mui}^{(b)}\mskip 2.00002mu+{}

\displaystyle{}+\mskip 2.00002mu\sum_{\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{1}{\scriptstyle j=1}{\scriptstyle j\ne i}}^{N}\,\frac{q_{j}}{q_{i}\mskip-0.99998mu-q_{j}}\,(\tilde{e}_{i,\mskip 0.99998muj}\mskip 2.00002mu\tilde{e}_{j,\mskip 0.99998mui}\mskip-0.99998mu-\tilde{e}_{i,\mskip 0.99998mui})\mskip-0.99998mu\Bigr)\mskip 2.00002mu,\kern-13.99995pt

where
\tilde{e}_{s,\mskip 0.99998mut}=\sum_{a=1}^{n}e_{s,\mskip 0.99998mut}^{(a)}
.
The differential operators

(2.6)

\nabla_{{\boldsymbol{q}},\kappa,i}\,=\,\kappa\mskip 2.00002muq_{i}\mskip 0.99998mu\frac{\partial}{\partial\mskip 0.99998muq_{i}}-X_{i}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h;{\boldsymbol{q}})\,,\qquad i=1,\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak N\mskip 2.00002mu,

preserve the weight decomposition of
({\mathbb{C}}^{N})^{\otimes\mskip 0.99998mun}
and pairwise commute,
see
[
TV3
]
, also
[
GRTV
, Section 3.4]
,
[
RTV1
, Section 7.1]
,

[
MTV
]
. The operators
\nabla_{{\boldsymbol{q}},\kappa,i}
define the
({\mathbb{C}}^{N})^{\otimes\mskip 0.99998mun}
-valued

dynamical connection
.

The system of differential equations

(2.7)

\kappa\mskip 2.00002muq_{i}\mskip 0.99998mu\frac{\partial f}{\partial\mskip 0.99998muq_{i}}\,=\,X_{i}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h;{\boldsymbol{q}})\mskip 2.00002muf\,,\qquad i=1,\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak N\mskip 2.00002mu,\kern-10.00002pt\ignorespaces

for a
\,({\mathbb{C}}^{N})^{\otimes\mskip 0.99998mun}
-valued function
\,f({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h;{\boldsymbol{q}}\mskip 0.99998mu;\kappa)\,
is called
the
dynamical equations
.

Theorem 2.2

(
[
TV3
]
)
.

The joint system of dynamical and
qKZ
equations with the same parameter
\kappa

is compatible.

Lemma 2.3
.

The dynamical Hamiltonians
\,\mskip 0.99998muX_{1},\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak X_{N}\mskip 0.99998mu
acting on
\mskip 0.99998mu\,({\mathbb{C}}^{N})^{\otimes\mskip 0.99998mun}\mskip-0.99998mu

can be written in the form

(2.8)

X_{i}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h;{\boldsymbol{q}})\,=\,\sum_{a=1}^{n}\,z_{a}\mskip 0.99998mue^{(a)}_{i,\mskip 0.99998mui}-\mskip 2.00002muh\!\sum_{1\leqslant a<b\leqslant n}\,\sum_{\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{1}{\scriptstyle j=1}{\scriptstyle j\ne i}}^{N}\,\Bigl(\,\frac{e_{i,\mskip 0.99998muj}^{(a)}\mskip 0.99998mue_{j,\mskip 0.99998mui}^{(b)}}{1-q_{j}/q_{i}}\,-\,\frac{e_{j,\mskip 0.99998mui}^{(a)}\mskip 0.99998mue_{i,\mskip 0.99998muj}^{(b)}}{1-q_{i}/q_{j}}\,\Bigr)\mskip 2.00002mu.\kern-23.99997pt

Proof.

The proof is by direct verification.
∎

3.
Limit
\,{h\to\infty}\,

3.1.
Limit of
qKZ
operators

Introduce the
R\mskip 0.99998mu
-matrix
R^{\mskip 0.99998mu{\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\circ$}}}\mskip-0.99998mu(u)
acting on
({\mathbb{C}}^{N})^{\otimes 2}
:

R^{\mskip 0.99998mu{\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\circ$}}}\mskip-0.99998mu(u)\,=\,P+\mskip 2.00002muu\!\sum_{1\leqslant i<j\leqslant N}\!e_{i,\mskip 0.99998mui}\otimes e_{j,\mskip 0.99998muj}\,.\ignorespaces

For example, for
\,N=2\,\mskip 0.99998mu
the matrix is

R^{\mskip 0.99998mu{\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\circ$}}}\mskip-0.99998mu(u)=\left(\,\begin{matrix}1&0&0&0\\
0&u&1&0\\
0&1&0&0\\
0&0&0&1\end{matrix}\,\right).

Lemma 3.1
.

We have

(3.1)

R^{\mskip 0.99998mu{\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\circ$}}}\mskip-0.99998mu(u)\,=\,\lim_{h\to\infty}\bigl((\mskip-0.99998mu-\mskip 0.99998muh)^{\,\sum_{i<j}e^{(1)}_{i,i}e^{(2)}_{j,j}}\,R(u;h)\;(\mskip-0.99998mu-\mskip 0.99998muh)^{\mskip 0.99998mu-\!\sum_{i<j}e^{(1)}_{j,j}e^{(2)}_{i,i}}\,\bigr)\,.\ignorespaces

Moreover,
R^{\mskip 0.99998mu{\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\circ$}}}\mskip-0.99998mu(u)
satisfies the Yang-Baxter and unitarity equations,

(3.2)

\displaystyle\bigl(R^{\mskip 0.99998mu{\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\circ$}}}\mskip-0.99998mu(u-v)\bigr)^{(12)}\bigl(R^{\mskip 0.99998mu{\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\circ$}}}\mskip-0.99998mu(u)\bigr)^{(13)}\bigl(R^{\mskip 0.99998mu{\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\circ$}}}\mskip-0.99998mu(v)\bigr)^{(23)}=\,\bigl(R^{\mskip 0.99998mu{\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\circ$}}}\mskip-0.99998mu(v)\bigr)^{(23)}\bigl(R^{\mskip 0.99998mu{\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\circ$}}}\mskip-0.99998mu(u)\bigr)^{(13)}\bigl(R^{\mskip 0.99998mu{\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\circ$}}}\mskip-0.99998mu(u-v)\bigr)^{(12)},

(3.3)

\displaystyle\bigl(R^{\mskip 0.99998mu{\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\circ$}}}\mskip-0.99998mu(u)\bigr)^{(12)}\bigl(R^{\mskip 0.99998mu{\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\circ$}}}\mskip-0.99998mu(-u)\bigr)^{(21)}=\,1\,.

Proof.

The proof of formula (
3.1
) is straightforward. Equations (
3.2
),
(
3.3
) follow from (
3.1
) and (
2.1
), (
2.2
), respectively.
∎

Let
\,{\boldsymbol{p}}=(p_{1},\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak p_{N})\in{\mathbb{C}}^{N}
.
Define the
qKZ
operators
\,K^{\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\circ$}}_{1},\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak K^{\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\circ$}}_{n}\mskip 0.99998mu
acting on
\,({\mathbb{C}}^{N})^{\otimes\mskip 0.99998mun}\,
:

(3.4)

\displaystyle\kern 20.00003ptK^{\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\circ$}}_{a}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;{\boldsymbol{p}}\mskip 0.99998mu;\kappa)\mskip 2.00002mu

\displaystyle{}=\,\bigl(R^{\mskip 0.99998mu{\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\circ$}}}\mskip-0.99998mu(z_{a}\mskip-0.99998mu-z_{a-1}+\kappa)\bigr)^{(a,\mskip 0.99998mua-1)}\mskip-0.99998mu\dots\,\bigl(R^{\mskip 0.99998mu{\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\circ$}}}\mskip-0.99998mu(z_{a}\mskip-0.99998mu-z_{1}+\kappa)\bigr)^{(a,1)}\times{}

\displaystyle{}\mskip 2.00002mu\times\,p_{1}^{e_{1,1}^{(a)}}\!\dots\,p_{N}^{e_{N,N}^{(a)}}\,\bigl(R^{\mskip 0.99998mu{\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\circ$}}}\mskip-0.99998mu(z_{i}\mskip-0.99998mu-z_{n})\bigr)^{(a,n)}\mskip-0.99998mu\dots\,\bigl(R^{\mskip 0.99998mu{\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\circ$}}}\mskip-0.99998mu(z_{a}\mskip-0.99998mu-z_{a+1})\bigr)^{(a,\mskip 0.99998mua+1)}\,.\kern-20.00003pt

Lemma 3.2
.

For
a=1,\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak n
, we have

K^{\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\circ$}}_{a}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;{\boldsymbol{p}}\mskip 0.99998mu;\kappa)\,=\lim_{h\to\infty}\bigl(\mskip-0.99998mu(\mskip-0.99998mu-\mskip 0.99998muh)^{\,\sum_{b<c,\,i<j}e^{(b)}_{j,j}e^{(c)}_{i,i}}\,\widetilde{K}_{i}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h;{\boldsymbol{p}}\mskip 0.99998mu;\kappa)\,(\mskip-0.99998mu-\mskip 0.99998muh)^{\mskip 0.99998mu-\sum_{b<c,\,i<j}e^{(b)}_{j,j}e^{(c)}_{i,i}}\,\bigr)\,,\kern-20.00003pt\ignorespaces

where the operators
\,\widetilde{K}_{1},\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak\widetilde{K}_{n}
are obtained from the
qKZ
operators

\,K_{1},\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak K_{n}
, see (
2.3
), by the substitution

(3.5)

q_{i}\mskip 2.00002mu\mapsto\,p_{i}\,(\mskip-0.99998mu-\mskip 0.99998muh)^{\,\sum_{b=1}^{n}\left(\sum_{j>i}e^{(b)}_{j,j}\,-\,\sum_{j<i}e^{(b)}_{j,j}\mskip 2.00002mu\right)}\,,\qquad i=1,\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak N\,.\kern-20.00003pt

Proof.

The lemma is proved by direct verification using Lemma
3.1
.
∎

Corollary 3.3
.

The
qKZ
operators
\,K^{\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\circ$}}_{1},\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak K^{\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\circ$}}_{n}\mskip 2.00002mu
preserve the weight decomposition of

\,({\mathbb{C}}^{N})^{\otimes\mskip 0.99998mun}
and define a discrete flat connection,

K^{\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\circ$}}_{a}(z_{1},\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak z_{b}+\kappa,\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak z_{n};{\boldsymbol{p}}\mskip 0.99998mu;\kappa)\,K^{\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\circ$}}_{b}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;{\boldsymbol{p}}\mskip 0.99998mu;\kappa)\,=\,K^{\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\circ$}}_{b}(z_{1},\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak z_{a}+\kappa,\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak z_{n};{\boldsymbol{p}}\mskip 0.99998mu;\kappa)\,K^{\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\circ$}}_{a}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;{\boldsymbol{p}}\mskip 0.99998mu;\kappa)\ignorespaces

for all
\,a,b=1,\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak n\,
, cf. (
2.4
).

Remark 3.4
.

The
R\mskip 0.99998mu
-matrix
\,R^{\mskip 0.99998mu{\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\circ$}}}\mskip-0.99998mu(u)\,
for
\,N=2\,
is analogous to the
R\mskip 0.99998mu
-matrix
of the five-vertex model and the
q\mskip 0.99998mu
-boson model, and to the
R\mskip 0.99998mu
-matrices
considered in
[
GK
,
GKS
]
in relation to
the quantum cohomology theory for Grassmannians.

Remark 3.5
.

Since
\,\mskip 0.99998mu\det\mskip 0.99998muR^{\mskip 0.99998mu{\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\circ$}}}\mskip-0.99998mu(u)=1\,
, the
qKZ
operators
\,K^{\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\circ$}}_{1},\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak K^{\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\circ$}}_{n}\mskip 0.99998mu
are
invertible for all
\,{\boldsymbol{z}},{\boldsymbol{p}},\kappa\,
provided
\,p_{i}\mskip-0.99998mu\neq 0\,
for all

\,i=1,\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak N\mskip 2.00002mu
. That is, the discrete flat connection on
\,({\mathbb{C}}^{N})^{\otimes\mskip 0.99998mun}\mskip 0.99998mu
defined
by
\,K^{\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\circ$}}_{1},\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak K^{\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\circ$}}_{n}\mskip 2.00002mu
is regular for all
\,{\boldsymbol{z}}\,
, unlike the discrete flat
connection defined by the
qKZ
operators
\,K_{1},\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak K_{n}\mskip 2.00002mu
, see (
2.3
).

3.2.
Dynamical operators
\,X^{\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\circ$}}_{1},\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak X^{\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\circ$}}_{\mskip-2.00002muN}\,

Recall
{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}=(\lambda_{1},\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak\lambda_{N})\,
. Let

\lambda^{(i)}\mskip-0.99998mu=\sum_{j=1}^{i}\,\lambda_{i}\,
,
\;i=1,\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak N\,
, so that

\,\lambda^{(N)}\mskip-0.99998mu=n\,
. For
\,1\leqslant i<j\leqslant n\,
, set

\;\lambda_{\langle i,\mskip 0.99998muj\rangle}=\mskip 0.99998mu\sum_{k=i}^{j-1}\,(\lambda_{k}\mskip-0.99998mu+\lambda_{k+1})\mskip 0.99998mu=\mskip 0.99998mu\lambda_{i}\mskip-0.99998mu+\lambda_{j}\mskip-0.99998mu+2\sum_{k=i+1}^{j-1}\lambda_{k}\,
.

Recall
\;I=(I_{1},\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak I_{N})\,
and the vectors
v_{I}\mskip-0.99998mu\in\mskip-0.99998mu({\mathbb{C}}^{N})^{\otimes\mskip 0.99998mun}
.
For
\,\sigma\in S_{n}\mskip 2.00002mu
and
\,I=(I_{1},\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak I_{N})\,
, set

\,\sigma(I)=\bigl(\sigma(I_{1}),\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak\sigma(I_{N})\bigr)\,
. For
a,b=1,\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak n\,
,
let
\,s_{a,\mskip 0.99998mub}\,
be the transposition of
\,a\mskip 0.99998mu,b\,
.

Given
\,I\mskip-0.99998mu\in{{\mathcal{I}}_{{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}}}\,
, let
\,\mskip 0.99998mua\in I_{i}\,
and
\,\mskip 0.99998mub\in I_{j}\,
.
A pair
\,(a,b)\,
is called
\,I\mskip-2.00002mu
-disordered

if either
\,a<b\,
,
\,i>j\,
, or
\,a>b\,
,
\,i<j\,
.
A pair
\,(a,b)\,
is called
\,I\mskip-2.00002mu
-ordered

if either
\,a<b\,
,
\,i<j\,
, or
\,a>b\,
,
\,i>j\,
.
A pair
\,(a,b)\,
is called
\,I\mskip-2.00002mu
-flat
if
\,i=j\,
.

Denote
\,M=\{\mskip 0.99998mu\min\mskip 0.99998mu(a,b)+1,\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak\max\mskip 0.99998mu(a,b)-1\mskip 0.99998mu\}\,
,

\,k=\min\mskip 0.99998mu(i,j)\,
,
\,l=\max\mskip 0.99998mu(i,j)\,
. If
\,i\neq j\,
, so that
\,k<l\,
,
set
m_{a,\mskip 0.99998mub,\mskip 0.99998muI}=\lambda_{\langle k,\mskip 2.00002mul\mskip 0.99998mu\rangle}\,
and

r_{a,\mskip 0.99998mub,\mskip 0.99998muI}\mskip 2.00002mu=\,|\mskip 0.99998muM\cap I_{i}\mskip 2.00002mu|+|\mskip 0.99998muM\cap I_{j}\mskip 2.00002mu|+2\mskip 2.00002mu\Bigl|\mskip 2.00002muM\cap\!\bigcup_{r=k+1}^{l-1}\!I_{r}\,\Bigr|\,.\ignorespaces

Clearly,
\,m_{a,\mskip 0.99998mub.\mskip 0.99998muI}\geqslant 2\;
and

\,\mskip 0.99998mu0\leqslant r_{a,\mskip 0.99998mub,\mskip 0.99998muI}\leqslant m_{a,\mskip 0.99998mub,\mskip 0.99998muI}-2\;
if
\,\mskip 0.99998mui\neq j\,
.
Set
\,I_{\mskip 0.99998mu[a,\mskip 0.99998mub\mskip 0.99998mu]}=\mskip 2.00002mu\bigcup_{\mskip 0.99998mur=k}^{\,l}I_{r}\,
.

Set

(3.6)

I^{\mskip 0.99998mu\min}_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\mskip 2.00002mu=\,\bigl(\mskip-0.99998mu(\lambda_{1},\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak\lambda^{(1)})\,,(\lambda^{(1)}\mskip-0.99998mu+1,\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak\lambda^{(2)})\,,\,\dots\,(\lambda^{(N-1)}\mskip-0.99998mu+1,\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak n)\bigr)\,.\ignorespaces

For
\,I\mskip-0.99998mu\in{{\mathcal{I}}_{{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}}}\,
, let
\,\sigma_{I}\in S_{n}\,
be the element of minimal length
such that
\,\sigma_{I}(I^{\mskip 0.99998mu\min}_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}})=I\,
. Notice that

(3.7)

|\mskip 0.99998mu\sigma_{I}\mskip 0.99998mu|\,=\,\bigl|\mskip 0.99998mu\{\mskip 0.99998mu(a,b)\ |\ a\in I_{i}\,,\;b\in I_{j}\,,\;a<b\,,\;i>j\mskip 2.00002mu\}\bigr|\,.\ignorespaces

Lemma 3.6
.

We have

a)
If the pair
\,(a,b)\mskip 0.99998mu
is
\,I\mskip-2.00002mu
-disordered, then

\,|\mskip 0.99998mu\sigma_{s_{a\mskip-0.99998mu,b}(I)}\mskip 0.99998mu|=|\mskip 0.99998mu\sigma_{I}\mskip 0.99998mu|-r_{a,\mskip 0.99998mub,\mskip 0.99998muI}-1\,
.

b)
If the pair
\,(a,b)\mskip 0.99998mu
is
\,I\mskip-2.00002mu
-ordered, then

\,|\mskip 0.99998mu\sigma_{s_{a\mskip-0.99998mu,b}(I)}\mskip 0.99998mu|=|\mskip 0.99998mu\sigma_{I}\mskip 0.99998mu|+r_{a,\mskip 0.99998mub,\mskip 0.99998muI}+1\,
.

c)
If the pair
\,(a,b)\mskip 0.99998mu
is
\,I\mskip-2.00002mu
-flat, then
\,s_{a,\mskip 0.99998mub}(I)=I\,
.

Corollary 3.7
.

If
\,|\mskip 0.99998mu\sigma_{s_{a\mskip-0.99998mu,b}(I)}\mskip 0.99998mu|<|\mskip 0.99998mu\sigma_{I}\mskip 0.99998mu|\,
, then
the pair
\,(a,b)\mskip 0.99998mu
is
\,I\mskip-2.00002mu
-disordered.
If
\,|\mskip 0.99998mu\sigma_{s_{a\mskip-0.99998mu,b}(I)}\mskip 0.99998mu|>|\mskip 0.99998mu\sigma_{I}\mskip 0.99998mu|\,
, then
the pair
\,(a,b)
is
\,I\mskip-2.00002mu
-ordered.

A pair
\,(a,b)\,
is called
\,I\mskip-2.00002mu
-admissible of the first kind

if
{\,|\mskip 0.99998mu\sigma_{s_{a\mskip-0.99998mu,b}(I)}\mskip 0.99998mu|=|\mskip 0.99998mu\sigma_{I}\mskip 0.99998mu|-1\,}
,
and
\,I\mskip-2.00002mu
-admissible of the second kind
if

\;|\mskip 0.99998mu\sigma_{s_{a\mskip-0.99998mu,b}(I)}\mskip 0.99998mu|=|\mskip 0.99998mu\sigma_{I}\mskip 0.99998mu|+m_{a,\mskip 0.99998mub,\mskip 0.99998muI}-1\,
.

Lemma 3.8
.

A pair
\,(a,b)
is
\,I\mskip-2.00002mu
-admissible of the first kind if and only if
it is
\,I\mskip-2.00002mu
-disordered and the intersection

\;\{\mskip 0.99998mu\min\mskip 0.99998mu(a,b)+1,\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak\max\mskip 0.99998mu(a,b)-1\mskip 0.99998mu\}\cap I_{\mskip 0.99998mu[a,\mskip 0.99998mub\mskip 2.00002mu]}
is empty.

Lemma 3.9
.

A pair
\,(a,b)
is
\,I\mskip-2.00002mu
-admissible of the second kind if and only if
it is
\,I\mskip-2.00002mu
-ordered and the intersection

\;\{\mskip 0.99998mu1,\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak\min\mskip 0.99998mu(a,b)-1,\,\max\mskip 0.99998mu(a,b)+1,\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak n\mskip 0.99998mu\}\cap I_{\mskip 0.99998mu[a,\mskip 0.99998mub\mskip 2.00002mu]}

is empty.

Example
.

Let
\,N=4\,
,
\,n=5\,
,
\,{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}=(2,1,1,1)\,
,

\,I=\bigl(\{\mskip 0.99998mu1,3\mskip 0.99998mu\},\{4\},\{2\},\{5\}\bigr)\,
.
Then the
\,I\mskip-0.99998mu
-admissible pairs of the first kind are
\,(3,2)\,
,

(2,3)\,
,
(4,2)\,
,
(2,4)\,
, and the
\,I\mskip-0.99998mu
-admissible pairs of the second
kind are
\,(1,4)\,
,
(4,1)\,
,
(1,5)\,
,
(5,1)\,
,
(2,5)\,
,
(5,2)\,
.

For all
\,i,j,a,b\,
, define linear operators
\,Q_{i,\mskip 0.99998muj}^{\mskip 2.00002mua,\mskip 0.99998mub}\mskip 2.00002mu

acting on
\,({\mathbb{C}}^{N})^{\otimes\mskip 0.99998mun}
by the rule

\displaystyle Q_{i,\mskip 0.99998muj}^{\mskip 2.00002mua,\mskip 0.99998mub}\mskip 2.00002muv_{I}\mskip 2.00002mu

\displaystyle{}=\,v_{s_{a\mskip-0.99998mu,b}(I)}\,,\quad

if
\,a\in I_{i}\,
,
\,b\in I_{j}\,
, and
the pair
\,(a,b)\,
is
\,I\mskip-0.99998mu
-admissible,

\displaystyle Q_{i,\mskip 0.99998muj}^{\mskip 2.00002mua,\mskip 0.99998mub}\mskip 2.00002muv_{I}\mskip 2.00002mu

\displaystyle{}=\,0\,,

otherwise.

Recall the linear operators
\,e_{i,\mskip 0.99998mui}^{(a)}\mskip 2.00002mu
acting on
\,({\mathbb{C}}^{N})^{\otimes\mskip 0.99998mun}\mskip 2.00002mu
.

The dynamical operators
\,X^{\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\circ$}}_{1},\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak X^{\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\circ$}}_{\mskip-2.00002muN}\,
, acting on
\,({\mathbb{C}}^{N})^{\otimes\mskip 0.99998mun}
are
given by the formula

(3.8)

\displaystyle X^{\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\circ$}}_{i}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;{\boldsymbol{p}})\,=\sum_{a=1}^{n}z_{a}\,e_{i,\mskip 0.99998mui}^{(a)}\mskip 2.00002mu

\displaystyle{}+\!\sum_{1\leqslant b<a\leqslant n}\mskip-0.99998mu\biggl(\mskip 2.00002mu\sum_{j=i+1}^{N}\mskip-2.00002muQ_{i,\mskip 0.99998muj}^{\mskip 2.00002mua,\mskip 0.99998mub}\mskip 0.99998mu-\sum_{j=1}^{i-1}\mskip 2.00002mu\frac{p_{i}}{p_{j}}\,Q_{i,\mskip 0.99998muj}^{\mskip 2.00002mua,\mskip 0.99998mub}\mskip 2.00002mu\biggr)+{}

\displaystyle{}+\!\sum_{1\leqslant a<b\leqslant n}\mskip-0.99998mu\biggl(\mskip 2.00002mu\sum_{j=i+1}^{N}\mskip 2.00002mu\frac{p_{j}}{p_{i}}\mskip 2.00002muQ_{i,\mskip 0.99998muj}^{\mskip 2.00002mua,\mskip 0.99998mub}\mskip 0.99998mu-\sum_{j=1}^{i-1}\mskip 2.00002muQ_{i,\mskip 0.99998muj}^{\mskip 2.00002mua,\mskip 0.99998mub}\mskip 2.00002mu\biggr)\mskip 2.00002mu.

Notice that the operators
\,X^{\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\circ$}}_{1},\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak X^{\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\circ$}}_{\mskip-2.00002muN}\mskip 2.00002mu
preserve the weight decomposition
of
({\mathbb{C}}^{N})^{\otimes\mskip 0.99998mun}
.

Example
.

Let
\,N=2\,
,
\,n=3\,
. The operators
\,X^{\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\circ$}}_{1}\mskip 2.00002mu,\mskip 0.99998muX^{\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\circ$}}_{2}\mskip 2.00002mu

preserve the subspace spanned by the vectors

v_{(\{1\},\{2,3\})}\mskip 0.99998mu=\mskip 2.00002muv_{1}\mskip-0.99998mu\otimes v_{2}\mskip-0.99998mu\otimes v_{2}\,,\qquad v_{(\{2\},\{1,3\})}\mskip 0.99998mu=\mskip 2.00002muv_{2}\mskip-0.99998mu\otimes v_{1}\mskip-0.99998mu\otimes v_{2}\,,\qquad v_{(\{3\},\{1,2\})}\mskip 0.99998mu=\mskip 2.00002muv_{2}\mskip-0.99998mu\otimes v_{2}\mskip-0.99998mu\otimes v_{1}\,.\ignorespaces

of weight
\,{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}=(1,2)\,
.
The matrices of
\,X^{\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\circ$}}_{1}\mskip 2.00002mu,\mskip 0.99998muX^{\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\circ$}}_{2}\mskip 2.00002mu
in this basis are

\quad X^{\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\circ$}}_{1}=\left(\,\begin{matrix}z_{1}&1&0\\
0&z_{2}&1\\
p_{2}/p_{1}&0&z_{3}\end{matrix}\,\right),\qquad X^{\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\circ$}}_{2}=\left(\,\begin{matrix}z_{2}\mskip 0.99998mu+z_{3}&-\mskip 0.99998mu1&0\\
0&z_{1}\mskip 0.99998mu+z_{3}&-\mskip 0.99998mu1\\
-\mskip 2.00002mup_{2}/p_{1}&0&z_{1}\mskip 0.99998mu+z_{2}\end{matrix}\,\right).\ignorespaces

Example
.

Let
\,N=5\,
,
\,n=6\,
,

\,I=\bigl(\{\mskip 0.99998mu2,5\},\{6\},\{3\},\{1\},\{4\}\bigr)\,
.
Then

\displaystyle X^{\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\circ$}}_{1}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;{\boldsymbol{p}})\,v_{I}\,=\,{}

\displaystyle(z_{2}+z_{5})\,v_{I}\mskip 0.99998mu+\mskip 0.99998muv_{(\{\mskip 0.99998mu1,5\},\{6\},\{3\},\{2\},\{4\})}+\mskip 0.99998muv_{(\{\mskip 0.99998mu2,4\},\{6\},\{3\},\{1\},\{5\})}+{}

\displaystyle\hphantom{(z_2+z_5)\,v_I}\mskip 0.99998mu+\,v_{(\{\mskip 0.99998mu2,3\},\{6\},\{5\},\{1\},\{4\})}+\mskip 2.00002mu\frac{p_{2}}{p_{1}}\,v_{(\{\mskip 0.99998mu5,6\},\{2\},\{3\},\{1\},\{4\})}\,,

\displaystyle X^{\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\circ$}}_{2}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;{\boldsymbol{p}})\,v_{I}\,=\,{}

\displaystyle z_{5}\,v_{I}\mskip 0.99998mu+\mskip 0.99998muv_{(\{\mskip 0.99998mu2,5\},\{4\},\{3\},\{1\},\{6\})}+\mskip 0.99998muv_{(\{\mskip 0.99998mu2,5\},\{3\},\{6\},\{1\},\{4\})}-\,\frac{p_{2}}{p_{1}}\,v_{(\{\mskip 0.99998mu5,6\},\{2\},\{3\},\{1\},\{4\})}\,.

3.3.
Limit of dynamical Hamiltonians

Let the operators
\,\widetilde{X}_{1},\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak\widetilde{X}_{N}
be obtained from the dynamical Hamiltonians

X_{1},\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak X_{N}
, see (
2.8
), by substitution (
3.5
) . In more detail,
the operators
\,\widetilde{X}_{1},\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak\widetilde{X}_{N}
preserve the weight decomposition of
({\mathbb{C}}^{N})^{\otimes\mskip 0.99998mun}

and the action of
\,\widetilde{X}_{i}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h;{\boldsymbol{p}})
on
\,({\mathbb{C}}^{N})^{\otimes\mskip 0.99998mun}_{\mskip 0.99998mu{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}}
coincides with the action
of
\,X_{i}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h;{\boldsymbol{q}})\,
for
\,{\boldsymbol{q}}=(q_{1},\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak q_{N})
,

(3.9)

q_{j}\mskip 2.00002mu=\,p_{j}\,(\mskip-0.99998mu-\mskip 0.99998muh)^{\,\sum_{k>j}\lambda_{k}\,-\,\sum_{k<j}\lambda_{k}}\,,\qquad j=1,\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak N\,.\kern-20.00003pt

Lemma 3.10
.

For
i=1,\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak N
, we have

(3.10)

X^{\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\circ$}}_{i}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;{\boldsymbol{p}})\,=\lim_{h\to\infty}\bigl(\mskip-0.99998mu(\mskip-0.99998mu-\mskip 0.99998muh)^{\,\sum_{b<c,\,j<k}e^{(b)}_{k,k}e^{(c)}_{j,j}}\,\widetilde{X}_{i}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h;{\boldsymbol{p}})\,(\mskip-0.99998mu-\mskip 0.99998muh)^{\,-\sum_{b<c,\,j<k}e^{(b)}_{k,k}e^{(c)}_{j,j}}\,\bigr)\,.\kern-20.00003pt

Proof.

The lemma is proved by direct verification using Lemma
2.3
,
the equality

\sum_{b<c,\,j<k}\!e^{(b)}_{k,k}\,e^{(c)}_{j,j}\,v_{I}\,=\,|\mskip 0.99998mu\sigma_{I}\mskip 0.99998mu|\,v_{I}\,,

following from (
3.7
), and Lemma
3.6
.
∎

Corollary 3.11
.

The differential operators

(3.11)

\nabla^{\mskip 0.99998mu{\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\circ$}}}_{\mskip-0.99998mu{\boldsymbol{p}},\kappa,i}\,=\,\kappa\mskip 2.00002mup_{i}\mskip 0.99998mu\frac{\partial}{\partial\mskip 0.99998mup_{i}}-X^{\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\circ$}}_{i}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;{\boldsymbol{p}})\,,\qquad i=1,\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak N\mskip 2.00002mu,

preserve the weight decomposition of
\,({\mathbb{C}}^{N})^{\otimes\mskip 0.99998mun}\!
and pairwise commute.

3.4.
Limiting equations

The system of difference equations with step
\kappa
,

(3.12)

f(z_{1},\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak z_{a}+\kappa,\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak z_{n};{\boldsymbol{p}})\,=\,K^{\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\circ$}}_{a}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;{\boldsymbol{p}}\mskip 0.99998mu;\kappa)\,f({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;{\boldsymbol{p}})\,,\qquad a=1,\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak n\mskip 2.00002mu,\kern-20.00003pt\ignorespaces

for a
\mskip 0.99998mu({\mathbb{C}}^{N})^{\otimes n}\mskip-0.99998mu
-valued function
f({\boldsymbol{z}},{\boldsymbol{p}})
will be called
the
limiting
qKZ
equations
.

The system of differential equations with parameter
\kappa
,

(3.13)

\kappa\mskip 2.00002mup_{i}\mskip 0.99998mu\frac{\partial f}{\partial\mskip 0.99998mup_{i}}\,=\,X^{\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\circ$}}_{i}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;{\boldsymbol{p}})\mskip 2.00002muf\,,\qquad i=1,\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak N\mskip 2.00002mu,

for a
({\mathbb{C}}^{N})^{\otimes\mskip 0.99998mun}
-valued function
f({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;{\boldsymbol{p}})
will be called the

limiting dynamical equations
.

Theorem 3.12
.

The joint systems of limiting dynamical and limiting
qKZ
equations
with the same parameter
\kappa
is compatible.

Proof.

The theorem follows from Theorem
2.2
and
Lemmas
3.2
,
3.10
.
∎

4.
Hypergeometric solutions of the dynamical and
qKZ
equations

4.1.
Weight functions
W_{I}({\boldsymbol{t}}\mskip 0.99998mu;{\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h)

For
I\in{{\mathcal{I}}_{{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}}}
, we define the weight functions

W_{I}({\boldsymbol{t}}\mskip 0.99998mu;{\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h)
, cf.
[
TV1
,
TV4
,
RTV1
,
TV6
]
.
The functions
W_{I}({\boldsymbol{t}}\mskip 0.99998mu;{\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h)
here coincide with those defined  in

[
TV6
, Section 3.4]
.

Recall
{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}=(\lambda_{1},\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak\lambda_{N})\,
and
\,I=(I_{1},\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak I_{N})
. Set

\bigcup_{\mskip 2.00002muk=1}^{\,j}I_{k}=\mskip 2.00002mu\{\mskip 2.00002mui^{(j)}_{1}\!<\allowbreak\dots<\allowbreak i^{(j)}_{\lambda^{(j)}}\}
.
Consider the variables
t^{(j)}_{a}
,
j=1,\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak N-1
,
a=1,\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak\lambda^{(j)}
.
Set
t^{(N)}_{a}\!=z_{a}
,
a=1,\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak n
.
Denote
\,{\boldsymbol{t}}^{(j)}=(t^{(j)}_{1},\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak t^{(j)}_{\lambda^{(j)}})\,
and

\,{\boldsymbol{t}}=({\boldsymbol{t}}^{(1)},\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak{\boldsymbol{t}}^{(N-1)})\,
.

For
\,I\mskip-0.99998mu\in{{\mathcal{I}}_{{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}}}\,
, define

(4.1)

\Sigma_{I}\mskip 2.00002mu=\,(z_{i^{(1)}_{1}},\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak z_{i^{(1)}_{\lambda^{\vphantom{1}\smash{(1)}}}}\mskip-2.00002mu,z_{i^{(2)}_{1}},\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak z_{i^{(2)}_{\lambda^{\vphantom{1}\smash{(2)}}}}\mskip-2.00002mu,\,\ldots\,,\,z_{i^{(N-1)}_{1}},\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak z_{i^{(N-1)}_{\lambda^{\vphantom{1}\smash{(N-1)}}}})\,,

so that,
\,{\boldsymbol{t}}=\Sigma_{I}\,
reads in detail as

t^{(k)}_{a}\mskip-0.99998mu=\mskip 2.00002muz_{i^{(k)}_{a}},\qquad k=1,\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak N-1\,,\quad i=a,\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak\lambda^{(k)}\mskip 2.00002mu.

For a function
\,f(x_{1},\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak x_{k})
of some variables, denote

\operatorname{Sym}_{\mskip 0.99998mux_{1},\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak x_{k}}\mskip-0.99998muf(x_{1},\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak x_{k})\,=\,\sum_{\sigma\in S_{k}}f(x_{\sigma(1)},\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak x_{\sigma(k)})\,.

The weight functions are

(4.2)

W_{I}({\boldsymbol{t}}\mskip 0.99998mu;{\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h)\,=\,\operatorname{Sym}_{\mskip 2.00002mut^{(1)}_{1}\!,\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak\,t^{(1)}_{\lambda^{(1)}}}\,\ldots\;\operatorname{Sym}_{\mskip 2.00002mut^{(N-1)}_{1}\!,\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak\,t^{(N-1)}_{\lambda^{(N-1)}}}U_{I}({\boldsymbol{t}}\mskip 0.99998mu;{\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h)\,,\ignorespaces

\displaystyle U_{I}({\boldsymbol{t}}\mskip 0.99998mu;{\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h)\,={}

\displaystyle{}\!=\,\prod_{j=1}^{N-1}\,\prod_{a=1}^{\lambda^{(j)}}\,\biggl(\prod_{\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{1}{\scriptstyle c=1}{\scriptstyle i^{(j+1)}_c\?<\>i^{(j)}_a}}^{\lambda^{(j+1)}}\!\!(t^{(j)}_{a}\mskip-2.00002mu-t^{(j+1)}_{c})\prod_{\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{1}{\scriptstyle d=1}{\scriptstyle i^{(j+1)}_d>\>i^{(j)}_a}}^{\lambda^{(j+1)}}\!\!(t^{(j)}_{a}\mskip-2.00002mu-t^{(j+1)}_{d}-h)\,\prod_{b=a+1}^{\lambda^{(j)}}\frac{t^{(j)}_{b}\mskip-2.00002mu-t^{(j)}_{a}\mskip-2.00002mu-h}{t^{(j)}_{b}\mskip-2.00002mu-t^{(j)}_{a}}\,\biggr)\,.\kern-10.00002pt

Example
.

Let
N=2
,
n=2
,
{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}=(1,1)
,
I=(\{1\},\{2\})
,
J=(\{2\},\{1\})
. Then

W_{I}({\boldsymbol{t}}\mskip 0.99998mu;{\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h)\,=\,t^{(1)}_{1}\mskip-2.00002mu-z_{2}-h\,,\qquad W_{J}({\boldsymbol{t}}\mskip 0.99998mu;{\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h)\,=\,t^{(1)}_{1}\mskip-2.00002mu-z_{1}\,.\ignorespaces

Lemma 4.1

(
[
RTV1
,
TV6
]
)
.

For any
\,I\in{{\mathcal{I}}_{{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}}}\,
,
\,i=1,\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak N-1\,
, and
\,a=1,\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak n-1\,
, we have

(4.3)

\displaystyle W_{s_{a\mskip-0.99998mu,a+1}(I)}({\boldsymbol{t}}\mskip 0.99998mu;{\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h)\,={}

\displaystyle{}=\,\frac{z_{a}\mskip-0.99998mu-z_{a+1}\mskip-0.99998mu-h}{z_{a}\mskip-0.99998mu-z_{a+1}}\,W_{I}({\boldsymbol{t}}\mskip 0.99998mu;z_{1},\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak z_{a+1},z_{a},\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak z_{n};h)\mskip 2.00002mu+\mskip 2.00002mu\frac{h}{z_{a}\mskip-0.99998mu-z_{a+1}}\,W_{I}({\boldsymbol{t}}\mskip 0.99998mu;{\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h)\,.

Remark 4.2
.

Define the operators
\hat{s}_{1},\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak\hat{s}_{n-1}
acting on functions of
\,z_{1},\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak z_{n}\,
:

(4.4)

\hat{s}_{a}\mskip 0.99998muf({\boldsymbol{z}})\,=\,\frac{z_{a}\mskip-0.99998mu-z_{a+1}\mskip-0.99998mu-h}{z_{a}\mskip-0.99998mu-z_{a+1}}\,f(z_{1},\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak z_{a+1},z_{a},\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak z_{n})\mskip 2.00002mu+\mskip 2.00002mu\frac{h}{z_{a}\mskip-0.99998mu-z_{a+1}}\,f({\boldsymbol{z}})\,.\kern-16.00008pt\ignorespaces

The assignment
s_{a,\mskip 0.99998mua+1}\mapsto\hat{s}_{a}\,,\;\;a=1,\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak n-1\,
, yields
a representation of the symmetric group
\,S_{n}\,
.

Set

(4.5)

c_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}({\boldsymbol{t}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h)\,=\mskip 2.00002mu\prod_{i=1}^{N-1}\,\prod_{a=1}^{\lambda^{(i)}}\mskip 2.00002mu\prod_{\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{1}{\scriptstyle b=1}{\scriptstyle b\ne a}}^{\lambda^{(i)}}\,(t^{(i)}_{a}\mskip-0.99998mu-t^{(i)}_{b}\mskip-0.99998mu-h)\,.\ignorespaces

Recall the permutations
\,\sigma_{I}\,
,
\,I\mskip-0.99998mu\in{{\mathcal{I}}_{{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}}}\,
, defined in Section

3.2
.

Lemma 4.3

(
[
RTV1
]
)
.

We have
\;W_{J}(\Sigma_{I}\mskip 0.99998mu;{\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h)=0\mskip 2.00002mu
unless
\,I=J
or

\,|\mskip 0.99998mu\sigma_{I}\mskip 0.99998mu|>|\mskip 0.99998mu\sigma_{J}\mskip 0.99998mu|\,
, and

(4.6)

W_{I}(\Sigma_{I}\mskip 0.99998mu;{\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h)\,=\,c_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}(\Sigma_{I}\mskip 0.99998mu;h)\;\prod_{j=1}^{N-1}\mskip 2.00002mu\prod_{k=j+1}^{N}\,\prod_{a\in I_{j}}\mskip 2.00002mu\biggl(\,\prod_{\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{1}{\scriptstyle b\in I_k}{\scriptstyle b<a}}\mskip 0.99998mu(z_{a}\mskip-0.99998mu-z_{b})\,\prod_{\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{1}{\scriptstyle b\in I_k}{\scriptstyle b>a}}\mskip 0.99998mu(z_{a}\mskip-0.99998mu-z_{b}\mskip-0.99998mu-h)\mskip-0.99998mu\biggr)\mskip 2.00002mu.\kern-13.99995pt\ignorespaces

Lemma 4.4
.

For any
\,J\mskip-0.99998mu\in{{\mathcal{I}}_{{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}}}\mskip 2.00002mu
, the polynomial
\;W_{J}(\Sigma_{I}\mskip 0.99998mu;{\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h)\mskip 2.00002mu

is divisible by

c_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}(\Sigma_{I}\mskip 0.99998mu;h)\;\prod_{j=1}^{N-1}\mskip 2.00002mu\prod_{k=j+1}^{N}\,\prod_{a\in I_{j}}\mskip 2.00002mu\prod_{\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{1}{\scriptstyle b\in I_k}{\scriptstyle b>a}}\,(z_{a}\mskip-0.99998mu-z_{b}\mskip-0.99998mu-h)\,.\kern-13.99995pt\ignorespaces

Lemma
4.4
is a version of
[
RTV2
, Lemma 3.1, item (I)]
.
A stronger more technical analogue of Lemma
4.4
is given by
Lemma
4.14
in Section
4.5
.

Let
\,\sigma_{0}\,
be the longest permutation,

\,\sigma_{0}(i)=n+1-i\,
,
\;i-1,\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak n\,
.
For
\,I\mskip-0.99998mu\in{{\mathcal{I}}_{{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}}}\,
, define

(4.7)

{\check{W}}_{I}({\boldsymbol{t}}\mskip 0.99998mu;{\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h)\,=\,W_{\sigma_{0}(I)}({\boldsymbol{t}}\mskip 0.99998mu;z_{n},\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak z_{1}\mskip 0.99998mu;h)\,.

Set

(4.8)

R_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}({\boldsymbol{z}})\,=\mskip 0.99998mu\prod_{i=1}^{N-1}\,\prod_{a=1}^{\lambda^{(i)}}\,\prod_{b=\lambda^{(i)}+1}^{\lambda^{(i+1)}}\!\mskip-2.00002mu(z_{a}\mskip-0.99998mu-z_{b})\,,\kern 18.99995ptQ_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h)\,=\mskip 0.99998mu\prod_{i=1}^{N-1}\,\prod_{a=1}^{\lambda^{(i)}}\,\prod_{b=\lambda^{(i)}+1}^{\lambda^{(i+1)}}\!\mskip-2.00002mu(z_{a}\mskip-0.99998mu-z_{b}\mskip-0.99998mu-h)\,,\kern-11.99998pt\ignorespaces

For
\,\sigma\mskip-0.99998mu\in\mskip-0.99998muS_{n}\,
, denote
\,{\boldsymbol{z}}_{\sigma}\mskip-0.99998mu=(z_{\sigma(1)},\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak z_{\sigma(n)})\,
.

Proposition 4.5

(
[
RTV1
, Lemma 3.4]
)
.

The functions
\,W_{I}({\boldsymbol{t}}\mskip 0.99998mu;{\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h)\mskip 2.00002mu
and
\,{\check{W}}_{J}({\boldsymbol{t}}\mskip 0.99998mu;{\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h)\mskip 2.00002mu

are biorthogonal,

(4.9)

\sum_{I\in{{\mathcal{I}}_{{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}}}}\,\frac{W_{J}(\Sigma_{I}\mskip 0.99998mu;{\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h)\,{\check{W}}_{K}(\Sigma_{I}\mskip 0.99998mu;{\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h)}{c_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}^{\mskip 2.00002mu2}(\Sigma_{I}\mskip 0.99998mu;h)\mskip 2.00002muR_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}({\boldsymbol{z}}_{\sigma_{I}}\mskip-0.99998mu)\mskip 2.00002muQ_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}({\boldsymbol{z}}_{\sigma_{I}};h)}\,=\,\delta_{J,\mskip 0.99998muK}\,.\kern-20.00003pt\ignorespaces

Corollary 4.6
.

We have

(4.10)

\sum_{I\in{{\mathcal{I}}_{{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}}}}\,W_{I}(\Sigma_{J}\mskip 0.99998mu;{\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h)\,{\check{W}}_{I}(\Sigma_{K};{\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h)\,=\,\delta_{J,\mskip 0.99998muK}\,c_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}^{\mskip 2.00002mu2}(\Sigma_{J}\mskip 0.99998mu;h)\mskip 2.00002muR_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}({\boldsymbol{z}}_{\sigma_{J}}\mskip-0.99998mu)\mskip 2.00002muQ_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}({\boldsymbol{z}}_{\sigma_{J}};h)\,.\kern-20.00003pt\ignorespaces

4.2.
Master function

Let
\,\phi(x,h,\kappa)=\Gamma(x/\kappa)\,\Gamma\bigl(\mskip-0.99998mu(h-x)/\kappa\bigr)\,
.
Define the
master function
:

(4.11)

\displaystyle\Phi_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}({\boldsymbol{t}}\mskip 0.99998mu;{\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h;{\boldsymbol{q}}\mskip 0.99998mu;\kappa)\,=\,(e^{\mskip 0.99998mu\pi\sqrt{\mskip-0.99998mu-1}\,(n\mskip 0.99998mu-\mskip-0.99998mu\lambda_{N})}q_{N})^{\mskip 2.00002mu\sum_{a=1}^{n}z_{a}\mskip-0.99998mu/\mskip-0.99998mu\kappa}\,\prod_{i=1}^{N-1}\mskip 2.00002mu\Bigl(\mskip 2.00002mue^{\mskip 0.99998mu\pi\sqrt{\mskip-0.99998mu-1}\,(\lambda_{i+1}-\lambda_{i})}\mskip 2.00002mu\frac{q_{i}}{q_{i+1}}\mskip 2.00002mu\Bigr)^{\sum_{a=1}^{\lambda^{(i)}}\mskip 0.99998mut^{(i)}_{a}\!\mskip-0.99998mu/\kappa}\times{}

\displaystyle{}\times\mskip-0.99998mu{}\prod_{i=1}^{N-1}\,\prod_{a=1}^{\lambda^{(i)}}\,\biggl(\,\prod_{\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{1}{\scriptstyle b=1}{\scriptstyle b\ne a}}^{\lambda^{(i)}}\,\frac{1}{(t_{a}^{(i)}\mskip-2.00002mu-t_{b}^{(i)}\mskip-2.00002mu-h)\,\phi(t_{a}^{(i)}\mskip-2.00002mu-t_{b}^{(i)}\mskip-0.99998mu,h,\kappa)}\,\prod_{c=1}^{\lambda^{(i+1)}}\mskip-0.99998mu\phi(t_{a}^{(i)}\mskip-2.00002mu-t_{c}^{(i+1)}\mskip-0.99998mu,h,\kappa)\mskip-0.99998mu\biggr)\,

\displaystyle,\kern-10.00002pt

where
\,\lambda^{(N)}\mskip-2.00002mu=n\,
and
\,t^{(N)}_{a}\mskip-2.00002mu=z_{a}\,
,
\;a=1,\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak n\,
.
The function
\,\mskip 0.99998mu\Phi_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}({\boldsymbol{t}}\mskip 0.99998mu;{\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h;{\boldsymbol{q}}\mskip 0.99998mu;\kappa)\,
is symmetric in
the variables
\;t^{(i)}_{1}\mskip-2.00002mu,\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak t^{(i)}_{\lambda^{(i)}}\mskip 0.99998mu
for each
i=1,\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak N-1
.

4.3.
Jackson integrals

Let
\,\lambda^{\{1\}}\mskip-0.99998mu=\sum_{i=1}^{N-1}\lambda^{(i)}\mskip 2.00002mu
. Consider the space

\,{\mathbb{C}}^{\lambda^{\{1\}}}\!\mskip-2.00002mu\times{\mathbb{C}}^{n}\!\times\allowbreak{\mathbb{C}}\times\allowbreak{\mathbb{C}}^{N}

with coordinates
{\boldsymbol{t}},{\boldsymbol{z}},h,{\boldsymbol{q}}
. The lattice
\kappa\mskip 2.00002mu{\mathbb{Z}}^{\lambda^{\{1\}}}\!

naturally acts on this space by shifting the
{\boldsymbol{t}}\mskip 0.99998mu
-coordinates.

For a function of
\,f({\boldsymbol{t}})
and a point
{\boldsymbol{s}}\mskip-0.99998mu\in{\mathbb{C}}^{\lambda^{\{1\}}}\!
,
define
\;\operatorname{Res}_{\mskip 2.00002mu{\boldsymbol{t}}={\boldsymbol{s}}}f({\boldsymbol{t}})\,
to be the iterated
residue,

\operatorname{Res}_{\mskip 2.00002mu{\boldsymbol{t}}={\boldsymbol{s}}}f({\boldsymbol{t}})\mskip 2.00002mu=\,\operatorname{Res}_{t^{(1)}_{1}\mskip-0.99998mu=\mskip 2.00002mus^{(1)}_{1}}\ldots\mskip 2.00002mu\operatorname{Res}_{t^{(1)}_{\lambda^{(1)}}\mskip-0.99998mu=\mskip 2.00002mus^{(1)}_{\lambda^{(1)}}}\ldots\;\operatorname{Res}_{t^{(N-1)}_{1}\mskip-0.99998mu=\mskip 2.00002mus^{(N-1)}_{1}}\ldots\mskip 2.00002mu\operatorname{Res}_{t^{(N-1)}_{\lambda^{(N-1)}}\mskip-0.99998mu=\mskip 2.00002mus^{(N-1)}_{\lambda^{(N-1)}}}f({\boldsymbol{t}})\,.\kern-5.0pt

Let
\,L^{\prime}\mskip 0.99998mu
be the complement in
\,{\mathbb{C}}^{n}\!\times{\mathbb{C}}\,
of the union of
the hyperplanes

(4.12)

h\mskip 2.00002mu=\mskip 2.00002mum\mskip 0.99998mu\kappa\,,\qquad z_{a}\mskip-0.99998mu-z_{b}\mskip 2.00002mu=\mskip 2.00002mum\mskip 0.99998mu\kappa\,,\qquad z_{a}\mskip-0.99998mu-z_{b}\mskip-0.99998mu+h\mskip 2.00002mu=\mskip 2.00002mum\mskip 0.99998mu\kappa\,,\ignorespaces

for all
a,b=1,\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak n
,
a\neq b
, and all
\,m\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\leqslant 0}\,
.
Let
L^{\prime\prime}\!\subset{\mathbb{C}}^{N}
be the domain

(4.13)

|\mskip 0.99998muq_{i+1}/q_{i}|<1\,,\qquad i=1,\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak N-1\,,\ignorespaces

with additional cuts fixing a branch of
\,\mskip 0.99998mu\log\mskip 0.99998muq_{i}\mskip 0.99998mu
for all
i=1,\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak N
.
Set
L=L^{\prime}\!\times L^{\prime\prime}\!\subset\mskip-0.99998mu{\mathbb{C}}^{n}\!\times{\mathbb{C}}\times{\mathbb{C}}^{N}
.

Let
\,{\boldsymbol{l}}=\bigl(\mskip 2.00002mul^{\mskip 0.99998mu(1)}_{\mskip 2.00002mu1}\!,\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak l^{\mskip 0.99998mu(1)}_{\mskip 0.99998mu\lambda^{(1)}},\,\ldots\,,l^{\mskip 0.99998mu(N-1)}_{\mskip 2.00002mu1}\!,\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak l^{\mskip 0.99998mu(N-1)}_{\mskip 0.99998mu\lambda^{(N-1)}}\bigr)\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{\lambda^{\{1\}}}\mskip-2.00002mu
.
By convention, set
\,l^{\mskip 0.99998mu(i)}_{a}\!=0\,
for
\,a>\mskip-0.99998mu\lambda^{(i)}
or
\,i=N\mskip 2.00002mu
.

Let
\,f(\mskip 0.99998mu{\boldsymbol{t}}\mskip 0.99998mu;{\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h;{\boldsymbol{q}})
be a polynomial in
{\boldsymbol{t}}
and a holomorphic
function of
{\boldsymbol{z}},h,{\boldsymbol{q}}\,
in
L
. For
({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h;{\boldsymbol{q}})\in L
, define

(4.14)

{\mathcal{M}}_{J}(\Phi_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\mskip 0.99998muf)({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h;{\boldsymbol{q}}\mskip 0.99998mu;\kappa)\,=\!\sum_{{\boldsymbol{l}}\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{\mskip 0.99998mu\lambda^{\{1\}}}\!\!\!\!}\operatorname{Res}_{\mskip 2.00002mu{\boldsymbol{t}}\mskip 2.00002mu=\mskip 2.00002mu\Sigma_{J}-\mskip 0.99998mu{\boldsymbol{l}}\kappa\mskip 2.00002mu}\bigl(\Phi_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}({\boldsymbol{t}}\mskip 0.99998mu;{\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h;{\boldsymbol{q}}\mskip 0.99998mu;\kappa)\mskip 2.00002muf({\boldsymbol{t}}\mskip 0.99998mu;{\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h;{\boldsymbol{q}})\bigr)\,.

This sum is called the
Jackson integral over the discrete cycle

\;\widehat{\Sigma}_{J}\mskip-0.99998mu\subset{\mathbb{C}}^{\lambda^{\{1\}}}\!\mskip-2.00002mu\times{\mathbb{C}}^{n}\!\times\allowbreak{\mathbb{C}}\times\allowbreak{\mathbb{C}}^{N}\mskip-2.00002mu
,

\widehat{\Sigma}_{J}\mskip 2.00002mu=\,\{(\mskip 0.99998mu\Sigma_{J}-{\boldsymbol{l}}\kappa\mskip 0.99998mu;{\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h\mskip 0.99998mu;{\boldsymbol{q}}\mskip 0.99998mu)\;\;|\,\,\,\,{\boldsymbol{l}}\mskip-0.99998mu\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{\lambda^{\{1\}}}\!,\;\;({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h\mskip 0.99998mu;{\boldsymbol{q}}\mskip 0.99998mu)\in L\mskip 2.00002mu\}\,.\ignorespaces

Notice that the master function
\,\Phi_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}({\boldsymbol{t}}\mskip 0.99998mu;{\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h;{\boldsymbol{q}}\mskip 0.99998mu;\kappa)\,

has only simple poles, and

\displaystyle\operatorname{Res}_{\mskip 2.00002mu{\boldsymbol{t}}\mskip 2.00002mu=\mskip 2.00002mu\Sigma_{J}-\mskip 0.99998mu{\boldsymbol{l}}\kappa\mskip 2.00002mu}\bigl(\Phi_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}({\boldsymbol{t}}\mskip 0.99998mu;{\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h;{\boldsymbol{q}}\mskip 0.99998mu;\kappa)\mskip 2.00002muf({\boldsymbol{t}}\mskip 0.99998mu;{\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h;{\boldsymbol{q}})\bigr)\,={}

\displaystyle\;{}=\,f(\Sigma_{J}\mskip-0.99998mu-{\boldsymbol{l}}\kappa\mskip 0.99998mu;{\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h;{\boldsymbol{q}})\,\operatorname{Res}_{\mskip 2.00002mu{\boldsymbol{t}}\mskip 2.00002mu=\mskip 2.00002mu\Sigma_{J}-\mskip 0.99998mu{\boldsymbol{l}}\kappa\mskip 2.00002mu}\Phi_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}({\boldsymbol{t}}\mskip 0.99998mu;{\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h;{\boldsymbol{q}}\mskip 0.99998mu;\kappa)\,.

A closed expression for the residue

\,\operatorname{Res}_{\mskip 2.00002mu{\boldsymbol{t}}\mskip 2.00002mu=\mskip 2.00002mu\Sigma_{J}-\mskip 0.99998mu{\boldsymbol{l}}\kappa\mskip 2.00002mu}\Phi_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}({\boldsymbol{t}}\mskip 0.99998mu;{\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h;{\boldsymbol{q}}\mskip 0.99998mu;\kappa)\,

is given by Lemma
4.7
below.

Recall
\,c_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}({\boldsymbol{t}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h)\,
, see (
4.5
). Set

(4.15)

M_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;\kappa)\,=\,\prod_{i=1}^{N-1}\mskip 2.00002mu\prod_{a=1}^{\lambda^{(i)}}\,\prod_{b=\lambda^{(i)}+1}^{\lambda^{(i+1)}}\!\frac{\sin\mskip 0.99998mu\bigl(\pi\mskip 2.00002mu(z_{a}\mskip-2.00002mu-z_{b})/\kappa\bigr)}{\pi\mskip 2.00002mue^{\mskip 0.99998mu\pi\sqrt{\mskip-0.99998mu-1}\mskip 2.00002mu(z_{a}+\mskip 0.99998muz_{b})/\kappa}}\;,\kern-20.00003pt\ignorespaces

and

(4.16)

A_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}({\boldsymbol{t}}\mskip 0.99998mu;{\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h;\kappa)\,=\,\prod_{i=1}^{N-1}\,\prod_{a=1}^{\lambda^{(i)}}\;\biggl(\,\prod_{\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{1}{\scriptstyle b=1}{\scriptstyle b\ne a}}^{\lambda^{(i)}}\mskip 2.00002mu\frac{\Gamma\bigl(1+(t^{(i)}_{b}\!-t^{(i)}_{a})/\kappa\bigr)}{\Gamma\bigl(\mskip-0.99998mu(t^{(i)}_{b}\!-t^{(i)}_{a}\!+h)/\kappa\bigr)}\;\prod_{c=1}^{\lambda^{(i+1)}}\mskip 0.99998mu\frac{\Gamma\bigl(\mskip-0.99998mu(t^{(i+1)}_{c}\!-t^{(i)}_{a}\!+h)/\kappa\bigr)}{\Gamma\bigl(1+(t^{(i+1)}_{c}\!-t^{(i)}_{a})/\kappa\bigr)}\,\biggr)\mskip 2.00002mu.\kern-6.99997pt

Lemma 4.7
.

If
\;{\boldsymbol{l}}\not\in\mskip-0.99998mu{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geqslant 0}^{\lambda^{\{1\}}}\mskip-2.00002mu
, then

\;\operatorname{Res}_{\mskip 2.00002mu{\boldsymbol{t}}\mskip 2.00002mu=\mskip 2.00002mu\Sigma_{J}-\mskip 0.99998mu{\boldsymbol{l}}\kappa\mskip 2.00002mu}\Phi_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}({\boldsymbol{t}}\mskip 0.99998mu;{\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h;{\boldsymbol{q}}\mskip 0.99998mu;\kappa)=0\,
.
For
\;{\boldsymbol{l}}\in\mskip-0.99998mu{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geqslant 0}^{\lambda^{\{1\}}}\mskip-2.00002mu
,

(4.17)

\displaystyle\operatorname{Res}_{\mskip 2.00002mu{\boldsymbol{t}}\mskip 2.00002mu=\mskip 2.00002mu\Sigma_{J}-\mskip 0.99998mu{\boldsymbol{l}}\kappa}\Phi_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}({\boldsymbol{t}}\mskip 0.99998mu;{\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h;{\boldsymbol{q}}\mskip 0.99998mu;\kappa)\,={}

\displaystyle{}=\,\frac{\kappa^{\mskip 0.99998mu\lambda^{\{1\}}}\mskip-2.00002muA_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}(\Sigma_{J}\mskip-0.99998mu-{\boldsymbol{l}}\kappa\mskip 0.99998mu;{\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h;\kappa)}{M_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}({\boldsymbol{z}}_{\sigma_{J}}\mskip 0.99998mu;\kappa)\mskip 2.00002muc_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}(\Sigma_{J}\mskip-0.99998mu-{\boldsymbol{l}}\kappa;h)}\;\prod_{i=1}^{N}\mskip 2.00002muq_{i}^{\mskip 2.00002mu\sum_{a\in J_{i}}\mskip-2.00002muz_{a}\mskip-0.99998mu/\mskip-0.99998mu\kappa}\,\prod_{i=1}^{N-1}\,(q_{i+1}/q_{i})^{\mskip 2.00002mu\sum_{a=1}^{\lambda^{(i)}}l_{a}^{(i)}}\!.

In particular,

(4.18)

\displaystyle\operatorname{Res}_{\mskip 2.00002mu{\boldsymbol{t}}\mskip 2.00002mu=\mskip 2.00002mu\Sigma_{J}}\mskip-2.00002mu\Phi_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}({\boldsymbol{t}}\mskip 0.99998mu;{\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h;{\boldsymbol{q}}\mskip 0.99998mu;\kappa)\,

\displaystyle{}=\,\frac{\bigl(\kappa\mskip 2.00002mu\Gamma(h/\mskip-0.99998mu\kappa)\bigr)^{\mskip-0.99998mu\lambda^{\{1\}}}}{c_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}(\Sigma_{J}\mskip 0.99998mu;h)}\;\prod_{i=1}^{N}\mskip 2.00002mu\bigl(\mskip 2.00002mue^{\mskip 0.99998mu\pi\sqrt{\mskip-0.99998mu-1}\,(n-\lambda_{i})}\mskip 2.00002muq_{i}\mskip 0.99998mu\bigr)^{\mskip 2.00002mu\sum_{a\in J_{i}}\mskip-2.00002muz_{a}\mskip-0.99998mu/\mskip-0.99998mu\kappa}\mskip 0.99998mu\times{}

\displaystyle\mskip 2.00002mu{}\times\,\,\prod_{i=1}^{N-1}\prod_{j=i+1}^{N}\,\mskip 0.99998mu\prod_{a\in J_{i}}\,\prod_{b\in J_{j}}\,\Gamma\bigl(\mskip-0.99998mu(z_{a}\mskip-0.99998mu-z_{b})/\kappa\bigr)\,\Gamma\bigl(\mskip-0.99998mu(z_{b}\mskip-0.99998mu-z_{a}\mskip-0.99998mu+h)/\kappa\bigr)\,.\kern-10.00002pt

By Lemma
4.7
, the actual summation in formula (
4.14
) is only
over the positive cone of the lattice,

(4.19)

{\mathcal{M}}_{J}(\Phi_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\mskip 0.99998muf)({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h;{\boldsymbol{q}}\mskip 0.99998mu;\kappa)\,=\mskip 2.00002mu\sum_{{\boldsymbol{l}}\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geqslant 0}^{\mskip 0.99998mu\lambda^{\{1\}}}\!}f(\Sigma_{J}\mskip-0.99998mu-{\boldsymbol{l}}\kappa\mskip 0.99998mu;{\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h;{\boldsymbol{q}})\,\operatorname{Res}_{\mskip 2.00002mu{\boldsymbol{t}}\mskip 2.00002mu=\mskip 2.00002mu\Sigma_{J}-\mskip 0.99998mu{\boldsymbol{l}}\kappa}\Phi_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}({\boldsymbol{t}}\mskip 0.99998mu;{\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h;{\boldsymbol{q}}\mskip 0.99998mu;\kappa)\,.\kern-20.00003pt

4.4.
Solutions of the dynamical and
qKZ
equations

For
J\mskip-0.99998mu\in{{\mathcal{I}}_{{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}}}
, define

(4.20)

\Psi_{\mskip-2.00002muJ}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h;{\boldsymbol{q}}\mskip 0.99998mu;\kappa)\,=\,\bigl(\kappa\mskip 2.00002mu\Gamma(h/\mskip-0.99998mu\kappa)\bigr)^{\mskip-0.99998mu-\mskip 0.99998mu\lambda^{\{1\}}}\mskip 2.00002mu\Omega_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}(h;{\boldsymbol{q}}\mskip 0.99998mu;\kappa)\,\sum_{I\in{{\mathcal{I}}_{{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}}}}\,{\mathcal{M}}_{J}(\Phi_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}W_{I})({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h;{\boldsymbol{q}}\mskip 0.99998mu;\kappa)\,v_{I}\,,\ignorespaces

where

(4.21)

\Omega_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}(h;{\boldsymbol{q}}\mskip 0.99998mu;\kappa)\,=\,\prod_{i=1}^{N-1}\prod_{j=i+1}^{N}(1-q_{j}/q_{i})^{h\mskip 0.99998mu\lambda_{i}/\mskip-0.99998mu\kappa}\mskip 2.00002mu.\kern-10.00002pt\ignorespaces

Definition 4.8
.

Say that a function
\,f({\boldsymbol{q}})\,
is holomorphic in the unit polydisk
around
\,{\boldsymbol{q}}={\mathchoice{\raise 0.68889pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\mskip 2.00002mu\wr$\hss}0}{\raise 0.68889pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\mskip 2.00002mu\wr$\hss}0}{\raise 0.55199pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\mskip 2.00002mu\wr$\hss}0}{\raise 0.38748pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\mskip 2.00002mu\wr$\hss}0}}\,
if
\,f({\boldsymbol{q}})=g(q_{2}/q_{1},\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak q_{N}/q_{N-1})\,
for
a function
\,g(s_{1},\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak s_{N-1})\,
holomorphic in
\,s_{1},\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak s_{N-1}\,
,
provided
\,|\mskip 0.99998mus_{i}\mskip 0.99998mu|<1\,
for all
\,i=1,\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak N-1\,
.
Denote
\,f({\mathchoice{\raise 0.68889pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\mskip 2.00002mu\wr$\hss}0}{\raise 0.68889pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\mskip 2.00002mu\wr$\hss}0}{\raise 0.55199pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\mskip 2.00002mu\wr$\hss}0}{\raise 0.38748pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\mskip 2.00002mu\wr$\hss}0}})=g(0,\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak 0)\,
.

In the given definition, we described homogeneous functions of
\,{\boldsymbol{q}}\,

that have behave regularly as the ratios of the subsequent coordinates
approaches zero. The symbol
\,{\mathchoice{\raise 0.68889pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\mskip 2.00002mu\wr$\hss}0}{\raise 0.68889pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\mskip 2.00002mu\wr$\hss}0}{\raise 0.55199pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\mskip 2.00002mu\wr$\hss}0}{\raise 0.38748pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\mskip 2.00002mu\wr$\hss}0}}\mskip 0.99998mu
used in the definition is formal
and does not represent any point of
\,{\mathbb{C}}^{n}
.

Theorem 4.9
.

The
\,({\mathbb{C}}^{N})^{\otimes\mskip 0.99998mun}_{\mskip 0.99998mu{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}}
-valued function
\,\Psi_{\mskip-2.00002muJ}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h;{\boldsymbol{q}}\mskip 0.99998mu;\kappa)\mskip 0.99998mu
is
a solution of the joint system of dynamical differential equations (
2.7
)
and
qKZ
difference equations (
2.5
). It has the form

(4.22)

\displaystyle\Psi_{\mskip-2.00002muJ}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h;{\boldsymbol{q}}\mskip 0.99998mu;\kappa)\,

\displaystyle{}=\,\Psi^{\raise 1.20552pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\diamond$}}_{\mskip-2.00002muJ}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h;{\boldsymbol{q}}\mskip 0.99998mu;\kappa)\;\prod_{i=1}^{N}\,\bigl(\mskip 2.00002mue^{\mskip 0.99998mu\pi\sqrt{\mskip-0.99998mu-1}\,(n-\lambda_{i})}\mskip 2.00002muq_{i}\mskip 0.99998mu\bigr)^{\mskip 2.00002mu\sum_{a\in J_{i}}\mskip-2.00002muz_{a}\mskip-0.99998mu/\mskip-0.99998mu\kappa}\mskip 0.99998mu\times{}

\displaystyle\mskip 2.00002mu{}\times\,\,\prod_{i=1}^{N-1}\prod_{j=i+1}^{N}\,\mskip 0.99998mu\prod_{a\in J_{i}}\mskip 2.00002mu\biggl(\,\prod_{b\in J_{j}}\mskip 2.00002mu\frac{\Gamma\bigl(1+(z_{b}\mskip-0.99998mu-z_{a}\mskip-0.99998mu+h)/\kappa\bigr)}{\sin\bigl(\pi\mskip 2.00002mu(z_{a}\mskip-0.99998mu-z_{b})/\kappa\bigr)}\;\prod_{\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{1}{\scriptstyle c\in J_j}{\scriptstyle c<a}}\mskip 2.00002mu\frac{1}{z_{a}\mskip-0.99998mu-z_{c}\mskip-0.99998mu-h}\mskip 0.99998mu\biggr)\mskip 2.00002mu,\kern-8.00003pt

where the function
\,\Psi^{\raise 1.20552pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\diamond$}}_{\mskip-2.00002muJ}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h;{\boldsymbol{q}}\mskip 0.99998mu;\kappa)\mskip 2.00002mu
is entire in
\,{\boldsymbol{z}},h\mskip 2.00002mu

and holomorphic in
\,{\boldsymbol{q}}\mskip 2.00002mu
in the unit polydisk around
\,{\boldsymbol{q}}={\mathchoice{\raise 0.68889pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\mskip 2.00002mu\wr$\hss}0}{\raise 0.68889pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\mskip 2.00002mu\wr$\hss}0}{\raise 0.55199pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\mskip 2.00002mu\wr$\hss}0}{\raise 0.38748pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\mskip 2.00002mu\wr$\hss}0}}\,
.
In more detail,

(4.23)

\displaystyle\Psi^{\raise 1.20552pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\diamond$}}_{\mskip-2.00002muJ}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h;{\boldsymbol{q}}\mskip 2.00002mu;\kappa)\,

\displaystyle{}=\,\prod_{i=1}^{N-1}\prod_{j=i+1}^{N}\,\mskip 0.99998mu\prod_{a\in J_{i}}\mskip 2.00002mu\prod_{b\in J_{j}}\mskip 2.00002mu\frac{-\mskip 2.00002mu\pi\mskip 2.00002mu\kappa}{\Gamma\bigl(1+(z_{b}\mskip-0.99998mu-z_{a})/\kappa\bigr)}\,\times{}

\displaystyle\mskip 2.00002mu{}\times\,\biggl(\Psi^{\raise 1.20552pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\diamond$}}_{\mskip-0.99998muJ,\mskip 0.99998mu0}\mskip 0.99998mu({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h)\mskip 2.00002mu+\!\!\sum_{\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{1}{\scriptstyle\,\mb\in\Z_{\ge 0}^{N-1}\!}{\scriptstyle\mb\ne 0}}\!\mskip-2.00002mu\Psi^{\raise 1.20552pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\diamond$}}_{\mskip-0.99998muJ,\mskip 0.99998mu{\boldsymbol{m}}}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h;\kappa)\,\prod_{i=1}^{N\mskip-0.99998mu-1}\mskip 2.00002mu(q_{i+1}/q_{i})^{m_{i}}\mskip-0.99998mu\biggr)\mskip 0.99998mu,\kern-20.00003pt

where

(4.24)

\displaystyle\Psi^{\raise 1.20552pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\diamond$}}_{\mskip-0.99998muJ,\mskip 0.99998mu0}\mskip 0.99998mu({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h)\,=\,\frac{1}{c_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}(\Sigma_{J}\mskip 0.99998mu;h)}\;\prod_{i=1}^{N-1}\prod_{j=i+1}^{N}\,\mskip 0.99998mu\prod_{a\in J_{i}}\mskip 2.00002mu\prod_{\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{1}{\scriptstyle b\in J_j}{\scriptstyle b>a}}\mskip 2.00002mu\frac{1}{z_{a}\mskip-0.99998mu-z_{b}\mskip-0.99998mu-h}\,\times{}\!\kern-20.00003pt

\displaystyle{}\times\Bigl(\mskip 0.99998muW_{J}(\Sigma_{J}\mskip 0.99998mu;{\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h)\,v_{J}\,+\!\sum_{\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{1}{\scriptstyle I\<\in\Il}{\scriptstyle|\si_I\<|<|\si_J\<|}}\!\!W_{I}(\Sigma_{J}\mskip 0.99998mu;{\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h)\,v_{I}\mskip 2.00002mu\Bigr)\kern-20.00003pt

is a polynomial in
\mskip 2.00002mu{\boldsymbol{z}},h\,
, and

\,\Psi^{\raise 1.20552pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\diamond$}}_{\mskip-0.99998muJ,\mskip 0.99998mu{\boldsymbol{m}}}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h;\kappa)
for
\,{\boldsymbol{m}}\neq 0\mskip 2.00002mu
are rational functions
of
\,\mskip 0.99998mu{\boldsymbol{z}},h,\kappa\mskip 2.00002mu
with at most simple poles
on the hyperplanes
\,z_{a}\mskip-0.99998mu-z_{b}\mskip-0.99998mu\in\mskip-0.99998mu\kappa\mskip 2.00002mu{\mathbb{Z}}_{>0}\mskip 2.00002mu
for
\,a\in\mskip-0.99998muJ_{i}\,
,

\,b\in\mskip-0.99998muJ_{j}\,
,
\,1\leqslant i<j\leqslant N\mskip 2.00002mu
. Furthermore, for any transposition

s_{a,\mskip 0.99998mub}\mskip-0.99998mu\in\mskip-0.99998muS_{n}\,
,

(4.25)

\Psi^{\raise 1.20552pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\diamond$}}_{\mskip-2.00002muJ}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h;{\boldsymbol{q}}\mskip 0.99998mu;\kappa)\big|_{\mskip 0.99998muz_{a}=z_{b}}=\,\Psi^{\raise 1.20552pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\diamond$}}_{\mskip-0.99998mus_{a\mskip-0.99998mu,b}(J)}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h;{\boldsymbol{q}}\mskip 0.99998mu;\kappa)\big|_{\mskip 0.99998muz_{a}=z_{b}}\mskip 0.99998mu.\ignorespaces

Proof.

The fact that
\,\Psi_{\mskip-2.00002muJ}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h;{\boldsymbol{q}}\mskip 0.99998mu;\kappa)\,
solves the dynamical equations
(
2.7
) is proved in
[
TV6
, Theorem 8.4]
, and the fact that

\,\Psi_{\mskip-2.00002muJ}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h;{\boldsymbol{q}}\mskip 0.99998mu;\kappa)
solves the
qKZ
equations (
2.5
) is proved
in
[
TV1
, Theorem 1.5.2]
, cf.
[
TV4
]
.

Analytic properties of
\,\Psi^{\raise 1.20552pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\diamond$}}_{\mskip-2.00002muJ}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h;{\boldsymbol{q}}\mskip 0.99998mu;\kappa)\,
are proved
in Section
4.5
. The fact that the right-hand side of
formula (
