) is a polynomial in
\,{\boldsymbol{z}},h\,
follows from
Lemmas
4.3
,
4.4
.
∎

The functions
\,\Psi_{\mskip-2.00002muJ}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h;{\boldsymbol{q}}\mskip 0.99998mu;\kappa)
are called
the
multidimensional
hypergeometric solutions
of the dynamical equations. In
[
TV5
]
,
we constructed another type of solutions of the dynamical equations
using
multidimensional hypergeometric integrals.

The next theorem computes the determinant of coordinates of
solutions
\,\Psi_{\mskip-2.00002muJ}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h;{\boldsymbol{q}}\mskip 0.99998mu;\kappa)\,
and is analogous
to
[
TV6
, Theorem 11.3]
.

Theorem 4.10
.

Let
n\geqslant 2
. Then

(4.26)

\displaystyle\det\mskip 0.99998mu\bigl(

\displaystyle\mskip 2.00002mu\Omega_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}({\boldsymbol{q}}\mskip 0.99998mu,\kappa)\,{\mathcal{M}}_{J}(\Phi_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}W_{I})({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h;{\boldsymbol{q}}\mskip 0.99998mu;\kappa)\bigr)_{I\mskip-0.99998mu,\mskip 0.99998muJ\in\mskip 2.00002mu{{\mathcal{I}}_{{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}}}}\mskip-0.99998mu={}

\displaystyle\!\mskip-0.99998mu{}=\,\bigl(\kappa\mskip 2.00002mu\Gamma(h/\mskip-0.99998mu\kappa)\bigr)^{\mskip-0.99998mu\lambda^{\{1\}}d_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}}\mskip 2.00002mu\Bigl(\mskip 0.99998mue^{\mskip 0.99998mu2\pi\sqrt{\mskip-0.99998mu-1}\,(n-1)\mskip 2.00002mud^{(2)}_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}}\mskip 2.00002mu\prod_{i=1}^{N}\,q_{i}^{\mskip 0.99998mu\smash{d^{(1)}_{{\boldsymbol{\lambda}},i}}\vphantom{|}}\mskip 2.00002mu\Bigr)^{\sum_{a=1}^{n}z_{a}\mskip-0.99998mu/\mskip-2.00002mu\kappa}\times{}

\displaystyle\mskip-2.00002mu\times\,\prod_{a=1}^{n-1}\,\prod_{b=a+1}^{n}\biggl(\mskip 0.99998mu\frac{\pi\mskip 0.99998mu\kappa^{2}\mskip 2.00002mu\Gamma\bigl(\mskip-0.99998mu(z_{a}\mskip-0.99998mu-z_{b}\mskip-0.99998mu+h)/\kappa\bigr)\mskip 2.00002mu\Gamma\bigl(1+(z_{b}\mskip-0.99998mu-z_{a}\mskip-0.99998mu+h)/\kappa\bigr)}{\sin\bigl(\pi\mskip 2.00002mu(z_{a}\mskip-0.99998mu-z_{b})/\kappa\bigr)}\mskip 2.00002mu\biggr)^{\!d^{(2)}_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}}\!\mskip-0.99998mu,

where
\;\lambda^{\{1\}}\mskip-0.99998mu=\sum_{i=1}^{N-1}\mskip 0.99998mu(N\mskip-2.00002mu-i)\mskip 2.00002mu\lambda_{i}\mskip 2.00002mu
,

(4.27)

d_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\mskip 2.00002mu=\,\frac{n\mskip 0.99998mu!}{\lambda_{1}\mskip 0.99998mu!\ldots\lambda_{N}\mskip 0.99998mu!}\;,\qquad d^{(1)}_{{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\mskip 0.99998mu,\mskip 0.99998mui}\mskip 2.00002mu=\,\frac{\lambda_{i}\mskip 2.00002mu(n-1)\mskip 0.99998mu!}{\lambda_{1}\mskip 0.99998mu!\ldots\lambda_{N}\mskip 0.99998mu!}\;,\qquad d^{(2)}_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\mskip 2.00002mu=\,\frac{(n-2)\mskip 0.99998mu!}{\lambda_{1}\mskip 0.99998mu!\ldots\lambda_{N}\mskip 0.99998mu!}\,\sum_{i=1}^{N-1}\sum_{j=i+1}^{N}\lambda_{i}\mskip 2.00002mu\lambda_{j}\,.\kern-20.00003pt\ignorespaces

Proof.

Denote by
\,F({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;{\boldsymbol{q}})\,
the determinant in the left-hand side of
formula (
4.26
). By Theorem
4.9
, it solves the differential
equations

\Bigl(\mskip 0.99998mu\kappa\mskip 2.00002muq_{i}\mskip 0.99998mu\frac{\partial}{\partial q_{i}}\mskip 2.00002mu-\mskip 2.00002mu\operatorname{tr}\mskip 0.99998muX_{i}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h;{\boldsymbol{q}})|_{\mskip 0.99998mu({\mathbb{C}}^{N})^{\otimes\mskip 0.99998mun}_{\mskip 0.99998mu{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}}}\Bigr)\mskip 2.00002muF({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;{\boldsymbol{q}})\,=\,0\,,\qquad i=1,\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak N\mskip 2.00002mu,\kern-20.00003pt\ignorespaces

where
\,X_{i}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h;{\boldsymbol{q}})|_{\mskip 0.99998mu({\mathbb{C}}^{N})^{\otimes\mskip 0.99998mun}_{\mskip 0.99998mu{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}}}\,
are the restrictions of dynamical
Hamiltonians (
2.8
) to the invariant subspace
\,({\mathbb{C}}^{N})^{\otimes\mskip 0.99998mun}_{\mskip 0.99998mu{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}}
. Since

\;\operatorname{tr}\mskip 0.99998muX_{i}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h;{\boldsymbol{q}})|_{\mskip 0.99998mu({\mathbb{C}}^{N})^{\otimes\mskip 0.99998mun}_{\mskip 0.99998mu{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}}}=d^{(1)}_{{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\mskip 0.99998mu,\mskip 0.99998mui}\mskip 2.00002mu\sum_{a=1}^{n}z_{a}\,
,
the function
\,F({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;{\boldsymbol{q}})\,
equals the product of powers of
\,q_{1},\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak q_{n}\mskip 0.99998mu

in the right-hand side of formula (
4.26
) multiplied by a factor that
does not depend on
\,{\boldsymbol{q}}\,
. This factor can be found by taking the limit

q_{i+1}/q_{i}\to 0\,
for all
\,i=1,\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak N-1\,
, using Theorem
4.9
.
∎

Remark 4.11
.

By Theorem
4.9
, the determinant
\,F({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;{\boldsymbol{q}})\,
in
Theorem
4.10
solves the difference equations

(4.28)

F(z_{1},\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak z_{a}+\kappa,\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak z_{n};{\boldsymbol{q}})\,=\,\det K_{a}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h;{\boldsymbol{q}}\mskip 0.99998mu;\kappa)|_{\mskip 0.99998mu({\mathbb{C}}^{N})^{\otimes\mskip 0.99998mun}_{\mskip 0.99998mu{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}}}\,F({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;{\boldsymbol{q}})\,,\qquad a=1,\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak n\mskip 2.00002mu,\ignorespaces

where
\,K_{a}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h;{\boldsymbol{q}}\mskip 0.99998mu;\kappa)|_{\mskip 0.99998mu({\mathbb{C}}^{N})^{\otimes\mskip 0.99998mun}_{\mskip 0.99998mu{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}}}
are the restrictions of the
qKZ

operators (
2.3
) to the invariant subspace
\,({\mathbb{C}}^{N})^{\otimes\mskip 0.99998mun}_{\mskip 0.99998mu{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}}
. Equations
(
4.28
) determine the product of Gamma functions in the right-hand side
of formula (
4.26
) up to a
\mskip 0.99998mu\kappa\mskip 0.99998mu
-periodic function of
\,z_{1},\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak z_{n}\,
.

4.5.
Proof of Theorem
4.9

Recall
\,\lambda^{\{1\}}\mskip-0.99998mu=\sum_{i=1}^{N-1}\lambda^{(i)}
.
Set
\,\lambda^{\{2\}}=\,\sum_{i=1}^{N-1}\,\bigl(\lambda^{(i)}\bigr)^{2}
and

\,\lambda_{\{2\}}=\sum_{1\leqslant i<j\leqslant N}\mskip 2.00002mu\lambda_{i}\mskip 2.00002mu\lambda_{j}\,
. Notice the homogeneity
properties

(4.29)

\displaystyle\Phi_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}({\boldsymbol{t}}\mskip 0.99998mu;{\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h;{\boldsymbol{q}}\mskip 0.99998mu;\kappa)\,=\,\kappa^{\mskip 0.99998mu\lambda^{\{1\}}\mskip-0.99998mu-\mskip 0.99998mu\lambda^{\{2\}}}\Phi_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}({\boldsymbol{t}}\mskip 0.99998mu/\kappa;{\boldsymbol{z}}/\kappa;h/\kappa;{\boldsymbol{q}}\mskip 0.99998mu;\mskip 0.99998mu1)\,,\kern-10.00002pt

\displaystyle\Psi_{\mskip-2.00002muJ}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h;{\boldsymbol{q}}\mskip 0.99998mu;\kappa)\,=\,\kappa^{\mskip 0.99998mu\lambda^{\{1\}}\mskip-0.99998mu+\mskip 0.99998mu\lambda_{\{2\}}}\Psi_{\mskip-2.00002muJ}({\boldsymbol{z}}/\kappa;h/\kappa;{\boldsymbol{q}}\mskip 0.99998mu;\mskip 0.99998mu1)\,.\kern-20.00003pt

To simplify writing, we assume in this section that
\,\kappa=1\,
and omit
the corresponding argument in all functions. The general case can be recovered
by the homogeneity.

Recall
I^{\mskip 0.99998mu\min}_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}},\,A_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}({\boldsymbol{t}}\mskip 0.99998mu;{\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h)
, see (
3.6
), (
4.16
).
For
\,{\boldsymbol{l}}\mskip-0.99998mu\in\mskip-0.99998mu{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geqslant 0}^{\lambda^{\{1\}}}\mskip-2.00002mu
, define

(4.30)

B_{\mskip 0.99998mu{\boldsymbol{l}}}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h)\,=\,\frac{A_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}(\Sigma_{I^{\mskip 0.99998mu\min}_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}}\mskip-0.99998mu-{\boldsymbol{l}}\mskip 0.99998mu;{\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h)}{c_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}(\Sigma_{I^{\mskip 0.99998mu\min}_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}}\mskip-0.99998mu-{\boldsymbol{l}};h)}\;.\ignorespaces

Set

(4.31)

{\mathcal{Z}}_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\,=\,\{\mskip 2.00002mu{\boldsymbol{l}}\mskip-0.99998mu\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{\lambda^{\{1\}}}_{\geqslant 0}\,\mskip 0.99998mu|\ \,l^{\mskip 0.99998mu(i)}_{\mskip 0.99998mua}\mskip-2.00002mu\geqslant l^{\mskip 0.99998mu(i+1)}_{\mskip 0.99998mua}\mskip-0.99998mu,\ \;i=1,\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak N-1\,,\ \;a=1,\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak\lambda^{(i)}\mskip 0.99998mu\}\,.\kern-20.00003pt\ignorespaces

Lemma 4.12
.

If
\;{\boldsymbol{l}}\mskip-0.99998mu\not\in{\mathcal{Z}}_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\,
, then
\,B_{\mskip 0.99998mu{\boldsymbol{l}}}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h)=0\,
.
For
\;{\boldsymbol{l}}\mskip-0.99998mu\in{\mathcal{Z}}_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\,
,

(4.32)

\displaystyle B_{\mskip 0.99998mu{\boldsymbol{l}}}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h)\,=\,\prod_{i=1}^{N-1}\,\prod_{a=1}^{\lambda^{(i)}}\;\biggl(\,\prod_{\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{1}{\scriptstyle b=1}{\scriptstyle b\ne a}}^{\lambda^{(i)}}\,\frac{\Gamma(z_{b}-z_{a}\mskip-2.00002mu+l^{\mskip 0.99998mu(i)}_{\mskip 0.99998mua}\!\mskip-0.99998mu-l^{\mskip 0.99998mu(i)}_{\mskip 0.99998mub}\mskip-2.00002mu+1)\,\Gamma(z_{b}-z_{a}\mskip-2.00002mu+l^{\mskip 0.99998mu(i)}_{\mskip 0.99998mua}\!\mskip-0.99998mu-l^{\mskip 0.99998mu(i+1)}_{\mskip 0.99998mub}\mskip-2.00002mu+h)\,}{\Gamma(z_{b}-z_{a}\mskip-2.00002mu+l^{\mskip 0.99998mu(i)}_{\mskip 0.99998mua}\!\mskip-0.99998mu-l^{\mskip 0.99998mu(i)}_{\mskip 0.99998mub}\mskip-2.00002mu+h+1)\,\Gamma(z_{b}-z_{a}\mskip-2.00002mu+l^{\mskip 0.99998mu(i)}_{\mskip 0.99998mua}\!\mskip-0.99998mu-l^{\mskip 0.99998mu(i+1)}_{\mskip 0.99998mub}\mskip-2.00002mu+1)}

\displaystyle{}\,\times{}\kern-8.00003pt

\displaystyle{}\times\,\frac{\Gamma(\mskip 0.99998mul^{\mskip 0.99998mu(i)}_{\mskip 0.99998mua}\!\mskip-0.99998mu-l^{\mskip 0.99998mu(i+1)}_{\mskip 0.99998mua}\mskip-2.00002mu+h)}{(\mskip 0.99998mul^{\mskip 0.99998mu(i)}_{\mskip 0.99998mua}\!\mskip-0.99998mu-l^{\mskip 0.99998mu(i+1)}_{\mskip 0.99998mua})\mskip 0.99998mu!}\;\prod_{c=\lambda^{(i)}+1}^{\lambda^{(i+1)}}\frac{\Gamma(z_{c}\mskip-0.99998mu-z_{a}\mskip-2.00002mu+l^{\mskip 0.99998mu(i)}_{\mskip 0.99998mua}\!-l^{\mskip 0.99998mu(i+1)}_{\mskip 0.99998muc}\mskip-2.00002mu+h)}{\Gamma(z_{c}\mskip-0.99998mu-z_{a}\mskip-2.00002mu+l^{\mskip 0.99998mu(i)}_{\mskip 0.99998mua}\!-l^{\mskip 0.99998mu(i+1)}_{\mskip 0.99998muc}\mskip-2.00002mu+1)}

\displaystyle\,\biggr)\mskip 2.00002mu.\kern-8.00003pt

Recall
\,{\boldsymbol{z}}_{\sigma}\mskip-0.99998mu=(z_{\sigma(1)},\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak z_{\sigma(n)})\,
and the permutations

\,\sigma_{I}\,
,
\,I\mskip-0.99998mu\in{{\mathcal{I}}_{{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}}}\,
, defined in Section
3.2
.
Let
\,{\boldsymbol{r}}=(r_{1},\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak r_{N-1})\,
. For
\,I,\mskip-0.99998muJ\mskip-0.99998mu\in{{\mathcal{I}}_{{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}}}\,
, define

(4.33)

{\mathcal{B}}_{I\mskip-0.99998mu,\mskip 0.99998muJ}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h;{\boldsymbol{r}})\,=\mskip 2.00002mu\sum_{{\boldsymbol{l}}\in{\mathcal{Z}}_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\!}\,B_{\mskip 0.99998mu{\boldsymbol{l}}}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h)\mskip 2.00002muW_{I}(\Sigma_{I^{\mskip 0.99998mu\min}_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}}\mskip-0.99998mu-{\boldsymbol{l}}\mskip 0.99998mu;{\boldsymbol{z}}_{\sigma_{J}^{-1}};h)\,\prod_{i=1}^{N-1}\mskip 0.99998mur_{i}^{\mskip 2.00002mu\sum_{a=1}^{\lambda^{(i)}}l_{a}^{(i)}}\!.\kern-10.00002pt\ignorespaces

Set

(4.34)

{\,\widetilde{\!M}}_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h)\,=\,\prod_{i=1}^{N-1}\,\prod_{a=1}^{\lambda^{(i)}}\,\biggl(\sin\mskip 0.99998mu(\pi\mskip 0.99998muh)\prod_{b=a+1}^{\lambda^{(i)}}\mskip 0.99998mu\frac{\sin\mskip 0.99998mu\bigl(\pi\mskip 2.00002mu(z_{a}\mskip-0.99998mu-z_{b})\bigr)}{z_{a}\mskip-0.99998mu-z_{b}}\prod_{c=\lambda^{(i)}+1}^{\lambda^{(i+1)}}\mskip 0.99998mu\sin\mskip 0.99998mu\bigl(\pi\mskip 2.00002mu(z_{a}\mskip-0.99998mu-z_{c}\mskip-0.99998mu-h)\bigr)\!\biggr)\mskip 2.00002mu,\kern-1.99997pt\ignorespaces

Proposition 4.13
.

For any
\,I,\mskip-0.99998muJ\mskip-0.99998mu\in{{\mathcal{I}}_{{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}}}\,
, the function

\,{\,\widetilde{\!M}}_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h)\mskip 2.00002mu{\mathcal{B}}_{I\mskip-0.99998mu,\mskip 0.99998muJ}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h;{\boldsymbol{r}})\mskip 2.00002mu
is entire in
\,{\boldsymbol{z}},h\,

and holomorphic in
\,{\boldsymbol{r}}\mskip 0.99998mu
provided
\,|\mskip 0.99998mur_{i}\mskip 0.99998mu|<1\mskip 2.00002mu
for all

\,i=1,\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak N-1\mskip 2.00002mu
.

Proof.

By Stirling’s formula, ratios of Gamma functions appearing in
formula (
4.32
) have the following asymptotics as
\,k\to+\mskip 0.99998mu\infty\,

over integers,

(4.35)

\frac{\Gamma(\alpha+k)}{\Gamma(\beta+k)}\,=\,k^{\mskip 2.00002mu\alpha-\beta}\mskip 2.00002mu\bigl(1+o(1)\bigr)\,,\qquad\frac{\Gamma(\alpha-k)}{\Gamma(\beta-k)}\,=\,k^{\mskip 2.00002mu\alpha-\beta}\,\frac{\sin\mskip 0.99998mu(\pi\mskip 0.99998mu\alpha)}{\sin\mskip 0.99998mu(\pi\mskip 0.99998mu\beta)}\mskip 2.00002mu\bigl(1+o(1)\bigr)\,,\kern-20.00003pt\ignorespaces

provided
\,\alpha\,
and
\,\beta\,
are not integers in the second case, and these
asymptotics can be differentiated with respect to
\,\alpha\,
and
\,\beta\,
.

The right-hand side of formula (
4.33
) allows one to present

\,{\,\widetilde{\!M}}_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h)\mskip 2.00002mu{\mathcal{B}}_{I\mskip-0.99998mu,\mskip 0.99998muJ}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h;{\boldsymbol{r}})\,
as a power series in
\,{\boldsymbol{r}}\,
:

{\,\widetilde{\!M}}_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h)\mskip 2.00002mu{\mathcal{B}}_{I}({\boldsymbol{z}}_{\sigma_{J}}\mskip 0.99998mu;{\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h;{\boldsymbol{r}})\,=\mskip-2.00002mu\sum_{{\boldsymbol{k}}\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{N-1}_{\geqslant 0}}\!\widetilde{B}_{I\mskip-0.99998mu,\mskip 0.99998muJ,\mskip 0.99998mu{\boldsymbol{k}}}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h)\,\prod_{i=1}^{N-1}\,r_{i}^{\mskip 2.00002muk_{i}}\mskip 0.99998mu.\ignorespaces

Formulae (
4.32
), (
4.35
) show that for given
\,{\boldsymbol{z}},h\,
,
this series and its formal derivatives with respect to
\,{\boldsymbol{z}},h\,
converge
provided
\,|\mskip 0.99998mur_{i}\mskip 0.99998mu|<1\mskip 2.00002mu
for all
\,i=1,\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak N-1\,
. Therefore, the function

\,{\,\widetilde{\!M}}_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h)\mskip 2.00002mu{\mathcal{B}}_{I\mskip-0.99998mu,\mskip 0.99998muJ}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h;{\boldsymbol{r}})\,
is holomorphic
in
\,{\boldsymbol{r}}\mskip 2.00002mu
provided
\,|\mskip 0.99998mur_{i}\mskip 0.99998mu|<1\mskip 2.00002mu
for all
\,i=1,\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak N-1\,
,
and is holomorphic in
\,{\boldsymbol{z}},h\,
whenever all the coefficients

\,\widetilde{B}_{I\mskip-0.99998mu,\mskip 0.99998muJ,\mskip 0.99998mu{\boldsymbol{k}}}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h)\,
are holomorphic in
\,{\boldsymbol{z}},h\,
.

It remains to show that all the coefficients
\,\widetilde{B}_{I\mskip-0.99998mu,\mskip 0.99998muJ,\mskip 0.99998mu{\boldsymbol{k}}}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h)\,

are entire in
\,{\boldsymbol{z}},h\,
. To this end, it suffices to show that
for any
\,{\boldsymbol{l}}\mskip-0.99998mu\in\mskip-0.99998mu{\mathcal{Z}}_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\,
, the product
\,{\,\widetilde{\!M}}_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h)\mskip 2.00002muB_{\mskip 0.99998mu{\boldsymbol{l}}}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h)\mskip 2.00002muW_{I}(\Sigma_{I^{\mskip 0.99998mu\min}_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}}\mskip-0.99998mu-{\boldsymbol{l}}\mskip 0.99998mu;{\boldsymbol{z}}_{\sigma_{J}^{-1}};h)\,

is an entire function of
\,{\boldsymbol{z}},h\,
.

Take
\,{\boldsymbol{l}}\mskip-0.99998mu\in\mskip-0.99998mu{\mathcal{Z}}_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\,
and
\,a\neq b\,
.
Since
\,l^{\mskip 0.99998mu(i)}_{\mskip 0.99998mua}\mskip-2.00002mu\geqslant l^{\mskip 0.99998mu(i+1)}_{\mskip 0.99998mua}
and

\,l^{\mskip 0.99998mu(i)}_{\mskip 0.99998mub}\mskip-2.00002mu\geqslant l^{\mskip 0.99998mu(i+1)}_{\mskip 0.99998mub}
, the ratio

\frac{\sin\mskip 0.99998mu\bigl(\pi\mskip 2.00002mu(z_{a}\mskip-2.00002mu-z_{b})\bigr)\,\Gamma(z_{a}\mskip-0.99998mu-z_{b}+l^{\mskip 0.99998mu(i)}_{\mskip 0.99998mub}\!\mskip-0.99998mu-l^{\mskip 0.99998mu(i)}_{\mskip 0.99998mua}\mskip-2.00002mu+1)\mskip 2.00002mu\Gamma(z_{b}-z_{a}\mskip-0.99998mu+l^{\mskip 0.99998mu(i)}_{\mskip 0.99998mua}\!\mskip-0.99998mu-l^{\mskip 0.99998mu(i)}_{\mskip 0.99998mub}\mskip-2.00002mu+1)}{(z_{a}\mskip-0.99998mu-z_{b})\,\Gamma(z_{a}-z_{b}\mskip-2.00002mu+l^{\mskip 0.99998mu(i)}_{\mskip 0.99998mub}\!\mskip-0.99998mu-l^{\mskip 0.99998mu(i+1)}_{\mskip 0.99998mua}\mskip-2.00002mu+1)\mskip 2.00002mu\Gamma(z_{b}-z_{a}\mskip-0.99998mu+l^{\mskip 0.99998mu(i)}_{\mskip 0.99998mua}\!\mskip-0.99998mu-l^{\mskip 0.99998mu(i+1)}_{\mskip 0.99998mub}\mskip-2.00002mu+1)}\kern-20.00003pt\ignorespaces

is an entire function of
\,z_{a}\mskip 0.99998mu,z_{b}\,
,
and if
\,\mskip 0.99998mul^{\mskip 0.99998mu(i)}_{\mskip 0.99998mub}\mskip-2.00002mu>l^{\mskip 0.99998mu(i+1)}_{\mskip 0.99998mub}
, then the ratio

\frac{\Gamma(z_{b}-z_{a}\mskip-2.00002mu+l^{\mskip 0.99998mu(i)}_{\mskip 0.99998mua}\!\mskip-0.99998mu-l^{\mskip 0.99998mu(i+1)}_{\mskip 0.99998mub}\mskip-2.00002mu+h)}{\Gamma(z_{b}-z_{a}\mskip-2.00002mu+l^{\mskip 0.99998mu(i)}_{\mskip 0.99998mua}\!\mskip-0.99998mu-l^{\mskip 0.99998mu(i)}_{\mskip 0.99998mub}\mskip-2.00002mu+h+1)}\kern-20.00003pt\ignorespaces

is a polynomial in
\,z_{a}\mskip 0.99998mu,z_{b}\mskip 0.99998mu,h\,
. Set

(4.36)

F_{\boldsymbol{l}}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h)\,=\,\prod_{i=1}^{N-1}\,\prod_{a=1}^{\lambda^{(i)}}\,\mskip 0.99998mu\prod_{\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{1}{\scriptstyle b=1}{\scriptstyle b\ne a\rlap{$\ssize{},\,l^{\:(i)}_{\:b}\?=\>l^{\:(i+1)}_{\:b}$}}}^{\lambda^{(i)}}\,(z_{b}-z_{a}\mskip-0.99998mu+l^{\mskip 0.99998mu(i)}_{\mskip 0.99998mua}\!\mskip-0.99998mu-l^{\mskip 0.99998mu(i)}_{\mskip 0.99998mub}\mskip-2.00002mu+h)\,.\kern-20.00003pt\ignorespaces

Then by formulae (
4.32
), (
4.34
), the product

\,{\,\widetilde{\!M}}_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h)\allowbreak\mskip 2.00002muB_{\mskip 0.99998mu{\boldsymbol{l}}}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h)\mskip 2.00002muF_{\boldsymbol{l}}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h)\,
is
an entire function of
\,{\boldsymbol{z}},h\,
. Since by Lemma
4.14
below, the ratio

\,W_{I}({\Sigma_{I^{\mskip 0.99998mu\min}_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}}\mskip-0.99998mu-{\boldsymbol{l}}\mskip 0.99998mu;{}}\allowbreak{\boldsymbol{z}}_{\sigma_{J}^{-1}};h)/F_{\boldsymbol{l}}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h)\,

is a polynomial in
\,{\boldsymbol{z}},h\,
, the product
\,{\,\widetilde{\!M}}_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h)\mskip 2.00002muB_{\mskip 0.99998mu{\boldsymbol{l}}}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h)\mskip 2.00002muW_{I}(\Sigma_{I^{\mskip 0.99998mu\min}_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}}\mskip-0.99998mu-{\boldsymbol{l}}\mskip 0.99998mu;{\boldsymbol{z}}_{\sigma_{J}^{-1}};h)\,

is an entire function of
\,{\boldsymbol{z}},h\,
too. Proposition
4.13
is proved.
∎

Lemma 4.14
.

Let
\,F_{\boldsymbol{l}}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h)\mskip 2.00002mu
be given by (
4.36
). Then the ratio

\,W_{I}({\Sigma_{I^{\mskip 0.99998mu\min}_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}}\mskip-0.99998mu-{\boldsymbol{l}}\mskip 0.99998mu;{}}\allowbreak{\boldsymbol{z}}_{\sigma_{J}^{-1}};h)/F_{\boldsymbol{l}}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h)\,

is a polynomial in
\,{\boldsymbol{z}},h\,
.

Proof.

The proof is by inspection, similarly to the proof of Lemma
4.4
.
∎

Proof of Theorem
4.9
.

Recall that we assume
\,\kappa=1\,
and skip the corresponding argument
in all functions. By formulae (
4.19
), (
4.30
) – (
4.33
),
and Lemmas
4.7
,
4.12
,

(4.37)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{M}}_{J}(\Phi_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\mskip 0.99998muW_{I}){}

\displaystyle{}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h;{\boldsymbol{q}})\,=\,{\mathcal{B}}_{I\mskip-0.99998mu,\mskip 0.99998muJ}({\boldsymbol{z}}_{\sigma_{J}};h;q_{2}/q_{1},\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak q_{N}/q_{N-1})\mskip 2.00002mu\times{}

\displaystyle{}\,\times\,\prod_{i=1}^{N}\mskip 2.00002mu\bigl(\mskip 2.00002mue^{\mskip 0.99998mu\pi\sqrt{\mskip-0.99998mu-1}\,(n-\lambda_{i})}\mskip 2.00002muq_{i}\mskip 0.99998mu\bigr)^{\mskip 2.00002mu\sum_{a\in J_{i}}\mskip-2.00002muz_{a}}\mskip 0.99998mu\prod_{i=1}^{N-1}\prod_{j=i+1}^{N}\,\prod_{a\in J_{i}}\,\prod_{b\in J_{j}}\mskip 2.00002mu\frac{\pi}{\sin\bigl(\pi\mskip 2.00002mu(z_{a}\mskip-0.99998mu-z_{b})\bigr)}\;.

Then by formulae (
4.20
), (
4.22
), (
4.37
), we have

(4.38)

\displaystyle\Psi^{\raise 1.20552pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\diamond$}}_{\mskip-2.00002muJ}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h;{\boldsymbol{q}})\,

\displaystyle{}=\mskip 2.00002mu\sum_{I\in{{\mathcal{I}}_{{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}}}}\mskip 0.99998mu{\mathcal{B}}_{I\mskip-0.99998mu,\mskip 0.99998muJ}({\boldsymbol{z}}_{\sigma_{J}};h;q_{2}/q_{1},\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak q_{N}/q_{N-1})\,v_{I}\,\times{}

\displaystyle{}\mskip 2.00002mu\times\,\frac{\Omega_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}({\boldsymbol{q}})}{\bigl(\Gamma(h)\bigr)^{\lambda^{\{1\}}}}\,\prod_{i=1}^{N-1}\prod_{j=i+1}^{N}\,\mskip 0.99998mu\prod_{a\in J_{i}}\mskip 2.00002mu\biggl(\,\prod_{b\in J_{j}}\mskip 2.00002mu\frac{\pi}{\Gamma(z_{b}\mskip-0.99998mu-z_{a}\mskip-0.99998mu+h+1)}\;\prod_{\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{1}{\scriptstyle c\in J_j}{\scriptstyle c<a}}(z_{a}\mskip-0.99998mu-z_{c}\mskip-0.99998mu-h)\mskip-2.00002mu\biggr).\kern-6.00006pt

By Proposition
4.13
, the function in the right-hand side of formula
(
4.38
) is holomorphic in
\,{\boldsymbol{q}}\,
in the unit polydisk around
\,{\mathchoice{\raise 0.68889pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\mskip 2.00002mu\wr$\hss}0}{\raise 0.68889pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\mskip 2.00002mu\wr$\hss}0}{\raise 0.55199pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\mskip 2.00002mu\wr$\hss}0}{\raise 0.38748pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\mskip 2.00002mu\wr$\hss}0}}\,
,
and may have poles in
\,{\boldsymbol{z}},h\,
at most at the hyperplanes

\,z_{a}\mskip-0.99998mu-z_{b}\mskip-0.99998mu\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mskip 0.99998mu\neq\mskip 0.99998mu0}\,
for
\,a\neq b\,
,

\,z_{a}\mskip-0.99998mu-z_{b}\mskip-0.99998mu-h\mskip-0.99998mu\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geqslant 0}\,
for
\,a\in J_{i}\,
,
\,b\in J_{j}\,
,

\,i<j\,
, and
\,h\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{>0}\,
. To complete the proof of
Theorem
4.9
, it remains to show that those poles
do not actually occur.

For the hyperplanes
\,z_{a}\mskip-0.99998mu-z_{b}\mskip-0.99998mu\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mskip 0.99998mu\neq\mskip 0.99998mu0}\,
,
\,a\neq b\,
,
we will show the regularity of the function

\,M_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}({\boldsymbol{z}}_{\sigma_{J}}\mskip-0.99998mu)\mskip 2.00002mu\Psi_{\mskip-2.00002muJ}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h;{\boldsymbol{q}})\,
.
Observe that
\,M_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}({\boldsymbol{z}}_{\sigma_{J}}\mskip-0.99998mu)\mskip 2.00002mu\Psi_{\mskip-2.00002muJ}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h;{\boldsymbol{q}})\,
is regular
at the hyperplanes
\,z_{a}\mskip-0.99998mu=z_{b}\,
for all
\,a\neq b\,
, and solves
qKZ

difference equations (
2.5
). Since all the
qKZ
operators

\,K_{1}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h;{\boldsymbol{q}}),\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak K_{n}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h;{\boldsymbol{q}})\,
and their inverses are regular
at the hyperplanes
\,z_{a}\mskip-0.99998mu-z_{b}\mskip-0.99998mu\in{\mathbb{Z}}\,
for
\,a\neq b\,
, the function

\,M_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}({\boldsymbol{z}}_{\sigma_{J}}\mskip-0.99998mu)\mskip 2.00002mu\Psi_{\mskip-2.00002muJ}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h;{\boldsymbol{q}})\,
is regular at all hyperplanes

\,z_{a}\mskip-0.99998mu-z_{b}\mskip-0.99998mu\in{\mathbb{Z}}\,
for
\,a\neq b\,
.

To deal with the hyperplanes
\,z_{a}\mskip-0.99998mu-z_{b}\mskip-0.99998mu-h\mskip-0.99998mu\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geqslant 0}\,
and

\,h\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{>0}\,
, we will show that for given
\,{\boldsymbol{z}}\,
, the function

\,\Psi^{\raise 1.20552pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\diamond$}}_{\mskip-2.00002muJ}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h;{\boldsymbol{q}})\,
is entire in
\,h\,
, and it suffices to do it
for generic
\,{\boldsymbol{z}}\,
.

To simplify writing, we will omit the argument
\,{\boldsymbol{z}}\,

in all functions for a while. Let
\,{\boldsymbol{r}}=(r_{1},\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak r_{N-1})
and

\,{\boldsymbol{r}}_{\!*}=\mskip 0.99998mu(1,r_{1}\mskip 0.99998mu,\mskip 0.99998mur_{1}r_{2}\mskip 2.00002mu,\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak\mskip 0.99998mur_{1}\ldots r_{N-1})\,
.
Denote
\,F(h;{\boldsymbol{r}})=\Psi^{\raise 1.20552pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\diamond$}}_{\mskip-2.00002muJ}(h;{\boldsymbol{r}}_{\!*})\,
, so that

\,\Psi^{\raise 1.20552pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\diamond$}}_{\mskip-2.00002muJ}(h;{\boldsymbol{q}})=F(h;q_{2}/q_{1},\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak q_{N}/q_{N-1})\,
.

Recall the dynamical operators
\,X_{1},\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak X_{N}\,
, see (
2.8
). Set

X_{(i)}(h;{\boldsymbol{r}})\,=\,\sum_{j=i+1}^{N}\Bigl(X_{j}(h;{\boldsymbol{r}}_{\!*})\mskip 0.99998mu-\sum_{a\in J_{j}\!}\,z_{a}\mskip 0.99998mu\Bigr)\Big|_{\mskip 0.99998mu({\mathbb{C}}^{N})^{\otimes\mskip 0.99998mun}_{\mskip 0.99998mu{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}}}\,,\qquad i=1,\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak N-1\,.\kern-20.00003pt\ignorespaces

The dynamical differential equations (
2.7
) for
\,\Psi_{\mskip-2.00002muJ}(h;{\boldsymbol{q}})\,

are equivalent to the following equations for
F(h;{\boldsymbol{r}})
,

(4.39)

r_{i}\mskip 0.99998mu\frac{\partial}{\partial\mskip 0.99998mur_{i}}\mskip 2.00002muF(h;{\boldsymbol{r}})\,=\,X_{(i)}(h;{\boldsymbol{r}})\mskip 2.00002muF(h;{\boldsymbol{r}})\,,\qquad i=1,\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak N-1\,.\kern-20.00003pt

The operators
\,X_{(1)}(h;{\boldsymbol{r}}),\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak X_{(N-1)}(h;{\boldsymbol{r}})\,
are linear functions in

\,h\,
and rational functions in
\,{\boldsymbol{r}}\,
, regular provided
|\mskip 0.99998mur_{i}\mskip 0.99998mu|<1\,

for all
\,i=1,\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak N-1\,
.
The eigenvalues of the operator
\,X_{(i)}(h;0\mskip 0.99998mu)\,
for a given
\,i\,
are

\,\sum_{j=i+1}^{N}\mskip 2.00002mu\bigl(\mskip 2.00002mu\sum_{a\in I_{j}\!}z_{a}-\sum_{a\in J_{j}\!}z_{a}\mskip 0.99998mu\bigr)\,
,
\;I\mskip-0.99998mu\in{{\mathcal{I}}_{{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}}}\,
. Hence, one of the eigenvalues
of
\,X_{(i)}(h;0\mskip 0.99998mu)\,
equals zero and all other eigenvalues are not integers
for generic
\,{\boldsymbol{z}}\,
. Therefore, a solution
\,F(h;{\boldsymbol{r}})\,
of equations
(
4.39
) holomorphic
|\mskip 0.99998mur_{i}\mskip 0.99998mu|<1\,
for all
\,i\,
, is uniquely determined
by the value
\,F(h;0)\,
, and
\,F(h,{\boldsymbol{r}})\,
is holomorphic in
\,h\,

whenever
\,F(h;0)\,
is. The value
\,F(h;0)=\Psi^{\raise 1.20552pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\diamond$}}(h;{\mathchoice{\raise 0.68889pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\mskip 2.00002mu\wr$\hss}0}{\raise 0.68889pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\mskip 2.00002mu\wr$\hss}0}{\raise 0.55199pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\mskip 2.00002mu\wr$\hss}0}{\raise 0.38748pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\mskip 2.00002mu\wr$\hss}0}})\,
can be found from
formulae (
4.20
), (
4.18
), (
4.32
), (
4.33
),
(
4.37
), (
4.38
). It is a polynomial in
\,h\,

by Lemmas
4.3
,
4.4
. Therefore,
\,F(h,{\boldsymbol{r}})\,
is
an entire function of
\,h\,
, and so is
\,\Psi^{\raise 1.20552pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\diamond$}}(h;{\boldsymbol{q}})\,
.

Formulae (
4.23
), (
4.24
), (
4.25
), and the rationality
of
\,\Psi^{\raise 1.20552pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\diamond$}}_{\mskip-0.99998muJ,\mskip 0.99998mu{\boldsymbol{m}}}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h)\,
follow from formulae (
4.20
),
(
4.30
) – (
4.33
), (
4.37
), (
4.38
),
and Lemma
4.3
. The properties of poles of

\,\Psi^{\raise 1.20552pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\diamond$}}_{\mskip-0.99998muJ,\mskip 0.99998mu{\boldsymbol{m}}}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h)\,
are determined by the analytic properties of

\Psi^{\raise 1.20552pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\diamond$}}_{\mskip-2.00002muJ}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h;{\boldsymbol{q}})\,
. The fact that
\,\Psi^{\raise 1.20552pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\diamond$}}_{\mskip-0.99998muJ,\mskip 0.99998mu0}\mskip 0.99998mu({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h)\,

is a polynomial in
\,{\boldsymbol{z}},h\,
follows from Lemma
4.4
.
∎

4.6.
Solutions of the dynamical and
qKZ
equations parametrized
by Laurent polynomials

In the sequel, we will use the following notation. For any variable
\,x\,
,
there is the companion
\,\acute{x}\vphantom{x}\,
, and for any function
\,f(\acute{x}\vphantom{x})\,
,
we set
\,\acute{f}\vphantom{f}(x;\kappa)=f(e^{\mskip 0.99998mu2\mskip 0.99998mu\pi\sqrt{\mskip-0.99998mu-1}\,x/\mskip-0.99998mu\kappa})\,
.
The convention for collections of variables is similar. For instance,

\,\mskip 2.00002mu\acute{\mskip-2.00002mu{\boldsymbol{z}}}\vphantom{{\boldsymbol{z}}}=(\acute{z}\vphantom{z}_{1},\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak\acute{z}\vphantom{z}_{n})\,
and
\,\acute{f}\vphantom{f}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;\kappa)=f(e^{\mskip 0.99998mu2\mskip 0.99998mu\pi\sqrt{\mskip-0.99998mu-1}\,z_{1}\mskip-0.99998mu/\mskip-0.99998mu\kappa},\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak e^{\mskip 0.99998mu2\mskip 0.99998mu\pi\sqrt{\mskip-0.99998mu-1}\,z_{n}\mskip-0.99998mu/\mskip-0.99998mu\kappa})\,
.

Introduce the variables
\gamma_{i,\mskip 0.99998muj}\,
,
\,i=1,\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak N
,
\;j=1,\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak\lambda_{i}\,
. Denote

(4.40)

{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}\mskip 2.00002mu=\,(\gamma_{1,1},\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak\gamma_{1,\mskip 2.00002mu\lambda_{1}}\mskip 0.99998mu,\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak\gamma_{N,1},\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak\gamma_{N\mskip-2.00002mu,\mskip 2.00002mu\lambda_{N}})\,,\qquad\acute{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}\vphantom{{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}}^{\pm 1}=\,(\,\acute{\!\gamma}\vphantom{\gamma}_{1,1}^{\pm 1},\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak\,\acute{\!\gamma}\vphantom{\gamma}_{N\mskip-2.00002mu,\mskip 2.00002mu\lambda_{N}}^{\pm 1})\,.\kern-20.00003pt\ignorespaces

Recall
\,{\boldsymbol{z}}_{\sigma}\mskip-0.99998mu=(z_{\sigma(1)},\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak z_{\sigma(n)})\,
, and the permutations

\,\sigma_{I}\,
,
\,I\mskip-0.99998mu\in{{\mathcal{I}}_{{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}}}\,
, defined in Section
3.2
.
For a Laurent polynomial
\,P(\mskip 0.99998mu\acute{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}\vphantom{{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}}\mskip 0.99998mu;\mskip 2.00002mu\acute{\mskip-2.00002mu{\boldsymbol{z}}}\vphantom{{\boldsymbol{z}}};\acute{h}\vphantom{h})\,
, set

(4.41)

\Psi_{P}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h;{\boldsymbol{q}}\mskip 0.99998mu;\kappa)\,=\,\sum_{J\in{{\mathcal{I}}_{{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}}}}\,\acute{P}\vphantom{P}({\boldsymbol{z}}_{\sigma_{J}};{\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h;\kappa)\,\Psi_{\mskip-2.00002muJ}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h;{\boldsymbol{q}}\mskip 0.99998mu;\kappa)\,,\kern-20.00003pt\ignorespaces

where the functions
\,\Psi_{\mskip-2.00002muJ}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h;{\boldsymbol{q}}\mskip 0.99998mu;\kappa)\,
,
are given by (
4.20
).

Proposition 4.15
.

The function
\,\Psi_{P}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h;{\boldsymbol{q}}\mskip 0.99998mu;\kappa)\mskip 2.00002mu
is a solution of the joint system
of dynamical differential equations (
2.7
) and
qKZ
difference equations
(
2.5
).

Proof.

By Theorem
4.9
, the function
\,\Psi_{P}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h;{\boldsymbol{q}}\mskip 0.99998mu;\kappa)\,

solves the system of equations (
2.7
) and (
2.5
) since

\,\acute{P}\vphantom{P}({\boldsymbol{z}}_{\sigma_{J}};{\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h;\kappa)\,
does not depend on
\,{\boldsymbol{q}}\,
and is

\,\kappa\mskip 0.99998mu
-periodic function in each of the variables
\,z_{1},\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak z_{n}\,
.
∎

Let
\,{\mathbb{C}}[\mskip 0.99998mu\acute{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}\vphantom{{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}}^{\pm 1}]^{\mskip 0.99998muS_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}}
be the space of Laurent polynomials in

\,\acute{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}\vphantom{{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}}
symmetric in
\,\,\acute{\!\gamma}\vphantom{\gamma}_{i,1},\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak\,\acute{\!\gamma}\vphantom{\gamma}_{i,\mskip 2.00002mu\lambda_{i}}
for each
\,i=1,\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak N\mskip 2.00002mu
.

Proposition 4.16
.

For any

\mskip 2.00002muP\mskip-2.00002mu\in{\mathbb{C}}[\mskip 0.99998mu\acute{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}\vphantom{{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}}^{\pm 1}]^{\mskip 0.99998muS_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}}\mskip-2.00002mu\otimes{\mathbb{C}}[\mskip 0.99998mu\mskip 2.00002mu\acute{\mskip-2.00002mu{\boldsymbol{z}}}\vphantom{{\boldsymbol{z}}}^{\pm 1}\mskip-2.00002mu,\acute{h}\vphantom{h}^{\pm 1}]\,
,
the function
\,\Psi_{P}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h;{\boldsymbol{q}}\mskip 0.99998mu;\kappa)\mskip 2.00002mu
is holomorphic in
\,{\boldsymbol{z}},h,{\boldsymbol{q}}\,

provided
\,z_{a}\mskip-0.99998mu-z_{b}\mskip-0.99998mu+h\not\in\mskip-0.99998mu\kappa\mskip 2.00002mu{\mathbb{Z}}_{\leqslant 0}\,
for all
\,a,b=1,\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak n\,
,

\,a\neq b\,
, and
\,\mskip 0.99998mu|\mskip 0.99998muq_{i+1}/q_{i}|<1\,
for all
\,i=1,\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak N-1\,
,
with a branch of
\,\mskip 0.99998mu\log\mskip 0.99998muq_{i}\mskip 0.99998mu
fixed for each
i=1,\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak N
.
The singularities of
\,\Psi_{P}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h;{\boldsymbol{q}}\mskip 0.99998mu;\kappa)\mskip 2.00002mu
at the hyperplanes

\;z_{a}\mskip-0.99998mu-z_{b}\mskip-0.99998mu+h\in\mskip-0.99998mu\kappa\mskip 2.00002mu{\mathbb{Z}}_{\leqslant 0}\,
are simple poles.

Proof.

By Theorem
4.9
, we need only to show that

\,\Psi_{P}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h;{\boldsymbol{q}}\mskip 0.99998mu;\kappa)\,
is regular at the hyperplanes

\,z_{a}\mskip-0.99998mu-z_{b}\mskip-0.99998mu\in\mskip-0.99998mu\kappa\mskip 2.00002mu{\mathbb{Z}}\,
,
\,a\neq b\,
, where it might have simple poles.

The regularity of
\,\Psi_{P}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h;{\boldsymbol{q}}\mskip 0.99998mu;\kappa)\,
at the hyperplanes

\,z_{a}\mskip-0.99998mu=z_{b}\,
for all
a\neq b
follows from formula (
4.25
).
Since
\,\Psi_{P}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h;{\boldsymbol{q}}\mskip 0.99998mu;\kappa)\,
solves
qKZ
difference
equations (
2.5
) and all the
qKZ
operators

\,K_{1}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h;{\boldsymbol{q}}\mskip 0.99998mu;\kappa),\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak K_{n}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h;{\boldsymbol{q}}\mskip 0.99998mu;\kappa)\,
and their inverses
are regular at the hyperplanes
\,z_{a}\mskip-0.99998mu-z_{b}\mskip-0.99998mu\in\mskip-0.99998mu\kappa\mskip 2.00002mu{\mathbb{Z}}\,
, the function

\,\Psi_{P}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h;{\boldsymbol{q}}\mskip 0.99998mu;\kappa)\,
is regular at all hyperplanes

\,z_{a}\mskip-0.99998mu-z_{b}\mskip-0.99998mu\in\mskip-0.99998mu\kappa\mskip 2.00002mu{\mathbb{Z}}\,
,
\,a\neq b\,
.
∎

Denote by
\mskip 2.00002mu\mathscr{S}_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\mskip 0.99998mu
the space of solutions of the system of dynamical
differential equations (
2.7
) and
qKZ
difference equations (
2.5
)
spanned over
\,{\mathbb{C}}\,
by the functions
\,\Psi_{P}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h;{\boldsymbol{q}}\mskip 0.99998mu;\kappa)\,
,

\mskip 2.00002muP\mskip-2.00002mu\in{\mathbb{C}}[\mskip 0.99998mu\acute{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}\vphantom{{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}}^{\pm 1}]^{\mskip 0.99998muS_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}}\mskip-2.00002mu\otimes{\mathbb{C}}[\mskip 0.99998mu\mskip 2.00002mu\acute{\mskip-2.00002mu{\boldsymbol{z}}}\vphantom{{\boldsymbol{z}}}^{\pm 1}\mskip-2.00002mu,\acute{h}\vphantom{h}^{\pm 1}]\,
.
The space
\mskip 2.00002mu\mathscr{S}_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\mskip 0.99998mu
is a
\,{\mathbb{C}}[\mskip 0.99998mu\mskip 2.00002mu\acute{\mskip-2.00002mu{\boldsymbol{z}}}\vphantom{{\boldsymbol{z}}}^{\pm 1}\mskip-2.00002mu,\acute{h}\vphantom{h}^{\pm 1}]\mskip 0.99998mu
-module
with
f(\mskip 2.00002mu\acute{\mskip-2.00002mu{\boldsymbol{z}}}\vphantom{{\boldsymbol{z}}}\mskip 0.99998mu;\acute{h}\vphantom{h})\,
acting as multiplication by
\acute{f}\vphantom{f}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h;\kappa)\,
.

Define the algebra

(4.42)

{\mathcal{K}}_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\mskip 0.99998mu=\,{\mathbb{C}}[\mskip 0.99998mu\acute{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}\vphantom{{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}}^{\pm 1}]^{\mskip 0.99998muS_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}}\mskip-2.00002mu\otimes{\mathbb{C}}[\mskip 0.99998mu\mskip 2.00002mu\acute{\mskip-2.00002mu{\boldsymbol{z}}}\vphantom{{\boldsymbol{z}}}^{\pm 1}\mskip-2.00002mu,\acute{h}\vphantom{h}^{\pm 1}]\mskip 2.00002mu\Big/\Bigl\langle\,\prod_{i=1}^{N}\prod_{j=1}^{\lambda_{i}}\,(u-\,\acute{\!\gamma}\vphantom{\gamma}_{i,\mskip 0.99998muj})\,=\,\prod_{a=1}^{n}\,(u-\acute{z}\vphantom{z}_{a})\Bigr\rangle\,,\kern-16.00008pt\ignorespaces

where
\,u\,
is a formal variable. By (
4.41
),
the assignment
\,P\mapsto\Psi_{P}\mskip 2.00002mu
defines a homomorphism

(4.43)

\mu^{\scriptscriptstyle\mskip 0.99998mu{\mathcal{K}}}_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}:\mskip 0.99998mu{\mathcal{K}}_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\to\mskip 0.99998mu\mathscr{S}_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\,,\qquad Y\mskip-0.99998mu\mapsto\Psi_{Y}\mskip 2.00002mu,

of
\,{\mathbb{C}}[\mskip 0.99998mu\mskip 2.00002mu\acute{\mskip-2.00002mu{\boldsymbol{z}}}\vphantom{{\boldsymbol{z}}}^{\pm 1}\mskip-2.00002mu,\acute{h}\vphantom{h}^{\pm 1}]\mskip 0.99998mu
-modules.

By Propositions
A.2
,
A.3
, the algebra
\,{\mathcal{K}}_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\,
is
a free
\,{\mathbb{C}}[\mskip 0.99998mu\mskip 2.00002mu\acute{\mskip-2.00002mu{\boldsymbol{z}}}\vphantom{{\boldsymbol{z}}}^{\pm 1}\mskip-2.00002mu,\acute{h}\vphantom{h}^{\pm 1}]\mskip 0.99998mu
-module generated
by the classes

(4.44)

Y_{I}(\mskip 0.99998mu{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}})\,=\mskip 2.00002muV_{I}(\,\acute{\!\gamma}\vphantom{\gamma}_{1,1}^{-1},\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak\,\acute{\!\gamma}\vphantom{\gamma}_{1,\mskip 2.00002mu\lambda_{1}}^{-1}\mskip 0.99998mu,\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak\,\acute{\!\gamma}\vphantom{\gamma}_{N\mskip-2.00002mu,1}^{-1},\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak\,\acute{\!\gamma}\vphantom{\gamma}_{N\mskip-2.00002mu,\mskip 2.00002mu\lambda_{N}}^{-1})\,,\qquad I\mskip-0.99998mu\in{{\mathcal{I}}_{{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}}}\,,\kern-20.00003pt\ignorespaces

where the polynomials
\,V_{\mskip-0.99998muI}\mskip 2.00002mu
are defined by formula (
A.6
).
Introduce the coordinates of solutions
\,\Psi_{Y_{I}}\mskip 2.00002mu
:

(4.45)

\Psi_{Y_{I}}\mskip-0.99998mu({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h;{\boldsymbol{q}}\mskip 0.99998mu;\kappa)\,=\mskip 2.00002mu\sum_{J\in{{\mathcal{I}}_{{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}}}}\,\bar{\Psi}_{I\mskip-0.99998mu,\mskip 0.99998muJ}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h;{\boldsymbol{q}}\mskip 0.99998mu;\kappa)\,v_{J}\,.\ignorespaces

Theorem 4.17
.

Let
n\geqslant 2
. Then

(4.46)

\displaystyle\,\det\mskip 0.99998mu\bigl(\mskip 2.00002mu\bar{\Psi}_{I\mskip-0.99998mu,\mskip 0.99998muJ}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h;{\boldsymbol{q}}\mskip 0.99998mu;\kappa)\bigr)_{I\mskip-0.99998mu,\mskip 0.99998muJ\in\mskip 2.00002mu{{\mathcal{I}}_{{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}}}}\mskip-0.99998mu=\,\Bigl(\mskip 0.99998mue^{\mskip 0.99998mu\pi\sqrt{\mskip-0.99998mu-1}\,(n-1)\mskip 2.00002mud^{(2)}_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}}\mskip 2.00002mu\prod_{i=1}^{N}\,q_{i}^{\mskip 0.99998mu\smash{d^{(1)}_{{\boldsymbol{\lambda}},i}}\vphantom{|}}\mskip 2.00002mu\Bigr)^{\sum_{a=1}^{n}z_{a}\mskip-0.99998mu/\mskip-0.99998mu\kappa}\,\,\prod_{j=2}^{n-1}\,j^{\mskip 0.99998mu(n-j)\mskip 2.00002mud^{(2)}_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}}\times{}\!\mskip-2.00002mu

\displaystyle{}\times\,\prod_{a=1}^{n-1}\,\prod_{b=a+1}^{n}\bigl(\mskip 0.99998mu2\mskip 0.99998mu\pi\mskip 0.99998mu\sqrt{\mskip-0.99998mu-1}\;\kappa^{2}\,\Gamma\bigl(\mskip-0.99998mu(z_{a}\mskip-0.99998mu-z_{b}\mskip-0.99998mu+h)/\kappa\bigr)\mskip 2.00002mu\Gamma\bigl(1+(z_{b}\mskip-0.99998mu-z_{a}\mskip-0.99998mu+h)/\kappa\bigr)\bigr)^{\mskip 2.00002mud^{(2)}_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}}\!\mskip-0.99998mu,

where
\;d^{(1)}_{{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\mskip 0.99998mu,\mskip 0.99998mui}\,,\,d^{(2)}_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}
are given by
formulae  (
4.27
).

Proof.

The statement follows from Theorem
4.10
and formula (
A.7
).
∎

Corollary 4.18
.

The map
\,\mu^{\scriptscriptstyle\mskip 0.99998mu{\mathcal{K}}}_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}:\mskip 0.99998mu{\mathcal{K}}_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\mskip-0.99998mu\to\mathscr{S}_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\mskip 2.00002mu
is an isomorphism of

\;{\mathbb{C}}[\mskip 0.99998mu\mskip 2.00002mu\acute{\mskip-2.00002mu{\boldsymbol{z}}}\vphantom{{\boldsymbol{z}}}^{\pm 1}\mskip-2.00002mu,\acute{h}\vphantom{h}^{\pm 1}]\mskip 0.99998mu
-modules.

Remark 4.19
.

The algebra
\,{\mathcal{K}}_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\mskip 0.99998mu
is the equivariant
\mskip 2.00002muK\mskip-2.00002mu
-theory algebra

\,K_{T\mskip-0.99998mu\times{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}}\mskip-0.99998mu({T^{*}\mskip-2.00002mu{\mathcal{F}}_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}}\mskip 2.00002mu;{\mathbb{C}})\mskip 2.00002mu
of the cotangent bundle of the partial
flag variety
\,{\mathcal{F}}_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\mskip 2.00002mu
, see the notation in Section
6
.

4.7.
Levelt fundamental solution

Recall Definition
4.8
of a function
f({\boldsymbol{q}})\,
holomorphic in the unit
polydisk around
\,{\mathchoice{\raise 0.68889pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\mskip 2.00002mu\wr$\hss}0}{\raise 0.68889pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\mskip 2.00002mu\wr$\hss}0}{\raise 0.55199pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\mskip 2.00002mu\wr$\hss}0}{\raise 0.38748pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\mskip 2.00002mu\wr$\hss}0}}\,
.
The dynamical Hamiltonians
\,X_{1}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h;{\boldsymbol{q}}),\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak X_{n}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h;{\boldsymbol{q}})\,
given by
(
2.8
) are holomorphic in
\,{\boldsymbol{q}}\,
in the unit polydisk around
\,{\mathchoice{\raise 0.68889pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\mskip 2.00002mu\wr$\hss}0}{\raise 0.68889pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\mskip 2.00002mu\wr$\hss}0}{\raise 0.55199pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\mskip 2.00002mu\wr$\hss}0}{\raise 0.38748pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\mskip 2.00002mu\wr$\hss}0}}\,
and

(4.47)

X_{i}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h;{\mathchoice{\raise 0.68889pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\mskip 2.00002mu\wr$\hss}0}{\raise 0.68889pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\mskip 2.00002mu\wr$\hss}0}{\raise 0.55199pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\mskip 2.00002mu\wr$\hss}0}{\raise 0.38748pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\mskip 2.00002mu\wr$\hss}0}})\,=\,\sum_{a=1}^{n}\,z_{a}\mskip 0.99998mue^{(a)}_{i,\mskip 0.99998mui}-\mskip 2.00002muh\mskip-2.00002mu\sum_{1\leqslant a<b\leqslant n}\mskip-2.00002mu\biggl(\,\sum_{j=1}^{i-1}\,e_{j,\mskip 0.99998mui}^{(a)}\mskip 0.99998mue_{i,\mskip 0.99998muj}^{(b)}\,-\mskip-2.00002mu\sum_{j=i+1}^{N}\mskip-2.00002mue_{i,\mskip 0.99998muj}^{(a)}\mskip 0.99998mue_{j,\mskip 0.99998mui}^{(b)}\mskip 2.00002mu\biggr)\mskip 0.99998mu.\kern-1.99997pt

Notice that for
\,I\mskip-0.99998mu\in{{\mathcal{I}}_{{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}}}\,
,

X_{i}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h;{\mathchoice{\raise 0.68889pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\mskip 2.00002mu\wr$\hss}0}{\raise 0.68889pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\mskip 2.00002mu\wr$\hss}0}{\raise 0.55199pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\mskip 2.00002mu\wr$\hss}0}{\raise 0.38748pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\mskip 2.00002mu\wr$\hss}0}})\,v_{I}\mskip 2.00002mu=\mskip 2.00002mu\sum_{\mskip 0.99998mua\in I_{i}}z_{a}\mskip 0.99998muv_{I}\mskip 0.99998mu+\!\mskip-0.99998mu\sum_{\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{1}{\scriptstyle J\in\:\Il}{\scriptstyle|\si_J\<|<|\si_I\<|}}\!\xi_{\mskip 0.99998mui,\mskip 0.99998muI\mskip-0.99998mu,\mskip 0.99998muJ}\,v_{J}\,,

where the coefficients
\,\xi_{\mskip 0.99998mui,\mskip 0.99998muI\mskip-0.99998mu,\mskip 0.99998muJ}\mskip 2.00002mu
take values
\;0\mskip 0.99998mu,\pm\mskip 2.00002muh\,
.
Therefore, the eigenvalues of the restriction of the operator

\,X_{i}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h;{\mathchoice{\raise 0.68889pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\mskip 2.00002mu\wr$\hss}0}{\raise 0.68889pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\mskip 2.00002mu\wr$\hss}0}{\raise 0.55199pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\mskip 2.00002mu\wr$\hss}0}{\raise 0.38748pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\mskip 2.00002mu\wr$\hss}0}})\,
on
\,({\mathbb{C}}^{N})^{\otimes\mskip 0.99998mun}_{\mskip 0.99998mu{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}}\,
are
\,\sum_{\mskip 0.99998mua\in I_{i}}\mskip-0.99998muz_{a}\,
,

\,I\mskip-0.99998mu\in{{\mathcal{I}}_{{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}}}\,
. A more detailed statement is given by Proposition
4.20

below.

Recall the function
\,\Psi^{\raise 1.20552pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\diamond$}}_{\mskip-0.99998muI\mskip-0.99998mu,\mskip 0.99998mu0}\mskip 0.99998mu({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h)\,
,
\,I\mskip-0.99998mu\in{{\mathcal{I}}_{{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}}}\,
,
given by (
4.24
).

Proposition 4.20
.

Given
\,I\mskip-0.99998mu\in{{\mathcal{I}}_{{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}}}\,
, we have

\;X_{i}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h;{\mathchoice{\raise 0.68889pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\mskip 2.00002mu\wr$\hss}0}{\raise 0.68889pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\mskip 2.00002mu\wr$\hss}0}{\raise 0.55199pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\mskip 2.00002mu\wr$\hss}0}{\raise 0.38748pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\mskip 2.00002mu\wr$\hss}0}})\,\Psi^{\raise 1.20552pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\diamond$}}_{\mskip-0.99998muI\mskip-0.99998mu,\mskip 0.99998mu0}\mskip 0.99998mu({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h)\mskip 0.99998mu=\mskip 0.99998mu\sum_{\mskip 0.99998mua\in I_{i}}\mskip-2.00002muz_{a}\,\Psi^{\raise 1.20552pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\diamond$}}_{\mskip-0.99998muI\mskip-0.99998mu,\mskip 0.99998mu0}\mskip 0.99998mu({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h)\,
, and

\,\Psi^{\raise 1.20552pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\diamond$}}_{\mskip-0.99998muI\mskip-0.99998mu,\mskip 0.99998mu0}\mskip 0.99998mu({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h)\neq 0\mskip 2.00002mu
provided
\,z_{a}\mskip-0.99998mu\neq z_{b}\,
for all pairs

\,a,b\mskip 2.00002mu
such that
\,a<b\mskip 2.00002mu
and
\,\sigma_{I}^{-1}(a)>\sigma_{I}^{-1}(b)\,
.

Proof.

The first part of the statement follows from Theorem
4.9
.
The nonvanishing of
\,\Psi^{\raise 1.20552pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\diamond$}}_{\mskip-0.99998muI\mskip-0.99998mu,\mskip 0.99998mu0}\mskip 0.99998mu({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h)\,
is implied by
formula (
4.24
) and Lemmas
4.3
,
4.4
.
∎

For
\,I\mskip-0.99998mu\in{{\mathcal{I}}_{{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}}}\,
, set

\,{\boldsymbol{E}}_{I}({\boldsymbol{z}})=\bigl(E^{(1)}_{I}({\boldsymbol{z}}),\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak E^{(N\mskip-0.99998mu-1)}_{I}({\boldsymbol{z}})\bigr)\,
,
where
\,E^{(i)}_{I}\mskip-0.99998mu({\boldsymbol{z}})=\sum_{j=1}^{i}\mskip 0.99998mu\sum_{\mskip 0.99998mua\in I_{j}}\mskip-0.99998muz_{a}\,

is the eigenvalue of

\,X_{1}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h;{\mathchoice{\raise 0.68889pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\mskip 2.00002mu\wr$\hss}0}{\raise 0.68889pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\mskip 2.00002mu\wr$\hss}0}{\raise 0.55199pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\mskip 2.00002mu\wr$\hss}0}{\raise 0.38748pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\mskip 2.00002mu\wr$\hss}0}})+\allowbreak\dots+\allowbreak X_{i}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h;{\mathchoice{\raise 0.68889pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\mskip 2.00002mu\wr$\hss}0}{\raise 0.68889pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\mskip 2.00002mu\wr$\hss}0}{\raise 0.55199pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\mskip 2.00002mu\wr$\hss}0}{\raise 0.38748pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\mskip 2.00002mu\wr$\hss}0}})\,
on
\,\mskip 0.99998mu\Psi^{\raise 1.20552pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\diamond$}}_{\mskip-0.99998muI\mskip-0.99998mu,\mskip 0.99998mu0}\mskip 0.99998mu({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h)\,
.
For
\,I\mskip-0.99998mu,J\mskip-0.99998mu\in{{\mathcal{I}}_{{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}}}\,
, denote by
D_{I\mskip-0.99998mu,\mskip 0.99998muJ}
the set of points
\,{\boldsymbol{z}}\,

such that

\,{\boldsymbol{E}}_{I}({\boldsymbol{z}})\neq{\boldsymbol{E}}_{J}({\boldsymbol{z}})\,

Set
\,D_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\mskip-0.99998mu=\mskip 0.99998mu\bigcup_{\mskip 0.99998muI\mskip-0.99998mu,\mskip 0.99998muJ\in\mskip 0.99998mu{{\mathcal{I}}_{{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}}}}D_{I\mskip-0.99998mu,\mskip 0.99998muJ}\,
.

Theorem 4.21
.

(i)

\,{\boldsymbol{z}},h\mskip 2.00002mu

is the identity operator and the function

(4.48)

solves dynamical differential equations (

).
For given
\,{\boldsymbol{z}},h\,

\,\mskip 0.99998mu\Psi^{\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mskip-0.99998mu$}}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h;{\boldsymbol{q}})\mskip 0.99998mu

\,{\boldsymbol{q}}\,
.

.

Lemma 4.23
.

Given
\,A\in\operatorname{End}\mskip 0.99998mu\bigl(({\mathbb{C}}^{N})^{\otimes\mskip 0.99998mun}_{\mskip 0.99998mu{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}}\bigr)\,
, assume that the function

F_{A}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h;{\boldsymbol{q}})\,=\,\prod_{i=1}^{N}\,q_{i}^{\mskip 2.00002mu\smash{X_{i}\mskip-0.99998mu({\boldsymbol{z}};h;\mskip 0.99998mu{\mathchoice{\raise 0.48222pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\mskip 2.00002mu\wr$\hss}0}{\raise 0.48222pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\mskip 2.00002mu\wr$\hss}0}{\raise 0.38638pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\mskip 2.00002mu\wr$\hss}0}{\raise 0.27122pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\mskip 2.00002mu\wr$\hss}0}})}}\,\mskip 0.99998muA\;\prod_{i=1}^{N}\,q_{i}^{\smash{-\mskip-0.99998muX_{i}\mskip-0.99998mu({\boldsymbol{z}};h;\mskip 0.99998mu{\mathchoice{\raise 0.48222pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\mskip 2.00002mu\wr$\hss}0}{\raise 0.48222pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\mskip 2.00002mu\wr$\hss}0}{\raise 0.38638pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\mskip 2.00002mu\wr$\hss}0}{\raise 0.27122pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\mskip 2.00002mu\wr$\hss}0}})}}

is holomorphic in
\,{\boldsymbol{q}}\,
in the unit polydisk around
\,{\mathchoice{\raise 0.68889pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\mskip 2.00002mu\wr$\hss}0}{\raise 0.68889pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\mskip 2.00002mu\wr$\hss}0}{\raise 0.55199pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\mskip 2.00002mu\wr$\hss}0}{\raise 0.38748pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\mskip 2.00002mu\wr$\hss}0}}\,
,
and
\,F_{A}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h;{\mathchoice{\raise 0.68889pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\mskip 2.00002mu\wr$\hss}0}{\raise 0.68889pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\mskip 2.00002mu\wr$\hss}0}{\raise 0.55199pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\mskip 2.00002mu\wr$\hss}0}{\raise 0.38748pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\mskip 2.00002mu\wr$\hss}0}})=1\,
, the identity operator. Then
\,A=1\mskip 2.00002mu
and

\,F_{A}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h;{\boldsymbol{q}})=1\,
, provided
\,{\boldsymbol{z}}\not\in D_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\,
. On the other hand,
if
\,{\boldsymbol{z}}\in D_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\,
, then there exists
\,A\neq 1\mskip 2.00002mu
such that the function

\,F_{A}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h;{\boldsymbol{q}})
has the requested properties and is not a constant function
of
\,{\boldsymbol{q}}\,
.

Proof.

The statement follows from a basic linear algebra reasoning.
∎

Proof of Theorem
4.21
.

For the uniqueness statement, assume that the function
\,\Psi^{\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mskip-0.99998mu$}}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h;{\boldsymbol{q}})\,

is as required in Theorem
4.21
. Then the function

\,\Psi^{\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mskip-0.99998mu$}}_{1}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h;{\boldsymbol{q}})\,
has the same requested properties
if and only if the product

E({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h)\,=\,\prod_{i=1}^{N}\,q_{i}^{\smash{-\mskip-0.99998muX_{i}\mskip-0.99998mu({\boldsymbol{z}};h;\mskip 0.99998mu{\mathchoice{\raise 0.48222pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\mskip 2.00002mu\wr$\hss}0}{\raise 0.48222pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\mskip 2.00002mu\wr$\hss}0}{\raise 0.38638pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\mskip 2.00002mu\wr$\hss}0}{\raise 0.27122pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\mskip 2.00002mu\wr$\hss}0}})}}\,\Psi^{\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mskip-0.99998mu$}}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h;{\boldsymbol{q}})^{-1}\mskip 2.00002mu\Psi^{\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mskip-0.99998mu$}}_{1}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h;{\boldsymbol{q}})\,\mskip 0.99998mu\prod_{i=1}^{N}\,q_{i}^{\mskip 2.00002mu\smash{X_{i}\mskip-0.99998mu({\boldsymbol{z}};h;\mskip 0.99998mu{\mathchoice{\raise 0.48222pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\mskip 2.00002mu\wr$\hss}0}{\raise 0.48222pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\mskip 2.00002mu\wr$\hss}0}{\raise 0.38638pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\mskip 2.00002mu\wr$\hss}0}{\raise 0.27122pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\mskip 2.00002mu\wr$\hss}0}})}}\ignorespaces

does not depend on
\,{\boldsymbol{q}}\,
, whilst the function

F({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h;{\boldsymbol{q}})\,=\,\prod_{i=1}^{N}\,q_{i}^{\mskip 2.00002mu\smash{X_{i}\mskip-0.99998mu({\boldsymbol{z}};h;\mskip 0.99998mu{\mathchoice{\raise 0.48222pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\mskip 2.00002mu\wr$\hss}0}{\raise 0.48222pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\mskip 2.00002mu\wr$\hss}0}{\raise 0.38638pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\mskip 2.00002mu\wr$\hss}0}{\raise 0.27122pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\mskip 2.00002mu\wr$\hss}0}})}}\mskip 2.00002muE({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h)\,\prod_{i=1}^{N}\,q_{i}^{\smash{-\mskip-0.99998muX_{i}\mskip-0.99998mu({\boldsymbol{z}};h;\mskip 0.99998mu{\mathchoice{\raise 0.48222pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\mskip 2.00002mu\wr$\hss}0}{\raise 0.48222pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\mskip 2.00002mu\wr$\hss}0}{\raise 0.38638pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\mskip 2.00002mu\wr$\hss}0}{\raise 0.27122pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\mskip 2.00002mu\wr$\hss}0}})}}\mskip 2.00002mu=\,\Psi^{\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mskip-0.99998mu$}}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h;{\boldsymbol{q}})^{-1}\mskip 2.00002mu\Psi^{\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mskip-0.99998mu$}}_{1}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h;{\boldsymbol{q}})\,.\kern-6.00006pt\ignorespaces

is holomorphic in
\,{\boldsymbol{q}}\,
in the unit polydisk around
\,{\mathchoice{\raise 0.68889pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\mskip 2.00002mu\wr$\hss}0}{\raise 0.68889pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\mskip 2.00002mu\wr$\hss}0}{\raise 0.55199pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\mskip 2.00002mu\wr$\hss}0}{\raise 0.38748pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\mskip 2.00002mu\wr$\hss}0}}\,
,
and
\,F({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h;{\mathchoice{\raise 0.68889pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\mskip 2.00002mu\wr$\hss}0}{\raise 0.68889pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\mskip 2.00002mu\wr$\hss}0}{\raise 0.55199pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\mskip 2.00002mu\wr$\hss}0}{\raise 0.38748pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\mskip 2.00002mu\wr$\hss}0}})\,
is the identity operator.
Thus the uniqueness statement follows from Lemma
4.23
.

Recall the functions
\,\Psi^{\raise 1.20552pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\diamond$}}_{\!J}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h;{\boldsymbol{q}})\,
and

\,\Psi^{\raise 1.20552pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\diamond$}}_{\mskip-0.99998muJ,\mskip 0.99998mu0}\mskip 0.99998mu({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h)\,
, see (

), (

).
By Lemma
4.3
,

(4.54)

\Psi^{\raise 1.20552pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\diamond$}}_{\mskip-0.99998muJ,\mskip 0.99998mu0}\mskip 0.99998mu({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h)\,=\,\prod_{i=1}^{N-1}\prod_{j=i+1}^{N}\,\mskip 0.99998mu\prod_{a\in J_{i}}\mskip 2.00002mu\prod_{\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{1}{\scriptstyle b\in J_j}{\scriptstyle b>a}}\mskip 0.99998mu\frac{1}{z_{a}\mskip-0.99998mu-z_{b}\mskip-0.99998mu-h}\;\sum_{I\mskip-0.99998mu\in{{\mathcal{I}}_{{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}}}}\mskip 2.00002mu\frac{W_{I}(\Sigma_{J}\mskip 0.99998mu;{\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h)}{c_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}(\Sigma_{J}\mskip 0.99998mu;h)}\;v_{I}\,.\kern-20.00003pt\ignorespaces

Let
\,\Psi^{\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mskip-0.99998mu$}}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h;{\boldsymbol{q}})\,
to be the operator such that for any

\,I\mskip-0.99998mu\in{{\mathcal{I}}_{{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}}}\,
,

(4.55)

\displaystyle\Psi^{\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mskip-0.99998mu$}}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h;{\boldsymbol{q}})\mskip 0.99998mu:\mskip 0.99998muv_{I}\,\mapsto\mskip 2.00002mu\sum_{J\in{{\mathcal{I}}_{{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}}}}\,

\displaystyle\Psi^{\raise 1.20552pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\diamond$}}_{\!J}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h;{\boldsymbol{q}})\,\prod_{i=1}^{N-1}\prod_{j=i+1}^{N}\,\mskip 0.99998mu\prod_{a\in J_{i}}\mskip 2.00002mu\prod_{b\in J_{j}}\mskip 2.00002mu\frac{\Gamma(z_{b}\mskip-0.99998mu-z_{a})}{\pi}\,\times{}

\displaystyle{}\times\,{}

\displaystyle\frac{{\check{W}}_{I}(\Sigma_{J}\mskip 0.99998mu;{\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h)}{c_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}(\Sigma_{J}\mskip 0.99998mu;h)}\;\prod_{i=1}^{N-1}\prod_{j=i+1}^{N}\,\mskip 0.99998mu\prod_{a\in J_{i}}\mskip 2.00002mu\prod_{\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{1}{\scriptstyle b\in J_j}{\scriptstyle b<a}}\mskip 2.00002mu\frac{1}{z_{a}\mskip-0.99998mu-z_{b}\mskip-0.99998mu-h}\kern-15.00002pt

We will verify below that the function
\,\Psi^{\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mskip-0.99998mu$}}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h;{\boldsymbol{q}})\,
is as required
in Theorem
4.21
.

By Theorem
4.9
, the functions
\,\Psi^{\raise 1.20552pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\diamond$}}_{\!J}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h;{\boldsymbol{q}})\,
are
holomorphic in
\,{\boldsymbol{q}}\,
in the unit polydisk around
\,{\mathchoice{\raise 0.68889pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\mskip 2.00002mu\wr$\hss}0}{\raise 0.68889pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\mskip 2.00002mu\wr$\hss}0}{\raise 0.55199pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\mskip 2.00002mu\wr$\hss}0}{\raise 0.38748pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\mskip 2.00002mu\wr$\hss}0}}\,
, hence
so does
\,\Psi^{\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mskip-0.99998mu$}}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h;{\boldsymbol{q}})\,
. Then
\,\Psi^{\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mskip-0.99998mu$}}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h;{\mathchoice{\raise 0.68889pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\mskip 2.00002mu\wr$\hss}0}{\raise 0.68889pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\mskip 2.00002mu\wr$\hss}0}{\raise 0.55199pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\mskip 2.00002mu\wr$\hss}0}{\raise 0.38748pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\mskip 2.00002mu\wr$\hss}0}})\,
is the identity
operator by formulae (

), (

), and orthogonality relation
(

).

By formulae (

), (
4.54
), and Proposition
4.20
,

\prod_{i=1}^{N}\mskip 0.99998muq_{i}^{\mskip 2.00002mu\smash{X_{i}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h;\mskip 0.99998mu{\mathchoice{\raise 0.48222pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\mskip 2.00002mu\wr$\hss}0}{\raise 0.48222pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\mskip 2.00002mu\wr$\hss}0}{\raise 0.38638pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\mskip 2.00002mu\wr$\hss}0}{\raise 0.27122pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\mskip 2.00002mu\wr$\hss}0}})}}\mskip 0.99998muv_{I}\mskip 2.00002mu=\mskip-2.00002mu\sum_{J,\mskip 0.99998muK\mskip-0.99998mu\in\mskip 0.99998mu{{\mathcal{I}}_{{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}}}}\mskip-0.99998mu\frac{{\check{W}}_{I}(\Sigma_{J}\mskip 0.99998mu;{\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h)\,W_{K}(\Sigma_{J}\mskip 0.99998mu;{\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h)}{c_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}^{\mskip 2.00002mu2}(\Sigma_{J}\mskip 0.99998mu;h)\mskip 2.00002muR_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}({\boldsymbol{z}}_{\sigma_{J}}\mskip-0.99998mu)\mskip 2.00002muQ_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}({\boldsymbol{z}}_{\sigma_{J}};h)}\,\,\prod_{i=1}^{N}\,q_{i}^{\mskip 2.00002mu\sum_{\smash{a\in J_{i}}}\mskip-2.00002muz_{a}}\mskip 2.00002muv_{K}\,.\kern-10.00002pt\ignorespaces

Then by formulae (
4.55
), (
4.10
), (
4.22
),
the functions

\displaystyle\Psi^{\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mskip-0.99998mu$}}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h;{\boldsymbol{q}})\mskip 2.00002mu\prod_{i=1}^{N}\mskip 0.99998muq_{i}^{\mskip 2.00002mu\smash{X_{i}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h;\mskip 0.99998mu{\mathchoice{\raise 0.48222pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\mskip 2.00002mu\wr$\hss}0}{\raise 0.48222pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\mskip 2.00002mu\wr$\hss}0}{\raise 0.38638pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\mskip 2.00002mu\wr$\hss}0}{\raise 0.27122pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\mskip 2.00002mu\wr$\hss}0}})}}\mskip 0.99998muv_{I}\mskip 2.00002mu=\mskip 2.00002mu\sum_{J\in{{\mathcal{I}}_{{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}}}}\Psi_{\mskip-2.00002muJ}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h;{\boldsymbol{q}})\;\frac{{\check{W}}_{I}(\Sigma_{J}\mskip 0.99998mu;{\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h)}{c_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}(\Sigma_{J}\mskip 0.99998mu;h)}\;\prod_{i=1}^{N}\,e^{\mskip 0.99998mu\pi\sqrt{\mskip-0.99998mu-1}\,(\lambda_{i}-n)\mskip 2.00002mu\sum_{a\in J_{i}}\mskip-2.00002muz_{a}}\mskip 0.99998mu\times{}\kern-11.99998pt

\displaystyle{}\times\mskip 2.00002mu\prod_{i=1}^{N-1}\prod_{j=i+1}^{N}\,\mskip 0.99998mu\prod_{a\in J_{i}}\mskip 2.00002mu\prod_{b\in J_{j}}\mskip 2.00002mu\frac{-\mskip 2.00002mu1}{\Gamma(1+z_{a}\mskip-0.99998mu-z_{b})\mskip 2.00002mu\Gamma(1-z_{a}\mskip-0.99998mu+z_{b}\mskip-0.99998mu+h)}\;\kern-11.99998pt

are linear combinations of the solutions
\,\Psi_{\mskip-2.00002muJ}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h;{\boldsymbol{q}})\,
of
dynamical differential equations (

) with coefficients independent
of
\,{\boldsymbol{q}}\,
. Hence,
\,\widehat{\Psi}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h;{\boldsymbol{q}})=\Psi^{\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mskip-0.99998mu$}}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h;{\boldsymbol{q}})\mskip 2.00002mu\prod_{i=1}^{N}\,q_{i}^{\mskip 2.00002mu\smash{X_{i}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h;\mskip 0.99998mu{\mathchoice{\raise 0.48222pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\mskip 2.00002mu\wr$\hss}0}{\raise 0.48222pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\mskip 2.00002mu\wr$\hss}0}{\raise 0.38638pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\mskip 2.00002mu\wr$\hss}0}{\raise 0.27122pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\mskip 2.00002mu\wr$\hss}0}})}}\mskip 2.00002mu
solves dynamical
differential equations (
2.7
).

The determinant
\;\det\mskip 0.99998mu\widehat{\Psi}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h;{\boldsymbol{q}})\,
satisfies the equations

\Bigl(\mskip 0.99998muq_{i}\mskip 0.99998mu\frac{\partial}{\partial q_{i}}\mskip 2.00002mu-\mskip 2.00002mu\operatorname{tr}\mskip 0.99998muX_{i}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h;{\boldsymbol{q}})|_{\mskip 0.99998mu({\mathbb{C}}^{N})^{\otimes\mskip 0.99998mun}_{\mskip 0.99998mu{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}}}\Bigr)\mskip 2.00002mu\det\mskip 0.99998mu\widehat{\Psi}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h;{\boldsymbol{q}})\,=\,0\,,\qquad i=1,\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak N\mskip 2.00002mu,\kern-20.00003pt\ignorespaces

Since
\,\operatorname{tr}\mskip 0.99998muX_{i}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h;{\boldsymbol{q}})|_{\mskip 2.00002mu({\mathbb{C}}^{N})^{\otimes\mskip 0.99998mun}_{\mskip 0.99998mu{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}}}\mskip-0.99998mu=\mskip 0.99998mu\operatorname{tr}\mskip 0.99998muX_{i}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h;{\mathchoice{\raise 0.68889pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\mskip 2.00002mu\wr$\hss}0}{\raise 0.68889pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\mskip 2.00002mu\wr$\hss}0}{\raise 0.55199pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\mskip 2.00002mu\wr$\hss}0}{\raise 0.38748pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\mskip 2.00002mu\wr$\hss}0}})|_{\mskip 2.00002mu({\mathbb{C}}^{N})^{\otimes\mskip 0.99998mun}_{\mskip 0.99998mu{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}}}\mskip-0.99998mu=\mskip 0.99998mud^{(1)}_{{\boldsymbol{\lambda}},i}\,(z_{1}\mskip-0.99998mu+\allowbreak\dots+\allowbreak z_{n})\,
,
where
\,d^{(1)}_{{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\mskip 0.99998mu,\mskip 0.99998mui}\mskip 2.00002mu
are given by (
4.27
),
the determinant
\;\det\mskip 0.99998mu\Psi^{\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mskip-0.99998mu$}}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h;{\boldsymbol{q}})\,
does not depend on
{\boldsymbol{q}}\,
.
Therefore,

\det\mskip 0.99998mu\Psi^{\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mskip-0.99998mu$}}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h;{\boldsymbol{q}})\mskip 2.00002mu=\mskip 2.00002mu\det\mskip 0.99998mu\Psi^{\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mskip-0.99998mu$}}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h;{\mathchoice{\raise 0.68889pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\mskip 2.00002mu\wr$\hss}0}{\raise 0.68889pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\mskip 2.00002mu\wr$\hss}0}{\raise 0.55199pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\mskip 2.00002mu\wr$\hss}0}{\raise 0.38748pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\mskip 2.00002mu\wr$\hss}0}})\mskip 2.00002mu=\mskip 2.00002mu1\kern 16.00008pt\text{and}\kern 16.00008pt\det\mskip 0.99998mu\widehat{\Psi}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h;{\boldsymbol{q}})\,=\,\prod_{i=1}^{N}\,q_{i}^{\mskip 0.99998mu\smash{d^{(1)}_{{\boldsymbol{\lambda}},i}}\mskip 0.99998mu\sum_{a=1}^{n}z_{a}}.\kern-3.99994pt

The functions
\,\Psi^{\raise 1.20552pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\diamond$}}_{\!J}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h;{\boldsymbol{q}})\,
in formula (
4.55
) are entire
in
\,{\boldsymbol{z}},h\,
by Theorem
4.9
, and the expressions

\frac{{\check{W}}_{I}(\Sigma_{J}\mskip 0.99998mu;{\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h)}{c_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}(\Sigma_{J}\mskip 0.99998mu;h)}\;\prod_{i=1}^{N-1}\prod_{j=i+1}^{N}\,\mskip 0.99998mu\prod_{a\in J_{i}}\mskip 2.00002mu\prod_{\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{1}{\scriptstyle b\in J_j}{\scriptstyle b<a}}\mskip 2.00002mu\frac{1}{z_{a}\mskip-0.99998mu-z_{b}\mskip-0.99998mu-h}\kern-15.00002pt

are polynomials by Lemma
4.4
applied to the functions

\,{\check{W}}_{I}({\boldsymbol{t}}\mskip 0.99998mu;{\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h)\,
. Hence,
\,\Psi^{\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mskip-0.99998mu$}}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h;{\boldsymbol{q}})\,
is holomorphic
in
\,{\boldsymbol{z}},h\,
provided
\,z_{a}\mskip-0.99998mu-z_{b}\mskip-0.99998mu\not\in{\mathbb{Z}}\,
for all
\,a\neq b\,
.
Moreover,
\,\Psi^{\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\#\mskip-0.99998mu$}}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h;{\boldsymbol{q}})\,
is regular for
\,z_{a}\mskip-0.99998mu=z_{b}\,

by formula (
4.25
), and the singularities at the hyperplanes

\;z_{a}\mskip-0.99998mu-z_{b}\mskip-0.99998mu\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mskip 0.99998mu\neq\mskip 0.99998mu0}\,
are simple poles.
Theorem
4.21
is proved.

4.9.
The map
\,{\mathchoice{\hbox{\cyr B}}{\hbox{\cyr B}}{\hbox{\scyr B}}{\hbox{\sscyr B}}}_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\,
