Appendix A
Polynomial identities

Recall
\,\lambda=(\lambda_{1},\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak\lambda_{N})\,
,
\,|\mskip 0.99998mu{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\mskip 0.99998mu|=n\,
, the set
\,{{\mathcal{I}}_{{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}}}\,
,

\,I^{\mskip 0.99998mu\min}_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\mskip-2.00002mu\in{{\mathcal{I}}_{{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}}}\,
, and the permutation

\,\sigma_{I}\mskip-2.00002mu\in\mskip-0.99998muS_{n}\,
,
\,I\mskip-0.99998mu\in{{\mathcal{I}}_{{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}}}\,
, such that
\,\sigma_{I}(I^{\mskip 0.99998mu\min}_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}})=I\,
.
Let
\,S_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\mskip-0.99998mu=S_{\lambda_{1}}\!\mskip-0.99998mu\times\allowbreak\dots\times\allowbreak S_{\lambda_{N}}\!\subset S_{n}\mskip 2.00002mu
be the isotropy subgroup of
\,I^{\mskip 0.99998mu\min}_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\,
.
Each coset in
\,S_{n}/\mskip-0.99998muS_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\,
contains exactly one permutation of the form

\,\sigma_{I}\,
,
\,I\mskip-0.99998mu\in{{\mathcal{I}}_{{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}}}\,
.

Consider the variables
\,{\boldsymbol{x}}=(x_{1},\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak x_{n})\,
,
\,{\boldsymbol{y}}=(y_{1},\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak y_{n})\,
. Define

(A.1)

V_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}({\boldsymbol{x}}\mskip 0.99998mu;{\boldsymbol{y}})\,=\mskip 2.00002mu\prod_{a=1}^{\lambda^{(i)}}\,\prod_{b=\smash{\lambda^{(i)}+1}}^{n}\!\mskip-2.00002mu(x_{a}\mskip-0.99998mu-y_{b})\,.\kern-18.00005pt\ignorespaces

Clearly,

\,V_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}({\boldsymbol{x}}\mskip 0.99998mu;{\boldsymbol{y}})\in{\mathbb{C}}[\mskip 0.99998mu{\boldsymbol{x}}\mskip 0.99998mu]^{\mskip 0.99998muS_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}}\mskip-2.00002mu\otimes{\mathbb{C}}[\mskip 0.99998mu{\boldsymbol{y}}\mskip 0.99998mu]^{\mskip 0.99998muS_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}}
.
For
\,\sigma\in\mskip-0.99998muS_{n}\,
, denote
\,{\boldsymbol{x}}_{\sigma}=(x_{\sigma(1)},\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak x_{\sigma(n)})\,
.

Lemma A.1
.

We have
\,V_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}({\boldsymbol{x}}\mskip 0.99998mu;{\boldsymbol{x}}_{\sigma}\mskip-0.99998mu)=0\,
unless
\,\sigma\mskip-0.99998mu\in\mskip-0.99998muS_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\,
, and

\,V_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}({\boldsymbol{x}}_{\sigma_{I}};{\boldsymbol{x}}_{\sigma_{I}}\mskip-0.99998mu)=(-1)^{\mskip 0.99998mu|\mskip 0.99998mu\sigma_{I}|}V_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}({\boldsymbol{x}}\mskip 0.99998mu;{\boldsymbol{x}})\,
.

Proof.

Straightforward.
∎

For any function
f({\boldsymbol{x}})
, define

\operatorname{Sym}^{\mskip 0.99998mu{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}}_{\mskip 2.00002mu{\boldsymbol{x}}}f({\boldsymbol{x}})\,=\mskip 0.99998mu\sum_{I\in{{\mathcal{I}}_{{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}}}\!}\,f({\boldsymbol{x}}_{\sigma_{I}})\,.\ignorespaces

Proposition A.2
.

We have

(A.2)

V_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}({\boldsymbol{x}}\mskip 0.99998mu;{\boldsymbol{z}})\,=\,\operatorname{Sym}^{\mskip 0.99998mu{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}}_{\mskip 2.00002mu{\boldsymbol{y}}}\biggl(\mskip 0.99998mu\frac{V_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}({\boldsymbol{x}}\mskip 0.99998mu;{\boldsymbol{y}})\,V_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}({\boldsymbol{y}}\mskip 0.99998mu;{\boldsymbol{z}})}{V_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}({\boldsymbol{y}}\mskip 0.99998mu;{\boldsymbol{y}})}\mskip 0.99998mu\biggr)\,.\kern-10.00002pt\ignorespaces

Proof.

By formula (
A.1
), the expression

\,V_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}({\boldsymbol{x}}\mskip 0.99998mu;{\boldsymbol{y}})\,V_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}({\boldsymbol{y}}\mskip 0.99998mu;{\boldsymbol{z}})/\mskip 0.99998muV_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}({\boldsymbol{y}}\mskip 0.99998mu;{\boldsymbol{y}})\,
is invariant
under permutations of
\,y_{1},\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak y_{n}\,
by elements of the subgroup
\,S_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\,
.
Thus the right-hand side of formula (
A.2
) is a symmetric polynomial in

\,y_{1},\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak y_{n}\mskip 0.99998mu
of degree zero in each of the variables
\,y_{1},\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak y_{n}\,
, hence a constant.
The constant can be found by evaluating this polynomial at
\,{\boldsymbol{y}}={\boldsymbol{x}}\,
or

\,{\boldsymbol{y}}={\boldsymbol{z}}\,
.
∎

Proposition A.3
.

For any polynomial
\;f\mskip-0.99998mu\in{\mathbb{C}}[{\boldsymbol{x}}]^{\mskip 0.99998muS_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}}\mskip 0.99998mu
, we have

(A.3)

f({\boldsymbol{x}})\,=\,\operatorname{Sym}^{\mskip 0.99998mu{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}}_{\mskip 2.00002mu{\boldsymbol{y}}}\biggl(\mskip 0.99998mu\frac{V_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}({\boldsymbol{x}}\mskip 0.99998mu;{\boldsymbol{y}})}{V_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}({\boldsymbol{y}}\mskip 0.99998mu;{\boldsymbol{y}})}\;\operatorname{Sym}^{\mskip 0.99998mu{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}}_{\mskip 2.00002mu{\boldsymbol{x}}}\biggl(\frac{f({\boldsymbol{x}})\,V_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}({\boldsymbol{y}}\mskip 0.99998mu;{\boldsymbol{x}})}{V_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}({\boldsymbol{x}}\mskip 0.99998mu;{\boldsymbol{x}})}\mskip 0.99998mu\biggr)\mskip-2.00002mu\biggr)\,.\kern-16.00008pt\ignorespaces

Proof.

Changing the order of symmetrizations in the right-hand side of
formula (
A.3
) and applying formula (
A.2
) yields

\displaystyle\operatorname{Sym}^{\mskip 0.99998mu{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}}_{\mskip 2.00002mu{\boldsymbol{y}}}\biggl(\mskip 0.99998mu\frac{V_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}({\boldsymbol{x}}\mskip 0.99998mu;{\boldsymbol{y}})}{V_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}({\boldsymbol{y}}\mskip 0.99998mu;{\boldsymbol{y}})}\,\biggl(\operatorname{Sym}^{\mskip 0.99998mu{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}}_{\mskip 2.00002mu{\boldsymbol{z}}}\biggl(\frac{f({\boldsymbol{z}})\,V_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}({\boldsymbol{y}}\mskip 0.99998mu;{\boldsymbol{z}})}{V_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;{\boldsymbol{z}})}\mskip 0.99998mu\biggr)\mskip-2.00002mu\biggr)\bigg|_{\mskip 0.99998mu{\boldsymbol{z}}={\boldsymbol{x}}}\mskip 2.00002mu\biggr)\mskip 0.99998mu={}

\displaystyle\,{}=\mskip 0.99998mu\biggl(\operatorname{Sym}^{\mskip 0.99998mu{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}}_{\mskip 2.00002mu{\boldsymbol{z}}}\biggl(\frac{f({\boldsymbol{z}})}{V_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;{\boldsymbol{z}})}\,\operatorname{Sym}^{\mskip 0.99998mu{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}}_{\mskip 2.00002mu{\boldsymbol{y}}}\biggl(\mskip 0.99998mu\frac{V_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}({\boldsymbol{x}}\mskip 0.99998mu;{\boldsymbol{y}})\,V_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}({\boldsymbol{y}}\mskip 0.99998mu;{\boldsymbol{z}})}{V_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}({\boldsymbol{y}}\mskip 0.99998mu;{\boldsymbol{y}})}\mskip 0.99998mu\biggr)\mskip-2.00002mu\biggr)\mskip-2.00002mu\biggr)\bigg|_{\mskip 0.99998mu{\boldsymbol{z}}={\boldsymbol{x}}}\!\!\!={}

\displaystyle\,{}=\mskip 0.99998mu\biggl(\operatorname{Sym}^{\mskip 0.99998mu{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}}_{\mskip 2.00002mu{\boldsymbol{z}}}\biggl(\frac{f({\boldsymbol{z}})\,V_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}({\boldsymbol{x}}\mskip 0.99998mu;{\boldsymbol{z}})}{V_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;{\boldsymbol{z}})}\mskip 0.99998mu\biggr)\mskip-2.00002mu\biggr)\bigg|_{\mskip 0.99998mu{\boldsymbol{z}}={\boldsymbol{x}}}\!\!\!=\mskip 2.00002muf({\boldsymbol{x}})\,.

∎

The expression
\;\operatorname{Sym}^{\mskip 0.99998mu{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}}_{\mskip 2.00002mu{\boldsymbol{x}}}\bigl(f({\boldsymbol{x}})\,V_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}({\boldsymbol{y}}\mskip 0.99998mu;{\boldsymbol{x}})/\mskip 0.99998muV_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}({\boldsymbol{x}}\mskip 0.99998mu;{\boldsymbol{x}})\bigr)\,

is a symmetric polynomial in
\,x_{1},\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak x_{n}\,
. Thus Lemma
A.1
and
Proposition
A.3
show that the polynomials
V_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}({\boldsymbol{x}}\mskip 0.99998mu;{\boldsymbol{y}}_{\sigma_{I}})\,
,

\,I\mskip-0.99998mu\in{{\mathcal{I}}_{{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}}}\,
, give a basis of the space
\,{\mathbb{C}}[{\boldsymbol{x}}]^{\mskip 0.99998muS_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}}
as a free
module over the ring of symmetric polynomials
\,{\mathbb{C}}[{\boldsymbol{x}}]^{\mskip 0.99998muS_{n}}
.

Proposition A.4
.

We have

(A.4)

\det\mskip 0.99998mu\bigl(V_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}({\boldsymbol{x}}_{\sigma_{I}};{\boldsymbol{y}}_{\sigma_{J}}\mskip-0.99998mu)\bigr)_{\mskip 0.99998muI\mskip-0.99998mu,\mskip 0.99998muJ\in\mskip 0.99998mu{{\mathcal{I}}_{{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}}}}=\,\prod_{a=1}^{n-1}\,\prod_{b=a+1}^{n}\bigl(\mskip-0.99998mu(x_{a}\mskip-0.99998mu-x_{b})\mskip 2.00002mu(y_{b}\mskip-0.99998mu-y_{a})\bigr)^{d^{(2)}_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}}\mskip-0.99998mu,\kern-20.00003pt\ignorespaces

where

d^{(2)}_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\mskip 2.00002mu=\,\frac{(n-2)\mskip 0.99998mu!}{\lambda_{1}\mskip 0.99998mu!\ldots\lambda_{N}\mskip 0.99998mu!}\,\sum_{i=1}^{N-1}\sum_{j=i+1}^{N}\lambda_{i}\mskip 2.00002mu\lambda_{j}\,.\kern-20.00003pt\ignorespaces

Proof.

Consider the matrix
\,M({\boldsymbol{x}}\mskip 0.99998mu;{\boldsymbol{y}})=\bigl(V_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}({\boldsymbol{x}}_{\sigma_{I}};{\boldsymbol{y}}_{\sigma_{J}\mskip-0.99998mu})\bigr)_{\mskip 0.99998muI\mskip-0.99998mu,\mskip 0.99998muJ\in\mskip 0.99998mu{{\mathcal{I}}_{{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}}}}\mskip 0.99998mu
.
Let
\,F({\boldsymbol{x}}\mskip 0.99998mu;{\boldsymbol{y}})=\det\mskip 0.99998muM({\boldsymbol{x}}\mskip 0.99998mu;{\boldsymbol{y}})\,
. Formula (
A.2
) reads

\,M({\boldsymbol{x}}\mskip 0.99998mu;{\boldsymbol{z}})=M({\boldsymbol{x}}\mskip 0.99998mu;{\boldsymbol{y}})\mskip 2.00002mu\bigl(M({\boldsymbol{y}}\mskip 0.99998mu;{\boldsymbol{y}})^{-1}\mskip 2.00002muM({\boldsymbol{y}}\mskip 0.99998mu;{\boldsymbol{z}})\,
, thus

(A.5)

F({\boldsymbol{x}}\mskip 0.99998mu;{\boldsymbol{z}})\,=\,\frac{F({\boldsymbol{x}}\mskip 0.99998mu;{\boldsymbol{y}})\mskip 2.00002muF({\boldsymbol{y}}\mskip 0.99998mu;{\boldsymbol{z}})}{F({\boldsymbol{y}}\mskip 0.99998mu;{\boldsymbol{y}})}\;,\ignorespaces

so that
\,F({\boldsymbol{x}}\mskip 0.99998mu;{\boldsymbol{y}})=G({\boldsymbol{x}})\,\widetilde{G}({\boldsymbol{y}})\,
for some functions
\,G\mskip 2.00002mu,\widetilde{G}\,
.
Also,
\,F({\boldsymbol{x}}\mskip 0.99998mu;{\boldsymbol{y}})=F({\boldsymbol{y}}\mskip 0.99998mu;{\boldsymbol{x}})\,
, since the matrix
\,M({\boldsymbol{y}}\mskip 0.99998mu;{\boldsymbol{x}})
is
the transpose of
\,M({\boldsymbol{x}}\mskip 0.99998mu;{\boldsymbol{y}})\,
. Hence the functions
\,G\mskip 2.00002mu
and
\,\widetilde{G}\mskip 2.00002mu

are proportional and one can take
\,\widetilde{G}=G\,
.
Finally, the matrix
\,M({\boldsymbol{x}}\mskip 0.99998mu;{\boldsymbol{x}})\,
is diagonal and

\bigl(G({\boldsymbol{x}})\bigr)^{2}\mskip 0.99998mu=\,F({\boldsymbol{x}}\mskip 0.99998mu;{\boldsymbol{x}})\,=\mskip 2.00002mu\prod_{I\in{{\mathcal{I}}_{{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}}}\!}\mskip 2.00002muV_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}({\boldsymbol{x}}_{\sigma_{I}};{\boldsymbol{x}}_{\sigma_{I}})\,=\,\prod_{a=1}^{n}\,\prod_{\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{1}{\scriptstyle b=1}{\scriptstyle b\ne a}}^{n}\,(x_{a}\mskip-0.99998mu-x_{b})^{\mskip 2.00002mud^{(2)}_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}}\mskip-0.99998mu,\kern-20.00003pt\ignorespaces

which implies formula (
A.4
).
∎

For
I\mskip-0.99998mu\in{{\mathcal{I}}_{{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}}}\,
, set

(A.6)

V_{I}({\boldsymbol{x}})\,=\,V_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\bigl(x_{1},\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak x_{n}\mskip 0.99998mu;\sigma_{I}(n)-1,\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak\sigma_{I}(1)-1\bigr)\,.\kern-20.00003pt\ignorespaces

We use the polynomials
\,V_{I}({\boldsymbol{x}})\,
in Section
4.6
to give
a basis of the algebra
\,{\mathcal{K}}_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\mskip 2.00002mu
. By formula (
A.4
),

(A.7)

\det\mskip 0.99998mu\bigl(V_{I}({\boldsymbol{x}}_{\sigma_{J}}\mskip-0.99998mu)\bigr)_{\mskip 0.99998muI\mskip-0.99998mu,\mskip 0.99998muJ\in\mskip 0.99998mu{{\mathcal{I}}_{{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}}}}=\,\prod_{k=2}^{n-1}\,j^{(n-j)\mskip 2.00002mud^{(2)}_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}}\;\prod_{a=1}^{n-1}\,\prod_{b=a+1}^{n}(x_{b}\mskip-0.99998mu-x_{a})^{\mskip 2.00002mud^{(2)}_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}}\mskip-0.99998mu.\kern-20.00003pt

Appendix B
Schubert polynomials

Consider the operators
\,\mskip 0.99998mu\Delta^{\!\raise 0.42194pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\boldsymbol{x}}$}}_{1},\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak\Delta^{\!\raise 0.42194pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\boldsymbol{x}}$}}_{\mskip 0.99998mun-1}\,
acting on functions
of
\,x_{1},\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak x_{n}\,
:

\Delta^{\!\raise 0.42194pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\boldsymbol{x}}$}}_{\mskip 0.99998mua}\mskip 0.99998muf({\boldsymbol{x}})\,=\,\frac{f({\boldsymbol{x}})-f(x_{1},\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak x_{a+1},x_{a},\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak x_{n})}{x_{a}\mskip-0.99998mu-x_{a+1}}\;.\kern-16.00008pt

They satisfy the nil-Coxeter relations,

(B.1)

(\Delta^{\!\raise 0.42194pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\boldsymbol{x}}$}}_{\mskip 0.99998mua})^{2}=\mskip 0.99998mu0\,,\kern 18.00005pt\Delta^{\!\raise 0.42194pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\boldsymbol{x}}$}}_{\mskip 0.99998mua}\mskip 2.00002mu\Delta^{\!\raise 0.42194pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\boldsymbol{x}}$}}_{\mskip 0.99998mub}=\mskip 0.99998mu\Delta^{\!\raise 0.42194pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\boldsymbol{x}}$}}_{\mskip 0.99998mub}\mskip 2.00002mu\Delta^{\!\raise 0.42194pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\boldsymbol{x}}$}}_{\mskip 0.99998mua}\,,\quad|\mskip 0.99998mua-b\mskip 0.99998mu|>1\,,\kern 18.00005pt\Delta^{\!\raise 0.42194pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\boldsymbol{x}}$}}_{\mskip 0.99998mua}\mskip 2.00002mu\Delta^{\!\raise 0.42194pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\boldsymbol{x}}$}}_{\mskip 0.99998mua+1}\mskip 2.00002mu\Delta^{\!\raise 0.42194pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\boldsymbol{x}}$}}_{\mskip 0.99998mua}=\mskip 0.99998mu\Delta^{\!\raise 0.42194pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\boldsymbol{x}}$}}_{\mskip 0.99998mua+1}\mskip 2.00002mu\Delta^{\!\raise 0.42194pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\boldsymbol{x}}$}}_{\mskip 0.99998mua}\mskip 2.00002mu\Delta^{\!\raise 0.42194pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\boldsymbol{x}}$}}_{\mskip 0.99998mua+1}\,,\kern-18.00005pt\ignorespaces

for any
\,a,b\,
.

Given a permutation
\,\sigma\mskip-0.99998mu\in\mskip-0.99998muS_{n}\,
, define the operator
\,\mskip 0.99998mu\Delta^{\!\raise 0.42194pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\boldsymbol{x}}$}}_{\sigma}\,

as follows. For
\,a=1,\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak n-1\,
, let
\,s_{a}\mskip 0.99998mu
be the transposition of
\,a\,

and
\,a+1\,
. If
\;\sigma=s_{a_{1}}\ldots\mskip 0.99998mus_{a_{k}}
is the reduced presentation,
then
\,\mskip 0.99998mu\Delta^{\!\raise 0.42194pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\boldsymbol{x}}$}}_{\sigma}\mskip-0.99998mu=\Delta^{\!\raise 0.42194pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\boldsymbol{x}}$}}_{\mskip 0.99998mua_{1}}\mskip-0.99998mu\ldots\mskip 2.00002mu\Delta^{\!\raise 0.42194pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\boldsymbol{x}}$}}_{\mskip 0.99998mua_{k}}
. In particular,

\,\mskip 0.99998mu\Delta^{\!\raise 0.42194pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\boldsymbol{x}}$}}_{\mskip 0.99998mus_{a}}\!\mskip-0.99998mu=\Delta_{\mskip 0.99998mua}\,
. The operators
\,\mskip 0.99998mu\Delta^{\!\raise 0.42194pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\boldsymbol{x}}$}}_{\sigma}\mskip 2.00002mu
are well-defined
due to relations (
B.1
).

Lemma B.1
.

For
\,\sigma,\tau\mskip-0.99998mu\in\mskip-0.99998muS_{n}\mskip 2.00002mu
, we have

\,\Delta^{\!\raise 0.42194pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\boldsymbol{x}}$}}_{\sigma}\mskip 2.00002mu\Delta^{\!\raise 0.42194pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\boldsymbol{x}}$}}_{\tau}=\mskip 0.99998mu\Delta^{\!\raise 0.42194pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\boldsymbol{x}}$}}_{\mskip 0.99998mu\sigma\tau}\mskip 0.99998mu

if
\,|\mskip 0.99998mu\sigma\mskip 2.00002mu\tau\mskip 0.99998mu|=|\mskip 0.99998mu\sigma\mskip 0.99998mu|+|\mskip 0.99998mu\tau\mskip 0.99998mu|\,
, and

\,\Delta^{\!\raise 0.42194pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\boldsymbol{x}}$}}_{\sigma}\mskip 2.00002mu\Delta^{\!\raise 0.42194pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\boldsymbol{x}}$}}_{\tau}=\mskip 0.99998mu0\,
, otherwise.

Proof.

The statement follows from relations (
B.1
).
∎

Define the operators
\,\mskip 0.99998mu\Delta^{\!\raise 0.42194pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\boldsymbol{y}}$}}_{\sigma}\,
,
\,\sigma\mskip-0.99998mu\in\mskip-0.99998muS_{n}\mskip 2.00002mu
, acting on functions
of
\,y_{1},\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak y_{n}\,
similarly.

The
A\mskip 0.99998mu
-type double Schubert polynomials
\,\mskip 0.99998mu\mathfrak{S}_{\sigma}\mskip 2.00002mu
,
\,\sigma\mskip-0.99998mu\in\mskip-0.99998muS_{n}\mskip 2.00002mu
,
see
[
L
, Chapter 2]
,
are defined as follows. For the longest permutation
\;\sigma_{0}\,
,

\,\sigma_{0}(i)=n+1-i\,
,
\;i-1,\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak n\,
, set

\mathfrak{S}_{\sigma_{0}}\mskip-0.99998mu({\boldsymbol{x}}\mskip 0.99998mu;{\boldsymbol{y}})\,=\,\prod_{i=1}^{n-1}\,\prod_{j=1}^{n-i}\,(x_{i}\mskip-0.99998mu-y_{j})\,,\kern-3.99994pt\ignorespaces

For any
\,\mskip 0.99998mu\sigma\mskip-0.99998mu\in\mskip-0.99998muS_{n}\mskip 2.00002mu
, set

\,\mskip 0.99998mu\mathfrak{S}_{\sigma}({\boldsymbol{x}}\mskip 0.99998mu;{\boldsymbol{y}})=\Delta^{\!\raise 0.42194pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\boldsymbol{x}}$}}_{\mskip 0.99998mu\sigma^{-1}\sigma_{0}}\mathfrak{S}_{\sigma_{0}}({\boldsymbol{x}}\mskip 0.99998mu;{\boldsymbol{y}})\,
.

Lemma B.2
.

For any
\,\mskip 0.99998mu\sigma\mskip-0.99998mu\in\mskip-0.99998muS_{n}\mskip 2.00002mu
, we have
\;\Delta^{\!\raise 0.42194pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\boldsymbol{x}}$}}_{\sigma}\mskip 2.00002mu\mathfrak{S}_{\sigma_{0}}({\boldsymbol{x}}\mskip 0.99998mu;{\boldsymbol{y}})=(-1)^{|\sigma|}\,\Delta^{\!\raise 0.42194pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\boldsymbol{y}}$}}_{\sigma_{0}\mskip 0.99998mu\sigma^{-1}\sigma_{0}}\mskip-0.99998mu\mathfrak{S}_{\sigma_{0}}({\boldsymbol{x}}\mskip 0.99998mu;{\boldsymbol{y}})\,
.

Proof.

The statement follow by induction on the length of
\,\sigma\,

from the equalities

\Delta^{\!\raise 0.42194pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\boldsymbol{x}}$}}_{\mskip 0.99998mus_{a}}\mskip-0.99998mu\mathfrak{S}_{\sigma_{0}}({\boldsymbol{x}}\mskip 0.99998mu;{\boldsymbol{y}})\,=\,\frac{\mathfrak{S}_{\sigma_{0}}({\boldsymbol{x}}\mskip 0.99998mu;{\boldsymbol{y}})}{x_{a}\mskip-0.99998mu-y_{n-a}}\,=\mskip 2.00002mu-\mskip 2.00002mu\Delta^{\!\raise 0.42194pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\boldsymbol{y}}$}}_{s_{n-a-1}}\mskip-0.99998mu\mathfrak{S}_{\sigma_{0}}({\boldsymbol{x}}\mskip 0.99998mu;{\boldsymbol{y}})\kern-3.99994pt\ignorespaces

and
\,s_{n-a-1}=\mskip 0.99998mu\sigma_{0}\,s_{a}\mskip 0.99998mu\sigma_{0}\,
for every
\,a=1,\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak n-1\,
.
∎

Proposition B.3
.

For any
\,\mskip 0.99998mu\sigma\mskip-0.99998mu\in\mskip-0.99998muS_{n}\mskip 2.00002mu
, we have

\mathfrak{S}_{\sigma}({\boldsymbol{x}}\mskip 0.99998mu;{\boldsymbol{y}})\,=\,(-1)^{|\sigma_{0}|-|\sigma|}\mskip 2.00002mu\Delta^{\!\raise 0.42194pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\boldsymbol{y}}$}}_{\sigma\sigma_{0}}\mathfrak{S}_{\sigma_{0}}({\boldsymbol{x}}\mskip 0.99998mu;{\boldsymbol{y}})\,=\,(-1)^{|\sigma|}\mskip 2.00002mu\mathfrak{S}_{\sigma^{-1}}({\boldsymbol{y}}\mskip 0.99998mu;{\boldsymbol{x}})\,.

Proof.

The statement follows from the equality

\,\mskip 0.99998mu\mathfrak{S}_{\sigma_{0}}({\boldsymbol{y}}\mskip 0.99998mu;{\boldsymbol{x}})=(-1)^{|\sigma_{0}|}\mskip 2.00002mu\mathfrak{S}_{\sigma_{0}}({\boldsymbol{x}}\mskip 0.99998mu;{\boldsymbol{y}})\,

and Lemma
B.2
.
∎

Denote by
\,\sigma_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\mskip 0.99998mu
the longest element of the subgroup

\,S_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\mskip-0.99998mu=S_{\lambda_{1}}\!\mskip-0.99998mu\times\allowbreak\dots\times\allowbreak S_{\lambda_{N}}\!\subset S_{n}\mskip 2.00002mu
,

\sigma_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}(a)=\lambda^{(i-1)}\mskip-2.00002mu+\lambda^{(i)}\mskip-2.00002mu+1-a\,,\qquad\lambda^{(i-1)}\mskip-2.00002mu+1<a\leqslant\lambda^{(i)}\mskip 0.99998mu,\quad i=1,\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak N\mskip 2.00002mu.\kern-8.00003pt\ignorespaces

Lemma B.4
.

We have

\Delta^{\!\raise 0.42194pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\boldsymbol{x}}$}}_{\mskip 0.99998mu\sigma_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}}\mskip-0.99998mu\mathfrak{S}_{\sigma_{0}}({\boldsymbol{x}}\mskip 0.99998mu;{\boldsymbol{y}})\,=\,\prod_{a=1}^{\lambda^{(i)}}\,\prod_{b=\smash{\lambda^{(i)}+1}}^{n}\!\mskip-2.00002mu(x_{a}\mskip-0.99998mu-y_{n-b+1})\,.\kern-18.00005pt\ignorespaces

Proof.

Straightforward.
∎

Recall the polynomials
\,\mskip 0.99998mu\mathrm{Stab}_{\mskip 0.99998muI}(\mskip 0.99998mu{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}\mskip 0.99998mu;{\boldsymbol{z}})\,
,

\mathrm{Stab}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mskip-0.99998mu\mathrm{o\mskip-0.99998mup}}_{\mskip 0.99998muI}(\mskip 0.99998mu{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}\mskip 0.99998mu;{\boldsymbol{z}})\,
, see Section
6.2
.

Proposition B.5
.

For any
\,I\mskip-0.99998mu\in{{\mathcal{I}}_{{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}}}\mskip 2.00002mu
,

(B.2)

\mathrm{Stab}_{\mskip 0.99998muI}(\mskip 0.99998mu{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}\mskip 0.99998mu;{\boldsymbol{z}})\,=\,\mathfrak{S}_{\sigma_{\mskip-2.00002mu\sigma_{\mskip-0.99998mu0}\mskip-0.99998mu(I)}}(\mskip 0.99998mu{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}\mskip 0.99998mu;{\boldsymbol{z}}_{\sigma_{0}})\,,\kern 16.00008pt\mathrm{Stab}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mskip-0.99998mu\mathrm{o\mskip-0.99998mup}}_{\mskip 0.99998muI}(\mskip 0.99998mu{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}\mskip 0.99998mu;{\boldsymbol{z}})\,=\,\mathfrak{S}_{\sigma_{I}}(\mskip 0.99998mu{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}\mskip 0.99998mu;{\boldsymbol{z}})\,.\kern-20.00003pt\ignorespaces

Proof.

By formula (
6.12
) and Lemmas
5.4
,

\mathrm{Stab}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mskip-0.99998mu\mathrm{o\mskip-0.99998mup}}_{\mskip 0.99998muI}(\mskip 0.99998mu{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}\mskip 0.99998mu;{\boldsymbol{z}})\,=\,(-1)^{|\mskip 0.99998mu\sigma_{\mskip-0.99998mu\sigma_{0}(I)}\mskip-0.99998mu|-|\sigma_{I}|}\mskip 2.00002mu\Delta^{\!\raise 0.42194pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\boldsymbol{z}}$}}_{\mskip 0.99998mu\sigma_{I}\mskip 0.99998mu\sigma_{\mskip-2.00002mu\sigma_{0}(I)}^{\smash{-1}}}\!V_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}(\mskip 0.99998mu{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}\mskip 0.99998mu;{\boldsymbol{z}}_{\sigma_{0}}\mskip-0.99998mu)\,,\kern-10.00002pt

where the operator
\,\mskip 0.99998mu\Delta^{\!\raise 0.42194pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\boldsymbol{z}}$}}_{\sigma}\,
acts on functions of
\,{\boldsymbol{z}}\,
.
By formula (
6.4
) and Lemmas
B.4
,
B.2
,

V_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}(\mskip 0.99998mu{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}\mskip 0.99998mu;{\boldsymbol{z}}_{\sigma_{0}}\mskip-0.99998mu)\,=\,(-1)^{|\sigma_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}|}\mskip 2.00002mu\Delta^{\!\raise 0.42194pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\boldsymbol{z}}$}}_{\mskip 0.99998mu\sigma_{0}\mskip 2.00002mu\sigma_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\sigma_{0}}\mathfrak{S}_{\sigma_{0}}({\boldsymbol{x}}\mskip 0.99998mu;{\boldsymbol{y}})\ignorespaces

because
\,\mskip 0.99998mu\sigma_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}^{\smash{-1}}\mskip-0.99998mu=\sigma_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\,
.
Since
\,\mskip 0.99998mu\sigma_{\mskip-2.00002mu\sigma_{0}(I)}=\mskip 0.99998mu\sigma_{0}\mskip 2.00002mu\sigma_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\,
and

\,\mskip 0.99998mu|\mskip 0.99998mu\sigma_{I}\mskip 2.00002mu\sigma_{\mskip-2.00002mu\sigma_{0}(I)}^{\smash{-1}}|+|\mskip 0.99998mu\sigma_{\sigma_{0}(I)}\mskip 2.00002mu\sigma_{0}\mskip 0.99998mu|=|\mskip 0.99998mu\sigma_{I}\mskip 2.00002mu\sigma_{0}\mskip 0.99998mu|\,
,

\mathrm{Stab}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mskip-0.99998mu\mathrm{o\mskip-0.99998mup}}_{\mskip 0.99998muI}(\mskip 0.99998mu{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}\mskip 0.99998mu;{\boldsymbol{z}})\,=\,(-1)^{|\sigma_{0}|-|\sigma|}\mskip 2.00002mu\Delta^{\!\raise 0.42194pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\boldsymbol{z}}$}}_{\sigma\sigma_{0}}\mathfrak{S}_{\sigma_{0}}(\mskip 0.99998mu{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}\mskip 0.99998mu;{\boldsymbol{z}})\,=\,\mathfrak{S}_{\sigma_{I}}(\mskip 0.99998mu{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}\mskip 0.99998mu;{\boldsymbol{z}})\,.\ignorespaces

by Lemma
B.1
and Proposition
B.3
, that proves the second
equality in (
B.2
). The first equality in (
B.2
) follows
from the relation

\,\mskip 0.99998mu\mathrm{Stab}_{\mskip 0.99998muI}(\mskip 0.99998mu{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}\mskip 0.99998mu;{\boldsymbol{z}})=\mathrm{Stab}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mskip-0.99998mu\mathrm{o\mskip-0.99998mup}}_{\mskip 0.99998mu\sigma_{0}(I)}(\mskip 0.99998mu{\boldsymbol{\Gamma}}\mskip 0.99998mu;{\boldsymbol{z}}_{\sigma_{0}}\mskip-0.99998mu)\,
.
∎

Appendix C
Proofs of Lemmas
5.2
and
5.3

Proof of Lemma
5.2
.

The proof is by induction on
\,N\mskip 0.99998mu
.
To show that
\;W^{\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\circ$}}_{I^{\mskip 0.99998mu\min}_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}}({\boldsymbol{t}}\mskip 0.99998mu;{\boldsymbol{z}})=1\,
,
set
f_{1}\mskip-0.99998mu=1\,
, and for
N\geqslant 2\,
, write

f_{N}({\boldsymbol{t}}^{(1)}\mskip-0.99998mu,\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak{\boldsymbol{t}}^{(N-1)};{\boldsymbol{z}})\,=\,W^{\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\circ$}}_{I^{\mskip 0.99998mu\min}_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}}({\boldsymbol{t}}\mskip 0.99998mu;{\boldsymbol{z}})\,,\ignorespaces

indicating the dependence on
\,N\mskip 0.99998mu
explicitly.
By formula (
5.1
), the function
\mskip 2.00002muf_{N}
is a polynomial in

\,t_{1}^{(N-1)}\mskip-0.99998mu,\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak t_{\lambda^{(N-1)}}^{(N-1)}\mskip 0.99998mu
of degree zero in each of
these variables, hence a constant. Evaluating

\mskip 2.00002muf_{N}({\boldsymbol{t}}^{(1)}\mskip-0.99998mu,\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak{\boldsymbol{t}}^{(N-1)};{\boldsymbol{z}})\,
at

\,{\boldsymbol{t}}^{(N-1)}\mskip-2.00002mu=(z_{1},\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak z_{\lambda^{(N-1)}}\mskip-0.99998mu)\,
yields

f_{N}({\boldsymbol{t}}^{(1)}\mskip-0.99998mu,\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak{\boldsymbol{t}}^{(N-1)};{\boldsymbol{z}})\,=\,f_{N-1}({\boldsymbol{t}}^{(1)}\mskip-0.99998mu,\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak{\boldsymbol{t}}^{(N-2)};z_{1},\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak z_{\lambda^{(N-1)}}\mskip-0.99998mu)\,.\ignorespaces

Thus
\mskip 2.00002muf_{N}({\boldsymbol{t}}^{(1)}\mskip-0.99998mu,\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak{\boldsymbol{t}}^{(N-1)};{\boldsymbol{z}})\mskip 0.99998mu=\mskip 0.99998mu1\;
by the induction
assumption.

The proof of formula (
5.2
) is similar.
Set
f_{1}({\boldsymbol{z}})=1\,
, and for
N\geqslant 2\,
, write

f_{N}({\boldsymbol{t}}^{(1)}\mskip-0.99998mu,\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak{\boldsymbol{t}}^{(N-1)};{\boldsymbol{z}})\,=\,W^{\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\circ$}}_{\sigma_{0}(I^{\mskip 0.99998mu\min}_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}})}({\boldsymbol{t}}\mskip 0.99998mu;{\boldsymbol{z}})\,,\ignorespaces

Define polynomials
g_{1},\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak g_{N-1}\,
,

g_{i}({\boldsymbol{t}}^{(i)};{\boldsymbol{z}})\,=\,\prod_{a=1}^{\lambda^{(i)}}\,\prod_{b=\lambda^{(i)}+1}^{\lambda^{(i+1)}}\!(t_{a}^{(i)}\mskip-2.00002mu-z_{n-b+1})\,.\ignorespaces

By formula (
5.1
), the function
\mskip 2.00002muf_{N}
is a polynomial in

\,t_{1}^{(N-1)}\mskip-0.99998mu,\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak t_{\lambda^{(N-1)}}^{(N-1)}\,
of degree
\,\lambda_{N}\,

in each of these variables, and
\mskip 2.00002muf_{N}
is divisible by

\,g_{N-1}({\boldsymbol{t}}^{(N-1)};{\boldsymbol{z}})\,
. Hence,

f_{N}({\boldsymbol{t}}^{(1)}\mskip-0.99998mu,\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak{\boldsymbol{t}}^{(N-1)};{\boldsymbol{z}})\,=\,r_{N}({\boldsymbol{t}}^{(1)}\mskip-0.99998mu,\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak{\boldsymbol{t}}^{(N-2)};{\boldsymbol{z}})\,g_{N-1}({\boldsymbol{t}}^{(N-1)};{\boldsymbol{z}})\ignorespaces

for some polynomial
\,r_{N}\,
. Evaluating both sides at

\,{\boldsymbol{t}}^{(N-1)}\mskip-2.00002mu=(z_{\lambda_{N}+1}\mskip 2.00002mu,\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak z_{n})\,

r_{N}({\boldsymbol{t}}^{(1)}\mskip-0.99998mu,\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak{\boldsymbol{t}}^{(N-2)};{\boldsymbol{z}})\,=\,f_{N-1}({\boldsymbol{t}}^{(1)}\mskip-0.99998mu,\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak{\boldsymbol{t}}^{(N-2)};z_{\lambda_{N}+1}\mskip 2.00002mu,\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak z_{n})\,.\ignorespaces

Thus
\mskip 2.00002muf_{N}({\boldsymbol{t}}^{(1)}\mskip-0.99998mu,\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak{\boldsymbol{t}}^{(N-1)};{\boldsymbol{z}})\mskip 0.99998mu=\mskip 0.99998mug_{1}({\boldsymbol{t}}^{(1)};{\boldsymbol{z}})\ldots g_{N-1}({\boldsymbol{t}}^{(N-1)};{\boldsymbol{z}})\,
,
which proves formula (
5.2
).
∎

Proof of Lemma
5.3
.

The proof is by induction on
N
, similarly to that of
Lemma
5.2
. Set
f_{1}\mskip-0.99998mu=1\,
, and for
N\geqslant 2\,
, write

f_{N}({\boldsymbol{t}}^{(1)}\mskip-0.99998mu,\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak{\boldsymbol{t}}^{(N-1)};{\boldsymbol{z}})\,=\,W^{\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\circ$}}_{I^{\mskip 0.99998mu\min}_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}}({\boldsymbol{t}}\mskip 0.99998mu;{\boldsymbol{z}})\,,\qquad f^{(i)}_{N}({\boldsymbol{t}}^{(1)}\mskip-0.99998mu,\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak{\boldsymbol{t}}^{(N-1)};{\boldsymbol{z}})\,=\,W^{\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\circ$}}_{\mskip-2.00002mus_{\lambda^{(i)}\!,\mskip 2.00002mu\lambda^{(i)}\mskip-0.99998mu+1}(I^{\mskip 0.99998mu\min}_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}})}({\boldsymbol{t}}\mskip 0.99998mu;{\boldsymbol{z}})\,,\ignorespaces

indicating the dependence on
\,N\mskip 0.99998mu
explicitly. By Lemma
5.2
,

\mskip 2.00002muf_{N}({\boldsymbol{t}}^{(1)}\mskip-0.99998mu,\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak{\boldsymbol{t}}^{(N-1)};{\boldsymbol{z}})=1\,
.

By formula (
5.1
), the function
\mskip 2.00002muf^{(N-1)}_{N}
is a symmetric linear
function in
\,t_{1}^{(N-1)}\mskip-0.99998mu,\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak t_{\lambda^{(N-1)}}^{(N-1)}\,
vanishing at

\,{\boldsymbol{t}}^{(N-1)}\mskip-2.00002mu=(z_{1},\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak z_{\lambda^{(N-1)}}\mskip-0.99998mu)\,
Hence,

f^{(N-1)}_{N}({\boldsymbol{t}}^{(1)}\mskip-0.99998mu,\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak{\boldsymbol{t}}^{(N-1)};{\boldsymbol{z}})\,=\,r_{N}({\boldsymbol{t}}^{(1)}\mskip-0.99998mu,\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak{\boldsymbol{t}}^{(N-2)};{\boldsymbol{z}})\mskip 2.00002mu\biggl(\,\sum_{j=1}^{\lambda^{(N-1)}}\;(\mskip 0.99998mut^{(N-1)}_{j}\mskip-2.00002mu-z_{j})\mskip 0.99998mu\biggr)

for some polynomial
\,r_{N}\,
. Evaluating both sides at

\,{\boldsymbol{t}}^{(N-1)}\mskip-2.00002mu=(z_{1},\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak z_{\lambda^{(N-1)}-1}\mskip 0.99998mu,\mskip 0.99998mu0\mskip 0.99998mu)\,
yields

r_{N}({\boldsymbol{t}}^{(1)}\mskip-0.99998mu,\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak{\boldsymbol{t}}^{(N-2)};{\boldsymbol{z}})\,=\,f_{N-1}({\boldsymbol{t}}^{(1)}\mskip-0.99998mu,\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak{\boldsymbol{t}}^{(N-2)};z_{1},\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak z_{\lambda^{(N-1)}-1}\mskip 0.99998mu,\mskip 0.99998mu0\mskip 0.99998mu)\,=\,1\ignorespaces

by Lemma
5.2
, which proves Lemma
5.3
for
\;i=N-1\,
.

For
\;i\neq N-1\,
, formula (
5.1
) implies that
the function
\mskip 2.00002muf^{(i)}_{N}
is a polynomial in

\,t_{1}^{(N-1)}\mskip-0.99998mu,\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak t_{\lambda^{(N-1)}}^{(N-1)}\mskip 0.99998mu
of degree zero in each of
these variables, hence a constant. Evaluating

\mskip 2.00002muf^{(i)}_{N}({\boldsymbol{t}}^{(1)}\mskip-0.99998mu,\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak{\boldsymbol{t}}^{(N-1)};{\boldsymbol{z}})\,
at

\,{\boldsymbol{t}}^{(N-1)}\mskip-2.00002mu=(z_{1},\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak z_{\lambda^{(N-1)}}\mskip-0.99998mu)\,
yields

f^{(i)}_{N}({\boldsymbol{t}}^{(1)}\mskip-0.99998mu,\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak{\boldsymbol{t}}^{(N-1)};{\boldsymbol{z}})\,=\,f^{(i)}_{N-1}({\boldsymbol{t}}^{(1)}\mskip-0.99998mu,\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak{\boldsymbol{t}}^{(N-2)};z_{1},\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak z_{\lambda^{(N-1)}}\mskip-0.99998mu)\,,=\,\sum_{j=1}^{\lambda^{(i)}}\;(\mskip 0.99998mut^{(i)}_{j}\mskip-2.00002mu-z_{j})\,,\ignorespaces

where the second equality holds by the induction assumption.
Lemma
5.3
is proved.
∎

Appendix D
Stirling’s formula

The next two lemmas are used in the proof of Lemma
5.10
.
The first lemma is well known.

Lemma D.1
.

For
\,|\mskip-0.99998mu\arg\mskip 0.99998mu(h/\kappa)\mskip 0.99998mu|<\pi
and any
\,\alpha\mskip-0.99998mu\in{\mathbb{C}}\,
, we have

\lim_{h\to\infty}\mskip 2.00002mu\frac{\Gamma(\alpha+h/\kappa)\mskip 2.00002mu}{(h/\kappa)^{\mskip 2.00002mu\alpha}\,\Gamma(h/\kappa)}\,=\,1\kern-20.00003pt\ignorespaces

locally uniformly in
\,\alpha
.

Lemma D.2
.

For any
\,\alpha\mskip 0.99998mu,r\mskip-2.00002mu\in{\mathbb{C}}\,
,
l\in{\mathbb{Z}}\,
,
we have

\lim_{h\to\infty}\mskip 2.00002mu\frac{r^{\mskip 0.99998mul}\,\Gamma(\alpha+l+h/\kappa)\mskip 2.00002mu}{(h/\kappa)^{\mskip 2.00002mul}\,\Gamma(\alpha+h/\kappa)\mskip 2.00002mu\Gamma(l+1)}\,=\,\frac{r^{\mskip 0.99998mul}}{\Gamma(l+1)}\kern-20.00003pt\ignorespaces

uniformly in
\,\mskip 0.99998mul
and locally uniformly in
\,\alpha\mskip 0.99998mu,r\mskip 0.99998mu
.

Proof.

Let
\,y=h/\kappa\,
and
\mskip 2.00002muf(x,y,\alpha)=(1-xy^{-1})^{\mskip-2.00002mu-\mskip 0.99998mu\alpha-y}\mskip 0.99998mu
.
For
\,r\mskip-0.99998mu<|\mskip 0.99998muy\mskip 0.99998mu|\,
by Cauchy’s formula,

\frac{r^{\mskip 0.99998mul}\,\Gamma(\alpha+l+y)\mskip 2.00002mu}{y^{\mskip 2.00002mul}\,\Gamma(\alpha+y)\mskip 2.00002mu\Gamma(l+1)}\,=\,\frac{r^{\mskip 0.99998mul}}{2\mskip 2.00002mu\pi\mskip 0.99998mu\sqrt{\mskip-0.99998mu-1}}\,\int\limits_{\!\!|x|=r}\!\frac{f(x,y,\alpha)}{x^{\mskip 0.99998mul+1}}\,d\mskip 0.99998mux\,.\ignorespaces

Since
\,\lim\limits_{y\to\infty}\mskip 0.99998muf(x,y,\alpha)=e^{x}\mskip 2.00002mu
locally uniformly
in
x,\alpha\,
, and

\frac{r^{\mskip 0.99998mul}}{\Gamma(l+1)}\,=\,\frac{r^{\mskip 0.99998mul}}{2\mskip 2.00002mu\pi\mskip 0.99998mu\sqrt{\mskip-0.99998mu-1}}\,\int\limits_{\!\!|x|=r}\!\frac{e^{x}}{x^{\mskip 0.99998mul+1}}\,d\mskip 0.99998mux\,,\ignorespaces

Lemma
D.2
follows.
∎

Appendix E
Polynomiality of solutions

Consider dynamical differential equations (
2.7
) for nonpositive integer
values of
\,h/\kappa\,
. Recall the
\,\mskip 0.99998mu\operatorname{End}\mskip 0.99998mu\bigl(({\mathbb{C}}^{N})^{\otimes\mskip 0.99998mun}_{\mskip 0.99998mu{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}}\bigr)
-valued Levelt
fundamental solution
\,\widehat{\Psi}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;h;{\boldsymbol{q}}\mskip 0.99998mu;\kappa)\,
, see Theorem
4.21
,
Proposition
4.24
, and formula (
4.47
) for the dynamical
Hamiltonians
\,X_{1},\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak X_{n}\mskip 0.99998mu
at
\,{\boldsymbol{q}}={\mathchoice{\raise 0.68889pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\mskip 2.00002mu\wr$\hss}0}{\raise 0.68889pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\mskip 2.00002mu\wr$\hss}0}{\raise 0.55199pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\mskip 2.00002mu\wr$\hss}0}{\raise 0.38748pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\mskip 2.00002mu\wr$\hss}0}}\,
.

Say that a rational function
\,f({\boldsymbol{z}})\,
is over integers if
\,f({\boldsymbol{z}})\,
is
a ratio of polynomials in
\,{\boldsymbol{z}}\,
with integer coefficients.

Theorem E.1
.

Let
\,m\mskip-0.99998mu\in\mskip-0.99998mu{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geqslant 0}\mskip 0.99998mu
and
\,\mskip 0.99998muh=\mskip-0.99998mu-\mskip 0.99998mum\kappa\,
.
Then the Levelt fundamental solution has the form

(E.1)

\widehat{\Psi}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;\mskip-0.99998mu-\mskip 0.99998mum\kappa\mskip 0.99998mu;{\boldsymbol{q}}\mskip 0.99998mu;\kappa)\,=\,P({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip-0.99998mu/\mskip-0.99998mu\kappa\mskip 0.99998mu;m;{\boldsymbol{q}})\,\mskip 0.99998mu\prod_{i=1}^{N-1}\prod_{j=i+1}^{N}(1-q_{j}/q_{i})^{-\mskip 0.99998mum\mskip 0.99998mu\lambda_{i}}\,\prod_{i=1}^{N}\,q_{i}^{\mskip 2.00002mu\smash{X_{i}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip-0.99998mu/\mskip-0.99998mu\kappa\mskip 0.99998mu;\mskip 0.99998mu-m\mskip 0.99998mu;\mskip 2.00002mu{\mathchoice{\raise 0.48222pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\mskip 2.00002mu\wr$\hss}0}{\raise 0.48222pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\mskip 2.00002mu\wr$\hss}0}{\raise 0.38638pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\mskip 2.00002mu\wr$\hss}0}{\raise 0.27122pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\mskip 2.00002mu\wr$\hss}0}})}}\mskip 2.00002mu,\kern-20.00003pt\ignorespaces

where
\,\mskip 0.99998muP({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;m;{\boldsymbol{q}})\mskip 0.99998mu
is a polynomial in the variables

\,\mskip 0.99998muq_{2}/q_{1},\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak q_{n}/q_{n-1}
with coefficients being rational functions
of
\,\mskip 0.99998mu{\boldsymbol{z}}
over integers. The degree of
\,\mskip 0.99998muP({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;m;{\boldsymbol{q}})\mskip 0.99998mu

in the variable
\,q_{i+1}/q_{i}\mskip 0.99998mu
is at most
\,\mskip 0.99998mum\mskip 2.00002mu(n-i)\mskip 2.00002mu\lambda^{(i)}
.
The funciton
\,\mskip 0.99998muP({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;m;{\boldsymbol{q}})\mskip 0.99998mu
is holomorphic in
\,{\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 2.00002mu
if

\,{z_{a}\mskip-0.99998mu-z_{b}\not\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mskip 0.99998mu\neq\mskip 0.99998mu0}}\mskip 2.00002mu
for all
\,\mskip 0.99998mu{1\leqslant a<b\leqslant n}\,
.
The singularities of
\,\mskip 0.99998muP({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;m;{\boldsymbol{q}})
at the hyperplanes

\,z_{a}\mskip-0.99998mu-z_{b}\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mskip 0.99998mu\neq\mskip 0.99998mu0}\mskip 2.00002mu
are simple poles.
For any
\,\mskip 0.99998mu{\boldsymbol{z}}
such that
\,z_{a}\mskip-0.99998mu-z_{b}\not\in\mskip-0.99998mu\kappa\mskip 2.00002mu{\mathbb{Z}}_{\mskip 0.99998mu\neq\mskip 0.99998mu0}\mskip 2.00002mu
for all

\,\mskip 0.99998mu1\leqslant a<b\leqslant n\,
, the columns of
\,\,\widehat{\Psi}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;\mskip-0.99998mu-\mskip 0.99998mum\kappa\mskip 0.99998mu;{\boldsymbol{q}}\mskip 0.99998mu;\kappa)\mskip 0.99998mu

form a basis of the space of
\;({\mathbb{C}}^{N})^{\otimes\mskip 0.99998mun}_{\mskip 0.99998mu{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}}\mskip-0.99998mu
-valued solutions of dynamical
differential equations (
2.7
).

Proof.

By formulae (
4.48
), (
4.60
),
the function
\,\mskip 0.99998mu\widehat{\Psi}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;\mskip-0.99998mu-\mskip 0.99998mum\kappa\mskip 0.99998mu;{\boldsymbol{q}}\mskip 0.99998mu;\kappa)\,
has the form
(
E.1
) with
\,P({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;m;{\boldsymbol{q}})\,
being a power series in

\,q_{2}/q_{1},\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak q_{n}/q_{n-1}\,
. The first step of the proof is to show
that the power series terminate so that
\,P({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;m;{\boldsymbol{q}})\,
is a polynomial
in
\,q_{2}/q_{1},\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak q_{n}/q_{n-1}\,
.

Denote by
\,P_{\mskip-0.99998muI\mskip-0.99998mu,\mskip 0.99998muJ}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;m;{\boldsymbol{q}})\,
the entries of
\,P({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;m;{\boldsymbol{q}})\,

in the standard basis
\,\{\mskip 0.99998muv_{I}\,,\allowbreak\,I\mskip-0.99998mu\in{{\mathcal{I}}_{{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}}}\mskip 0.99998mu\}\,
of
\,({\mathbb{C}}^{N})^{\otimes\mskip 0.99998mun}_{\mskip 0.99998mu{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}}
.
By formula (
4.60
),

(E.2)

P_{\mskip-0.99998muI\mskip-0.99998mu,\mskip 0.99998muJ}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;m;{\boldsymbol{q}})\,=\,\sum_{{\boldsymbol{l}}\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geqslant 0}^{\mskip 0.99998mu\lambda^{\{1\}}}\!\!}{\mathcal{J}}_{I\mskip-0.99998mu,\mskip 0.99998muJ,\,{\boldsymbol{l}}}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;-\mskip 0.99998mum)\,\prod_{i=1}^{N-1}\,(q_{i+1}/q_{i})^{\mskip 2.00002mu\sum_{a=1}^{\lambda^{(i)}}l_{a}^{(i)}},\kern-20.00003pt

where the coefficients
\,{\mathcal{J}}_{I\mskip-0.99998mu,\mskip 0.99998muJ,\,{\boldsymbol{l}}}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;-\mskip 0.99998mum)\,
are given by formula
(
E.4
) below.

Denote
\,\mskip 0.99998mu[\mskip 0.99998mux,\mskip-0.99998muj\mskip 2.00002mu]\mskip 0.99998mu=\mskip 0.99998mux\mskip 2.00002mu(x-1)\ldots(x-j+1)\,
. Recall

{\mathcal{Z}}_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\,=\,\{\mskip 2.00002mu{\boldsymbol{l}}\mskip-0.99998mu\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{\lambda^{\{1\}}}_{\geqslant 0}\,\mskip 0.99998mu|\ \,l^{\mskip 0.99998mu(i)}_{\mskip 0.99998mua}\mskip-2.00002mu\geqslant l^{\mskip 0.99998mu(i+1)}_{\mskip 0.99998mua}\mskip-0.99998mu,\ \;i=1,\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak N-1\,,\ \;a=1,\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak\lambda^{(i)}\mskip 0.99998mu\}\,,\kern-20.00003pt\ignorespaces

see (
4.31
). For
\,\mskip 0.99998mu{\boldsymbol{l}}\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geqslant 0}^{\mskip 0.99998mu\lambda^{\{1\}}}\mskip-0.99998mu
,
set
\,F_{\boldsymbol{l}}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;m)=0\,
if
\,\mskip 0.99998mu{\boldsymbol{l}}\mskip-0.99998mu\not\in\mskip-0.99998mu{\mathcal{Z}}_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\,
, and

(E.3)

\displaystyle F_{\boldsymbol{l}}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;m)\,

\displaystyle{}=\mskip 2.00002mu\prod_{i=1}^{N-1}\,\prod_{a=1}^{\lambda^{(i)}}\,\biggl(\mskip 0.99998mu\frac{(-1)^{\mskip 0.99998mul^{\mskip 0.99998mu(i)}_{\mskip 0.99998mua}\!-\mskip 2.00002mul^{\mskip 0.99998mu(i+1)}_{\mskip 0.99998mua}}\mskip 2.00002mu[\mskip 0.99998mum\mskip 2.00002mu,l^{\mskip 0.99998mu(i)}_{\mskip 0.99998mua}\!\mskip-0.99998mu-l^{\mskip 0.99998mu(i+1)}_{\mskip 0.99998mua}\mskip 0.99998mu]}{(\mskip 0.99998mul^{\mskip 0.99998mu(i)}_{\mskip 0.99998mua}\!\mskip-0.99998mu-l^{\mskip 0.99998mu(i+1)}_{\mskip 0.99998mua})\mskip 0.99998mu!}\,\times{}

\displaystyle\mskip 2.00002mu{}\times\mskip 2.00002mu\prod_{\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{1}{\scriptstyle b=1}{\scriptstyle b\ne a}}^{\lambda^{(i)}}\,\frac{[\mskip 0.99998muz_{b}-z_{a}\mskip-2.00002mu+l^{\mskip 0.99998mu(i)}_{\mskip 0.99998mua}\!\mskip-0.99998mu-l^{\mskip 0.99998mu(i)}_{\mskip 0.99998mub}\mskip-2.00002mu,m+1\mskip 0.99998mu]}{[\mskip 0.99998muz_{b}-z_{a}\mskip 0.99998mu,m+1\mskip 0.99998mu]}\;\prod_{\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{1}{\scriptstyle c=1}{\scriptstyle c\ne a}}^{\lambda^{(i+1)}}\frac{[\mskip 0.99998muz_{c}\mskip-0.99998mu-z_{a}\mskip 0.99998mu,m+1\mskip 0.99998mu]}{[\mskip 0.99998muz_{c}\mskip-0.99998mu-z_{a}\mskip-2.00002mu+l^{\mskip 0.99998mu(i)}_{\mskip 0.99998mua}\!-l^{\mskip 0.99998mu(i+1)}_{\mskip 0.99998muc}\mskip-2.00002mu,m+1\mskip 0.99998mu]}\,\mskip 0.99998mu\biggr)\kern-3.00003pt

if
\,\mskip 0.99998mu{\boldsymbol{l}}\mskip-0.99998mu\in\mskip-2.00002mu{\mathcal{Z}}_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}\,
. Here
\,\lambda^{(N)}\mskip-2.00002mu=n\,
and

\,\mskip 0.99998mul^{(N)}_{a}\!=0\,\mskip 0.99998mu
for all
\,a=1,\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak n\,
.

Notice that the factor
\,\mskip 0.99998mu[\mskip 0.99998mum\mskip 2.00002mu,l^{\mskip 0.99998mu(i)}_{\mskip 0.99998mua}\!\mskip-0.99998mu-l^{\mskip 0.99998mu(i+1)}_{\mskip 0.99998mua}\mskip 0.99998mu]\,

in formula (
E.3
) equals zero if

\,\mskip 0.99998mul^{\mskip 0.99998mu(i)}_{\mskip 0.99998mua}\!\mskip-0.99998mu-l^{\mskip 0.99998mu(i+1)}_{\mskip 0.99998mua}\mskip-2.00002mu>m\,
. Therefore,

\,F_{\boldsymbol{l}}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;m)=0\,
unless
l^{\mskip 0.99998mu(i)}_{\mskip 0.99998mua}\!\mskip-0.99998mu-l^{\mskip 0.99998mu(i+1)}_{\mskip 0.99998mua}\mskip-2.00002mu\leqslant m\,

for all
\,a,i\,
, and in particular,
\,F_{\boldsymbol{l}}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;m)=0\,
unless

\,l^{\mskip 0.99998mu(i)}_{\mskip 0.99998mua}\!\leqslant m\mskip 2.00002mu(n-i)\,
for all
\,a,i\,
.

Denote

{\mathcal{Z}}_{{\boldsymbol{\lambda}},\mskip 0.99998mum}\,=\,\{\mskip 2.00002mu{\boldsymbol{l}}\mskip-0.99998mu\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{\lambda^{\{1\}}}_{\geqslant 0}\,\mskip 0.99998mu|\ \,0\leqslant l^{\mskip 0.99998mu(i)}_{\mskip 0.99998mua}\!\mskip-0.99998mu-l^{\mskip 0.99998mu(i+1)}_{\mskip 0.99998mua}\mskip-2.00002mu\leqslant m\,,\ \;i=1,\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak N-1\,,\ \;a=1,\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak\lambda^{(i)}\mskip 0.99998mu\}\,,\kern-20.00003pt\ignorespaces

By formulae (
4.59
), (
4.57
), (
E.3
),

(E.4)

{\mathcal{J}}_{I\mskip-0.99998mu,\mskip 0.99998muJ,\,{\boldsymbol{l}}}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;-\mskip 0.99998mum)\,=\,\sum_{K\in{{\mathcal{I}}_{{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}}}}\mskip 2.00002mu\frac{F_{\boldsymbol{l}}({\boldsymbol{z}}_{\sigma_{K}}\mskip-0.99998mu;m)\,W_{I}(\Sigma_{K}\mskip-2.00002mu-{\boldsymbol{l}};{\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;-\mskip 0.99998mum)\,{\check{W}}_{J}(\Sigma_{K};{\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;-\mskip 0.99998mum)}{R_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}({\boldsymbol{z}}_{\sigma_{K}}\mskip-0.99998mu)\,Q_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}({\boldsymbol{z}}_{\sigma_{K}};-\mskip 0.99998mum)\,c_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}(\Sigma_{K}\mskip-2.00002mu-{\boldsymbol{l}}\mskip 0.99998mu;-\mskip 0.99998mum)\,c_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}(\Sigma_{K};-\mskip 0.99998mum)}\ignorespaces

if
\,\mskip 0.99998mu{\boldsymbol{l}}\mskip-0.99998mu\in\mskip-2.00002mu{\mathcal{Z}}_{{\boldsymbol{\lambda}},\mskip 0.99998mum}\,
, and
\,{\mathcal{J}}_{I\mskip-0.99998mu,\mskip 0.99998muJ,\,{\boldsymbol{l}}}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;-\mskip 0.99998mum)=0\,

if
\,\mskip 0.99998mu{\boldsymbol{l}}\mskip-0.99998mu\not\in\mskip-0.99998mu{\mathcal{Z}}_{{\boldsymbol{\lambda}},\mskip 0.99998mum}\,
. Therefore,

(E.5)

P_{\mskip-0.99998muI\mskip-0.99998mu,\mskip 0.99998muJ}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;m;{\boldsymbol{q}})\,=\,\sum_{{\boldsymbol{l}}\in{\mathcal{Z}}_{{\boldsymbol{\lambda}},m}\!\!}{\mathcal{J}}_{I\mskip-0.99998mu,\mskip 0.99998muJ,\,{\boldsymbol{l}}}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;-\mskip 0.99998mum)\,\prod_{i=1}^{N-1}\,(q_{i+1}/q_{i})^{\mskip 2.00002mu\sum_{a=1}^{\lambda^{(i)}}l_{a}^{(i)}},\kern-20.00003pt\ignorespaces

so that
\,P({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;m;{\boldsymbol{q}})\,
is a polynomial in
\,\mskip 0.99998muq_{2}/q_{1},\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak q_{n}/q_{n-1}

of degree at most
\,m\mskip 2.00002mu(n-i)\mskip 2.00002mu\lambda^{(i)}\mskip 0.99998mu
in the variable
\,q_{i+1}/q_{i}\,
,

\,i=1,\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak N\mskip 2.00002mu
. Moreover, by formulae (
E.3
), (
4.2
),
(
4.5
), (
4.8
), all the coefficients

\,{\mathcal{J}}_{I\mskip-0.99998mu,\mskip 0.99998muJ,\,{\boldsymbol{l}}}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;-\mskip 0.99998mum)\,
are rational functions of
\,{\boldsymbol{z}}\,

over integers. The regularity properties of
\,P({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;m;{\boldsymbol{q}})\,
follow from
Theorem
4.21
, item (ii).

The columns of
\,\mskip 0.99998mu\widehat{\Psi}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;\mskip-0.99998mu-\mskip 0.99998mum\kappa\mskip 0.99998mu;{\boldsymbol{q}}\mskip 0.99998mu;\kappa)\,
form a basis of
the space of
\;({\mathbb{C}}^{N})^{\otimes\mskip 0.99998mun}_{\mskip 0.99998mu{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}}\mskip-0.99998mu
-valued solutions of dynamical differential equations
(
2.7
) by Theorem
4.21
, see formula (
4.49
).
∎

Theorem
E.1
allows us to describe the monodromy of the system of
dynamical differential equations (
2.7
) if
\,h/\kappa\,
is a nonpositive
integer.

Corollary E.2
.

Let
\,\mskip 0.99998muh\mskip-0.99998mu\in\mskip-0.99998mu\kappa\mskip 2.00002mu{\mathbb{Z}}_{\leqslant 0}\,
. Then the monodromy of the system
of dynamical differential equations (
2.7
) is abelian.
The monodromy representation is generated by the matrices

\;e^{\mskip 2.00002mu2\mskip 0.99998mu\pi\sqrt{\mskip-0.99998mu-1}\,\smash{X_{1}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip-0.99998mu/\mskip-0.99998mu\kappa\mskip 0.99998mu;\mskip 0.99998muh\mskip-0.99998mu/\mskip-0.99998mu\kappa\mskip 0.99998mu;\mskip 2.00002mu{\mathchoice{\raise 0.48222pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\mskip 2.00002mu\wr$\hss}0}{\raise 0.48222pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\mskip 2.00002mu\wr$\hss}0}{\raise 0.38638pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\mskip 2.00002mu\wr$\hss}0}{\raise 0.27122pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\mskip 2.00002mu\wr$\hss}0}})}}\mskip 0.99998mu,\allowbreak\dots,\allowbreak\mskip 2.00002mue^{\mskip 2.00002mu2\mskip 0.99998mu\pi\sqrt{\mskip-0.99998mu-1}\,\smash{X_{N}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip-0.99998mu/\mskip-0.99998mu\kappa\mskip 0.99998mu;\mskip 0.99998muh\mskip-0.99998mu/\mskip-0.99998mu\kappa\mskip 0.99998mu;\mskip 2.00002mu{\mathchoice{\raise 0.48222pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\mskip 2.00002mu\wr$\hss}0}{\raise 0.48222pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\mskip 2.00002mu\wr$\hss}0}{\raise 0.38638pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\mskip 2.00002mu\wr$\hss}0}{\raise 0.27122pt\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\mskip 2.00002mu\wr$\hss}0}})}}\mskip 0.99998mu
.
For any
\,\mskip 0.99998mu{\boldsymbol{z}}
such that
\,z_{a}\mskip-0.99998mu-z_{b}\not\in\mskip-0.99998mu\kappa\mskip 2.00002mu{\mathbb{Z}}\mskip 2.00002mu

for all
\,\mskip 0.99998mu1\leqslant a<b\leqslant n\,
, the monodromy representation is
the direct sum of one-dimensional representations generated by the solutions

\,\,\Psi_{\mskip-0.99998muI}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;\mskip-0.99998mu-\mskip 0.99998mum\kappa\mskip 0.99998mu;{\boldsymbol{q}}\mskip 0.99998mu;\kappa)\,
,
\,\mskip 0.99998muI\mskip-0.99998mu\in{{\mathcal{I}}_{{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}}}\,
,
defined by (
4.20
).

Notice that for integer values of
\,h/\kappa\,
, the factors

\,(1-q_{j}/q_{i})^{\lambda_{i}h/\mskip-0.99998mu\kappa}
in formula (
4.21
) are rational functions
and the only nontrivial monodromy of the solutions

\,\mskip 0.99998mu\Psi_{\mskip-0.99998muJ}({\boldsymbol{z}}\mskip 0.99998mu;\mskip-0.99998mu-\mskip 0.99998mum\kappa\mskip 0.99998mu;{\boldsymbol{q}}\mskip 0.99998mu;\kappa)\,
comes from the factors

\,q_{i\vphantom{|^{1}}}^{\mskip 0.99998mu\smash{\sum_{a\in J_{i}}\!\mskip-0.99998muz_{a}\mskip-0.99998mu/\mskip-0.99998mu\kappa}}\mskip 0.99998mu

in formula (
4.22
).

The results of this appendix were motivated by the corresponding statements in

[
OP
]
on the quantum differential equation of the Hilbert scheme of points
in the plane.
